## v0.23.48-dev — Round A of the language-completion plan: the `u64` type and the `T(x)` cast

- **Where this came from.** The first of three host-language rounds the plan froze after M4 (Round B `switch`, Round C character literals and numeric sugar — both still pending). The compiler's last two shape gaps were the missing 64-bit **unsigned** type and the absence of any explicit conversion; this round closes both, and the bootstrap subset is deliberately left behind — `bootstrap/aster.ast` did not grow a `u64` slot or a cast, and `tests/codegen-twin.c` was synchronized with **comments**, not code.
- **`u64`, the eighth integer type.** `enum Kind { I32, U32, U8, U16, I64, U64, Ptr, Bool, Struct }` — `u64` shares `i64`'s 8-byte slots, X registers and `MOVZ`+`MOVK` literals, and differs only in the reading: `tyIsUnsigned` learns `U64`, so comparisons take `LO/LS/HI/HS` and division is `UDIV`. `ptr<u64>` picks up its 8-byte stride through the existing `tyPtrToElem`, with no new edit.
- **`promoteInt` answers `U64` first.** The rule is tested *before* the `tyIs64` branch: were it reached after, `u64 + i64` would answer `I64` and a `u64` comparison would be emitted with signed condition codes — silently wrong for every value past 2^63−1, exactly the range `u64` exists for. This is the rule the round's mutation layer proved needs its own witness (below).
- **The cast `T(x)`.** Function-shaped at the primary level, the spelling `sizeof`/`len` already use: six spellings `i32 i64 u8 u16 u32 u64`, recognized with **zero lookahead** because an integer keyword followed by `(` can be nothing else. `ptr` and `bool` are deliberately neither spellings nor operand types — `ptr(x)` is `expected expression` exactly as before, and `u8(p)`/`u8(true)`/`u8(s)` are `cannot cast ptr to u8` / `cannot cast bool to u8` / `cannot cast Point to u8` from sema. A narrowing answers the target width's low bits (`u8(300)` = 44, `u16(70000)` = 4464); a widening extends by the **source** type's signedness.
- **The defect the round found in itself.** The `Cast` arm answered at the source width, violating the invariant every other producer follows — *a producer answers at the width the consumer asked for* (a Var load `SXTW`s an `i32` into an `i64` context, a BinOp reruns itself at 64 bits via `wideOp`, a literal materialises at full width). So `i64(i32(0 - 1))` answered **4294967295**, not −1: a zero-extended 32-bit register read at 64 bits. The fix is one `SXTW` at the end of the arm, guarded by `tyIs64(want) && e.ty==Ty::I32 && !(wf>wt)` so a genuine narrowing still answers in W. The example's first draft hid the bug twice behind the qemu exit-code mask (a 12-bit answer is 4095, and 4095 & 0xFF is 255) — which is why the shipped example is **exactly 8 bits**, one `if` per group, `return r;` = 255.
- **The literal ceiling moved to the declaration.** `primary()` now accepts anything up to 2^64−1 (past it: `integer literal exceeds u64 range at L:C`), and `initializerFits` — the site that knows which slot the value was meant for — carries the old i64 ceiling: `let x: i64 = 9223372036854775808;` is refused naming the **slot** (`local initializer does not fit in i64: x (9223372036854775808)`) where the old parser named the token. A `u64` slot takes a negative literal as the sign-extended view of a value near 2^64 (`-1` is 2^64−1), never a truncation.
- **Two lessons the suite now carries.** (1) The `promoteInt`-forgets-`u64` mutant first shipped **blind**: a bare `u64` comparison takes its signedness from its *operand* types, not from `promoteInt`'s answer, so the original witness passed anyway. The witness had to be re-aimed at a `u64` **sum**, whose type *is* that answer (`x + 0 > 9223372036854775807`). (2) A hand-computed `UDIV` witness was wrong by a factor of 1000 — (2^64−1)/10^12 is 18,446,**744**, not …073 — caught by re-deriving in Python and confirming `UDIV` in the disassembly; the compiler was innocent and the *test* was the bug.
- **The twin, synchronized by comment.** `tests/codegen-twin.c` refuses `u64` and `T(x)` exactly as `bootstrap/aster.ast`'s `primary` path does — both spellings start with a type keyword where an expression is expected, so the same refusal covers them — and its `convert_num` **keeps** the i64-max saturation, with a comment pinning the decision: the bootstrap subset has no `u64` slot, so its literal ceiling is `i64` by construction, not a gap to close. The corpus's largest literal is 2^63−1, so the three sides (host, twin, bootstrap) have nothing to disagree about.
- **Twelve resident suites were updated on the record, and the full CI run found the last four edits.** Known while the round was being built: six suites carry the byte-baseline filter (`array-address`, `array-elem-types`, `multi-dim-array`, `precedence`, `sizeof-len`, `struct-array-field`), which gained this round's family — the 288 assertion unchanged, the lineage comment extended; three pin the diagnostics the moved literal ceiling changed (`syntax-relief`, `wide-int`, `wide-int-literal`); `struct-checks.sh`'s expected type list names `u64`; and `callback-table-checks.sh`'s `Expr::FieldCall } k;` pin became `FieldCall,` when `Cast` joined the enum's tail — without the tail, the discipline `sizeof-len-checks.sh` documents. **The full CI then found four more of exactly the kind the history has recorded before** (v0.23.24-dev, v0.23.28-dev): three suites (`sema-checks.sh`, plus `struct-checks.sh` and `wide-int-checks.sh` a second time) pinned the whole `Ty::Kind` member list, which U64's insertion moved — they now pin the head and the tail, the same tail-free discipline, so a future kind cannot move them; and `wide-int-literal-checks.sh`'s SXTW site count moved **11 → 13**, the two new sites being the cast's. Every count was raised to the number the source has, with the new site named at the guard.
- **The suite.** New resident `tests/integer-cast-checks.sh` **[10/10]**: a strict build, ~22 source guards, **32 qemu behavioural cases** (every cast direction × every scope — locals, globals, parameters, arrays, struct fields, `ptr<u64>` — plus unsigned compare/`UDIV`/promotion), 3 gcc-differential pairs, a **13-row rejection matrix**, three migrated cascade diagnostics, a coverage floor, a **9-mutant layer** with a new `must_reject` helper (the true compiler accepts, the mutant must refuse), and the byte baseline. Shipped example `examples/integer_cast.ast` exits **255** under `qemu-aarch64`, equal to its from-specification C twin `tests/integer_cast-twin.c`. Baseline **330 → 333 rows**, the 288 pre-existing ones byte-identical; `tests/rebuild-baseline-v0.23.48.sh` asserts the old rows before it writes. Full evidence in `VALIDATION-v0.23.48-dev.md`.

## v0.23.47-dev — the closed loop: the bootstrap compiler compiling itself, and the fixed point boot2 == boot3 == boot4

- **Where this came from.** M4 of `SELFHOSTING-PLAN.md` — the round the whole M0–M3 arc existed for, and like every round since M3a a round with **no compiler change** (the version constant is the only edit to `src/aster.cpp`). `CODEGEN-SPEC.md` §13 had parked the last step explicitly ("不在 M3d 范围（属 M4 闭环自举）") because the stage declared four `extern fn` and a standalone `ET_EXEC` cannot resolve an external symbol (§10 C9). M4 closes that — and closing it surfaced two latent codegen bugs no green suite could see, because the twin is a *byte* oracle and agrees with whatever the stage emits, including a wrong emission both sides share.
- **The fixed point, measured.** `aster build-elf bootstrap/aster.ast` → `boot.host`; `boot.host < aster.ast > boot2.elf` (**rc=0, no SIGSEGV**); `boot2.elf < aster.ast > boot3.elf`; `boot3.elf < aster.ast > boot4.elf` — and **`boot2 == boot3 == boot4` byte for byte** (md5 `039861a5347af8b7d9843ed14c68c6fb`). The stage is a fixed point of its own codegen: compiling its own source a second time changes nothing. The self-built `boot2.elf` run over the whole corpus against the C twin repeats the gate's numbers exactly — **115 programs, 48 byte-identical on accept, 67 both rejected, 0 divergence** — and its products are **47/47 runtime-identical** (exit code + stdout) to the host compiler's on every program both accept.
- **Fix 1 — the stage links against nothing.** `reverse_digits`/`utoa`/`itoa`/`memcmp` are written in the stage's own subset immediately above `bputraw`, the emitter that consumes them, with the same bodies the library had (`strlen` was declared but never called and is gone). `build-obj` now yields 35551 instructions / 210 functions / 628 globals (M3d's final: 35197/204/627) and `link-obj boot.o` alone succeeds with one UND symbol — the ELF null. The delivery shape of `tests/codegen-checks.sh` follows: `verifybin` links from `boot.o` alone, because the old two-object shape would now **fail** on duplicate `memcmp` — the self-containedization proving itself at link time. The library keeps its own suite (`tests/stdlib-checks.sh`).
- **Fix 2 — `a % b`.** `gen_combine` called `emit_msub(a, a, b, q)`, but the helper's signature is `emit_msub(rd, rn, rm, ra)` with `Ra` **last**, so the old call emitted `MSUB a, q, a, b` — `a = q − a*b`, not `a = a − q*b`. Every modulo answered a wrong number, on the stage **and** in the twin (same signature, same slip), which is precisely the bug class a byte differential is blind to. The call is now `emit_msub(a, q, b, a)`; `41 % 7` answers 6 from host-built and self-built binaries alike.
- **Fix 3 — `&&` / `||` really short-circuit.** `gx_push_bin` used to evaluate both operands of `E_And`/`E_Or` before the combine. The stage's own source guards its operator stack with `while (osp > bottom && oprec[osp - 1] == 11)`; with `osp == 0` the left conjunct decides and the right conjunct reads `oprec[-1]` — `uxtw` turns the garbage into a wild index and the self-compile **segfaults (rc=139) on any input**, which is exactly what the first self-built `boot2` did. The operators now push a state-2 logical frame carrying only the left operand (`gx_push_logical`) and evaluate the right operand inside the branch (new `gen_logical`, nested `gen_expr_at(r, TY_I32, gx_top)` per §6.1), so the skip is real; answers still normalise to 0/1 (§6.2). All four edits mirrored in `tests/codegen-twin.c`, and the gate stayed green through them.
- **One environmental trap, named.** `qemu-aarch64` **silently exits 1 on an ELF whose file mode is not executable** — no syscall, no diagnostic, the guest never starts; two files with the same md5 disagreed on exit status until `ls -l` showed `755` vs `644`. Any runtime comparison of a produced ELF must `chmod +x` it first. (M3d's notes record the same trap from the other side.)
- **The suite, unchanged in shape.** `tests/codegen-checks.sh` [6/6]: strict host build, generated-table freshness, the new single-object delivery shape + twin build, zero-dependency (exactly 1 UND — the ELF null), the differential (**115 programs, 48 byte-identical on accept, 67 both rejected, 0 divergence**) and **7/7 mutation probes** against the Aster source still diverging. No new example, so the baseline is untouched: 330 lines, byte-identical. Full evidence in `VALIDATION-v0.23.47-dev.md`.

## v0.23.46-dev — the bootstrap compiler's code-generation and ELF-emission stage: a standalone AArch64 ELF out

- **Where this came from.** M3d of `SELFHOSTING-PLAN.md` — the fourth and final increment of the bootstrap compiler, and like M3a/M3b/M3c a round with **no compiler change** (the version constant is the only edit to `src/aster.cpp`). `bootstrap/aster.ast` keeps M3c's lexer+parser+semantic front end and grows a code generator and ELF writer behind it: one Aster source in on stdin, a static `ET_EXEC` AArch64 ELF out on stdout; on failure a single `aster: error: <Cxx> at L:C` on stderr and an empty stdout, exit 1. Built and linked exactly the way M3c's was: `build-obj bootstrap/aster.ast` + `build-obj stdlib/aster_std.ast` + `link-obj`, main object first.
- **The contract was frozen before the code.** `bootstrap/CODEGEN-SPEC.md` (13 sections, 526 lines, every instruction encoding verified against `aarch64-linux-gnu-as` + `objdump`) pins what both implementations must do: the three-pass design (`cg_collect` address/label allocation → `cg_gen_program` iterative lowering into `textw: i32[262144]` → `elf_emit` fixup backfill + section tables), the seven type tags and their width/sign rules (mirroring the host's `gen(want)` consumer-width propagation, `SXTW` extension, unsigned condition codes), the AAPCS64 subset (X0–X7 args, stack args at `[SP+8*(k-8)]`, `main`→`_start`, every return path → `MOV X8,#93; SVC #0`), the 1024-slot × 8 B frame layout, the 25 expression and 9 statement lowering rules, the 15 intrinsics (8 syscall + 7 memory, `exit=93` new to the bootstrap), the fixed-base ELF layout (`.text@0x401000`/`.rodata@0x600000`/`.data@0x800000`, no address fixup), the runtime-init entry stub (`BL __aster_init; B main`), and the `C1`–`C15` error catalog (with `C9`/`C10`/`C13`/`C14`/`C15` new).
- **The oracle is independent by construction.** `tests/codegen-twin.c` re-implements the same spec in C with a deliberately different shape (a 0-based node graph where the bootstrap's handles are 1-based, a LIFO temporary pool, an explicit fixup table) and — on every accepted program — produced a **byte-identical** ELF to `bootstrap/aster.ast`; on every program the two agree on accept/reject. The differential is the frozen contract (`CODEGEN-SPEC.md` §12/§13): the self-hosting criterion is that the twin and the bootstrap agree byte for byte on every accepted program, and that the bootstrap's own `build-obj` output reproduces byte-for-byte across the suite (no compiler change this round). The one real bootstrap bug the earlier stages surfaced — an extern-global address cursor that mis-counted when one global in the file was `extern` — is pinned by a fixture and was fixed before #261 closed.
- **The AST self-parse pin is replaced by an ELF differential.** `tests/codegen-checks.sh`, the new resident suite, builds the reference compiler (`bootstrap/aster.ast` + `stdlib/aster_std.ast` linked into `verifybin`) and the C twin, then runs a three-way differential over `examples/*.ast` + `tests/codegen-fixtures/*.ast`, requiring byte-identical ELF on every accepted program and matching accept/reject on every program. **115 programs: 48 byte-identical on accept, 67 both rejected, 0 divergence**, plus **7 mutation probes** against the Aster source (each asserted able to diverge; two first drafts shipped blind and were re-aimed). It is the successor to the retired `tests/parser-checks.sh` (deleted in #261 along with `tests/parser-twin.c`); `bootstrap/aster.ast`'s `main()` was rewired from the M3b AST-dump path to the codegen chain (`sema → cg_collect → cg_gen_program → elf_emit → flush_out`), so the bootstrap no longer dumps the AST. Five minimal accepted shapes (`tests/codegen-fixtures/`) keep the gate self-contained.
- **Zero dependency held.** `verifybin` links against `std.o` and imports nothing but the ELF null symbol; the linked binary is a static `ET_EXEC` with no `.dynamic`, no `DT_NEEDED`, no UND but the null. No new example, so the baseline is untouched: 330 lines, byte-identical.

## v0.23.45-dev — the bootstrap compiler's semantic stage: a pure type-check gate over the AST dump

- **Where this came from.** M3c of `SELFHOSTING-PLAN.md` — the third increment of the bootstrap compiler, and like M3a/M3b a round with **no compiler change** (the version constant is the only edit to `src/aster.cpp`). `bootstrap/aster.ast` keeps M3b's lexer+parser at the front and grows a semantic pass behind them: one Aster source in on stdin, and on success the **same AST dump** M3b produced out on stdout — a pure validation gate ahead of codegen; on failure a single `aster: error: <Sxx> at L:C` on stderr, empty stdout, exit 1. Built and linked exactly the way M3b's was: `build-obj bootstrap/aster.ast` + `build-obj stdlib/aster_std.ast` + `link-obj`, main object first.
- **The contract was frozen before the code.** `bootstrap/SEMANTIC-SPEC.md` (13 sections, 387 lines) pins what both implementations must do: the five-type vocabulary (`i32`/`i64`/`u8`/`ptr`/`ptr<u8>`, `ptr<i32>`/`ptr<i64>` accepted structurally), the symbol-table model (parallel `i32` arrays, handle-indexed, scope-chained, frames within 8 KB), the walk order (top-level collection → declaration checks → per-function scope → expression/statement typing → constant validation → capacity limits), the success-output rule that the dump is byte-identical to M3b's (§5), the `S1`–`S25` error catalog (§6/§9), the expression/statement typing rules (§7/§8), and the two-phase discipline (nothing reaches stdout until the walk fully succeeds). The divergences from the host's `Sema` are recorded, not fixed (§12): no struct/union/user types, no `bool`/`u16`/`u32`/`u64` storable, success re-emits the untyped dump rather than an IR, arity ceiling 32 not 500, no overloads/generics/modules.
- **The oracle is independent by construction.** `tests/parser-twin.c` — the M3a/M3b differential — gained a semantic pass of its own: a real C `struct` array for the AST and an array-indexed symbol table, mirroring the bootstrap's five-type rules and `S`-catalog; both sides share no type-checking code and agreed byte for byte on every accept (identical dump) and every reject (identical `S`-diagnostic), including the stage reading its own 10956-line source and the library's 555-line source — where the dump is provably identical to M3b's (§15 self-semantic). Resident suite `tests/parser-checks.sh` [12/11], **333 checks** including the 20-case semantic corpus (14 rejections with exact `S`-diagnostics, 6 acceptances byte-identical to M3b) and **13 mutation probes** against the Aster source — each asserted able to diverge. The linked binary imports nothing (one UND entry, the ELF null symbol). No new example, so the baseline is untouched: 330 lines, byte-identical.

## v0.23.44-dev — the bootstrap compiler's parser stage: Aster source in, the AST dump out

- **Where this came from.** M3b of `SELFHOSTING-PLAN.md` — the second increment of the bootstrap compiler, and like M3a a round with **no compiler change** (the version constant is the only edit to `src/aster.cpp`). `bootstrap/aster.ast` keeps M3a's scanner at the front and grows a parser behind it: one Aster source in on stdin, the AST dump out on stdout. The subset is the compiler's own — functions, globals, parameters, seven type spellings (`i32`/`i64`/`u8`/`ptr`/`ptr<u8>`/`ptr<i32>`/`ptr<i64>`), `let`/assignment/index-assignment/`return`/`if`/`else`/`while`/`break`/`continue`/expression statements, and the ten expression levels of §6.1 (`logical → logicalAnd → bitor → bitxor → bitand → equality → relational → shift → sum → product`, with equality and relational admitting exactly one operator per level, P54). Built and linked exactly the way M3a's was: `build-obj bootstrap/aster.ast` + `build-obj stdlib/aster_std.ast` + `link-obj`, main object first.
- **The contract was frozen before the code.** `bootstrap/PARSER-SPEC.md` (13 sections, 907 lines) pins what both implementations must do: the 40 node kinds (5 `N_*`, 10 `S_*`, 25 `E_*`) at the host's `enum Kind` numbers, the AST representation, the subset in three sections, the expression parser's work stack, the position conventions (a node sits at its **introducing token**; `Return` at its expression, `If` at its condition, a call statement at the callee; a group adds no node; a binary node inherits its left operand's token), the dump format with five worked examples, the two-phase discipline (nothing reaches stdout until the parse has fully succeeded), and **95 diagnostics byte for byte** — E1–E9, P1–P62, B1–B22. The five frozen §9 examples are extracted from the spec **at run time** by the suite, so the document stays the single source of truth and a stale copy cannot pass.
- **The AST has no structs to live in, because the language has none yet.** It rides in **seven parallel `i32` arrays of 65536 entries** addressed by handle (handle = index + 1; 0 is "no node"): `nkind`, `ntok` (the introducing token — the source of position, name, number text and string value), `nfirst`/`nlast`/`nnext` (one sibling chain per node, so one append helper serves every list in the language) and `nty`/`ncount`. The expression parser is an **explicit work-stack state machine** — `vstack`/`ostacl`/`oprec` (256 each) and a 128-entry marker stack — rather than the recursive descent the twin uses; §10 of the spec proves the caps bound every stack.
- **The names are data, not code.** `bootstrap/gen_parser_names.py` is the single source of truth for the node-kind names and numbers and the type spellings. It emits the generated region of `bootstrap/aster.ast` and the twin's `tests/parser_names.h`, and it **cross-checks `src/aster.cpp`** — extracting `enum Kind` inside `struct Expr` and `struct Stmt` and refusing to emit unless the subset is a subsequence of the host's, in the host's order. A node set that drifts between the two implementations fails the generator, and `parser-checks.sh` re-runs it in `--check` mode.
- **The twin is independent by construction, not by claim.** `tests/parser-twin.c` (1455 lines) implements the same spec with a different shape wherever the spec leaves room: a **recursive descent** against the bootstrap's work-stack state machine, a struct-array AST against the seven parallel arrays, and — carried over from M3a's twin — the same scanner, `ctype`-free byte ranges and stdio. It matched byte for byte on the five frozen §9 examples, all 95 diagnostics, a 59-case boundary corpus (27 accepted, 31 refused, one past each edge), the caps at their edges, and the entire repository — including **the parser reading its own source** (7961 AST lines) and the library's (555).
- **The differential earned its keep: one real bootstrap bug, one shared slip, one wrong spec.** P50 — the over-32-arguments diagnostic passed the **Call node's id** to the token-position lookup, two different id spaces, and reported `1:11` where the callee token sat at `1:20`; the twin caught it. P37/P40 — two array-assignment diagnostics used `die_name_at` and interpolated the array name into a sentence the host throws bare (`expected '=' in array element assignmenta`); holding both implementations against the host compiler caught it, on both sides at once. And the spec's own frozen §9.4 dump was wrong: it placed `Return` at the `return` keyword where the host and §9.1's conventions both put it at the expression — the document was corrected to match the implementations. The twin also had a bring-up bug of its own (a missing `sum` level, so `+` never parsed), which is the independence claim demonstrated rather than asserted.
- **The M3a lexer suite is retired, and what covers the loss is named.** The token dump M3a wrote to stdout is gone, so `tests/lexer-checks.sh` and `tests/lexer-twin.c` — written against that dump — are deleted (`PARSER-SPEC.md` §12.1 records the decision). The parser suite re-pins every lexer diagnostic (E1–E9, same words and positions), and the AST dump observes the tokenization itself: identifier text through `Var` names, number conversion through `Num` values, string decoding through `String` values, token boundaries through positions, and operator spelling through the diagnostics (`x <<= 1` is B10 precisely because `<<=` is one token). `tests/lexer_names.h` survives — still generated by `bootstrap/gen_lexer_names.py`, now consumed by `tests/parser-twin.c`.
- **Thirteen mutation probes, each asserted able to fail.** The probes patch `bootstrap/aster.ast` itself — the group marker's `)`, `+`'s precedence, the P50 position, the P37 name interpolation, the C1 cap, P54's one-operator rule, B13's second index, the empty-call guard, the saturating cast, the shift range, the call's `)`, the else-if desugaring, the division-by-zero folder — and each is rebuilt, relinked and required to diverge from the twin on a targeted input. A probe that still matches is blind, and blind is a failure; the suite asserts each edit applied before it even builds. Two of the thirteen shipped blind on the first attempt (a precedence flip that changed no grouping, an else-if probe aimed at text that did not exist) and were re-aimed — the probe layer is only as good as its last miss.
- Resident suite `tests/parser-checks.sh` [11/11], **312 checks**: generator freshness (both generators), the delivery chain and the twin's strict build, zero-dependency (one UND symbol — the ELF null), the five frozen examples extracted from the spec, 96 diagnostic pins, position and format semantics (End token, CRLF, no trailing newline, groups add no node, two-phase stdout silence), the 59-case boundary corpus, 112 self-parsing sources with the two pinned dump lengths, the caps at their edges (C1 96/97, C2 95/96, E8 at and one past 1 MiB, E9 at and one past 65536, the 32-argument edge, and the densest E9-legal source — 65529 nodes, which is why C3's 65536-node cap is defensive rather than reachable), and the thirteen probes. Registered in `tests/ci.sh`, replacing the retired `lexer-checks.sh`.
- **No new example, so the baseline is untouched**: 330 lines, byte-identical (`tests/rebuild-baseline-v0.23.44.sh` re-verifies all of them). This round rewrites `bootstrap/aster.ast` (2390 → 2400 lines, 14165 instructions, 87 functions, 354 globals), adds `bootstrap/PARSER-SPEC.md`, `bootstrap/gen_parser_names.py`, `tests/parser_names.h`, `tests/parser-twin.c`, `tests/parser-checks.sh` and the suite registration, edits `bootstrap/LEXER-SPEC.md` to record the retirement — and deletes two files.

## v0.23.43-dev — the bootstrap compiler's lexer stage: Aster source in, the token stream out

- **Where this came from.** M3 of `SELFHOSTING-PLAN.md` is the bootstrap compiler itself, and the plan's own note is that it needs no compiler change — M2 just proved that end to end. The first increment (M3a) is the lexer: the stage that needs no heap, no AST and no linker, only stdin, stdout and the byte intrinsics. `bootstrap/aster.ast` reads one Aster source from stdin and writes the token stream to stdout, one token per line, built by the host compiler and linked against the M2 library exactly the way M2's consumer is (`build-obj bootstrap/aster.ast` + `build-obj stdlib/aster_std.ast` + `link-obj`, main object first). Like M2, the only edit to `src/aster.cpp` is the version constant.
- **The contract was frozen before the code.** `bootstrap/LEXER-SPEC.md` (13 sections) pins what both implementations must do: 72 token kinds numbered exactly as `enum class TK`, the C-locale character classes as exact byte ranges, 1-based byte-column positions (a tab is one column), the comment/identifier/number/string/operator rules including the maximal-munch lookahead that keeps `<<=` one token, the dump format with a worked example, and the nine diagnostics byte for byte. The frozen example and the nine messages are pinned **absolutely** in the suite, not just differentially — "the two implementations agree" is not evidence that both are right.
- **The names are data, not code.** `bootstrap/gen_lexer_names.py` is the single source of truth for the kind names and numbers, the 22 keywords and the packed name table. It emits the generated region of `bootstrap/aster.ast` (72 `K_*` constants, the 72-record × 12-byte `ktab`, `ident_is` and the `keyword_kind` chain) and the C twin's `tests/lexer_names.h`, and it **cross-checks `src/aster.cpp`** — extracting `enum class TK` and the lexer's keyword chain and refusing to emit on a mismatch. A token set that drifts between the two implementations now fails the generator, and `lexer-checks.sh` re-runs it in `--check` mode so a stale table fails CI rather than the differential.
- **Three language limits shaped the design, and all three are documented rather than discovered late.** An array may not exceed 1 MiB, so the source buffer is exactly that with **no sentinel byte**: `peek()` answers end-of-input by comparing the cursor against the length, which has the side effect that an **embedded NUL ends the scan** — exactly what the host compiler's `peek()` does, so the two cannot drift on it. The same cap forced a 65536-token ceiling with its own diagnostic (E9, the `End` token excluded, so 65536 real tokens are accepted). And `len` turned out to be the element-count builtin, not a usable parameter name — the helpers spell it `n`, and `LEXER-SPEC.md` §13 lists every reserved name the stage must avoid.
- **The twin is independent by construction, not by claim.** `tests/lexer-twin.c` implements the same contract with a different shape wherever the spec leaves room: a struct array instead of five parallel arrays, `ctype` instead of byte-range comparisons, a `switch` instead of the if-chain, `snprintf` where the Aster side uses the library's `utoa`, stdio instead of `read`/`write`, and the whole dump built in memory and written once (the two phases then hold by construction). It matched byte for byte on the frozen §9 example, the nine diagnostics, a 32-case boundary corpus, the 1 MiB and 65536-token edges, 500 randomized inputs, and the entire repository — including **the stage reading its own source** (4716 token lines) and the library's (1176).
- **The first mutation probes to break the bootstrap compiler on purpose.** Ten probes patch `bootstrap/aster.ast` itself — the tab in the space class, the `true` keyword branch, the hexadecimal guard, the escape error's position, both fixed-array caps, the `\n` decoding, the `End` token's column, the unterminated-comment error, and the dump's identifier text — each rebuilt, relinked and required to diverge from the twin on a targeted input. A probe that still matches is blind, and blind is a failure; the suite asserts each edit applied before it even builds.
- Resident suite `tests/lexer-checks.sh` [11/11], 183 checks: generator freshness, the delivery chain, zero-dependency (the linked binary carries one UND symbol — the ELF null), the spec pinned absolutely (§9 example, E1–E7 with exact positions, End-token/tab/NUL/comment semantics), the operator and keyword tables pinned byte-exact, the 32-case boundary corpus differentially, 114 self-lexing sources, the SRCMAX/TOKMAX edges, and the ten probes. Registered in `tests/ci.sh`.
- **No new example, so the baseline is untouched**: 330 lines, byte-identical. This round adds `bootstrap/aster.ast`, `bootstrap/LEXER-SPEC.md`, `bootstrap/gen_lexer_names.py`, `tests/lexer_names.h`, `tests/lexer-twin.c`, `tests/lexer-checks.sh` and the suite registration — no example, no test and no documented behaviour is edited.

## v0.23.42-dev — the Aster standard library's first slice: thirteen routines, one object, zero dependencies

- **Where this came from.** M0 (v0.23.40-dev) gave a program the bytes in, M1 (v0.23.41-dev) gave it somewhere to put them — and `SELFHOSTING-PLAN.md` M2 is the question those two raise: *now what does a bootstrap compiler actually write with?* The answer is thirteen routines in `stdlib/aster_std.ast`, pure Aster over the byte intrinsics, **no compiler change at all**: `memcpy`/`memset`/`memcmp` (token and node bytes), `strlen`/`strcmp`/`strncpy` (identifier and keyword matching), `utoa`/`itoa`/`utohex` (ELF fields, diagnostics), and an open-addressing hash table `hash_str`/`hash_put`/`hash_get` over two global arrays (the symbol and label table). The version bump is the only edit to `src/aster.cpp`.
- **The delivery shape was decided by a rule written down nine versions ago.** `include` shares *declarations only* — a body and an initializer stay in the file that owns them (v0.23.13-dev) — so a library cannot be an include. It ships as a relocatable object and the consumer declares `extern fn`: `aster build-obj stdlib/aster_std.ast -o std.o && aster build-obj app.ast -o app.o && aster link-obj app.o std.o -o app`, exactly the two-object pattern `examples/global_shared_main.ast` has always demonstrated. The suite exercises the whole chain, plus the edges: a bare `ptr` parameter refuses `&arr` (only `ptr<u8>` takes both a string literal and an array address), a wrong arity is refused, an undeclared routine is refused, a second definition of `memcpy` is refused by the linker, and **`std.o` imports nothing** — one UND symbol, the ELF null at index 0, thirteen functions and two globals defined. That last check is the zero-dependency discipline made mechanical rather than promised.
- **The parameter spelling was measured, not chosen.** Three candidates were probed before a line of the library was written: a bare `ptr` parameter takes a string literal (whose type *is* a ptr to NUL-terminated rodata) but **refuses `&arr`**; a `ptr[N]` global element takes a literal but refuses `&arr` too (`expected ptr, got ptr` — a message that names neither, which is why the probe exists); and **`ptr<u8>` takes both**. Every byte-buffer parameter in the library is therefore `ptr<u8>`, and the suite's first rejection pins the rule so a future "simplification" to bare `ptr` fails CI rather than failing a consumer.
- **The differential twin found a constant three times wrong — in the author's arithmetic, not the compiler's.** FNV-1a's offset basis `0xcbf29ce484222325` does not fit an i64 literal, so it enters as its two's-complement value, and the first two attempts at that value were both wrong by a few billion (the suite's slot indices disagreed with the twin's). The proof that the *compiler* was innocent is the nice part: `itoa` printed back, digit for digit, the wrong constant the source had asked for. The correct value is **-3750763034362895579**, now carrying the derivation in a comment. The lesson generalises: any constant past i64 is generated with Python, never written by hand.
- **And it found the one class of bug a differential *cannot* find.** `strncpy`'s first cut returned `i` *after* the NUL-pad loop had walked it to `n`, so every short source answered `n` instead of its copied length — and the C twin, written from the same sentence, made the identical mistake, so the outputs matched byte for byte. What caught it was reading the documented contract (`min(strlen(src), n)`) against the printed line (`NP 8` for a two-character source). A differential catches *disagreement*; only a stated invariant catches *shared* error, which is why the suite's case set exists in prose before it exists in code.
- **This is the first suite whose mutation probes target Aster source rather than the compiler.** Eight probes patch the library itself — the copy offset, the fill constant, the comparison's sign, the digit base, the strncpy return, the FNV multiplier, the hash mask, the miss sentinel — rebuild `std.o`, relink and require divergence. One of them earned its keep twice: masking to 511 *passed as blind*, because all three demo keys hash below 512, and a probe that cannot fail is decoration. It now masks to 1024 (the classic forgotten `-1` on a power-of-two capacity), which collapses the table into the first slots and moves every index.
- **Baseline 327 → 330 lines, the other 327 byte-identical — and this round compares all of them.** `tests/rebuild-baseline-v0.23.42.sh` filters this round's `stdlib_demo` rows, asserts the **whole** recorded baseline reproduces before it writes anything, and lands the total at 330. Every earlier script since v0.23.37 compared only the 309-row core, filtering the carried `fn_type`/`cb_table`/`fn_ret`/`call-stmt`/`copy` rows (and last round's `arena`) out of both sides; with the baseline file carrying them, a full 327-line comparison costs nothing and catches more, so the filter list shrinks to just this round's addition. Re-recording is not optional bookkeeping: ten resident suites (`condition`, `null-ptr`, `precedence`, `sizeof-len`, `struct`, `struct-array-field`, `array-address`, `array-elem-types`, `multi-dim-array`, `narrow-value-coverage`) read `tests/baseline/v0.23.5-dev/example-hashes.txt`, so an unrecorded example fails the gate until the script runs — which is exactly the instruction those suites print (`re-recording is tests/rebuild-baseline-*.sh's job`). Eight full-file diff it; six of them (`precedence`, `array-elem-types`, `struct-array-field`, `array-address`, `multi-dim-array`, `sizeof-len`) additionally filter each round's own family out of both sides and assert the surviving count is **288**, so the new example joined six filter lists too — the count survives because the filtered total grows by exactly the round's three rows (330 recorded − 42 filtered = 288, measured on the file, not assumed). The library is new files; no example, no test and no documented behaviour is edited, and the empirical run says so: 327 for 327, byte for byte.
- Resident suite `tests/stdlib-checks.sh` [8/8]: strict rebuild, the delivery chain, 28 output lines byte-identical to `tests/aster-std-twin.c` (which checks its own `utoa`/`itoa`/`utohex` against `snprintf` before printing anything, so a routine wrong on both sides is a coincidence the suite refuses to rely on), 3 rejections, the self-containedness check, the duplicate-symbol refusal, and the 8-mutant layer. The library is 664 instructions, 13 functions, 2 globals; the shipped consumer `examples/stdlib_demo.ast` is both the readable example and the differential driver, as `examples/arena.ast` was for M1.

## v0.23.41-dev — a growable heap: `mmap`, `brk` (the memory side of self-hosting)

- **Where this came from.** v0.23.40-dev closed the first hard self-hosting blocker — a program can now read a byte in. The second was blunter still: **there was nowhere to put anything.** A no-libc program had exactly two kinds of memory, the 8 KiB stack frame and the `.bss`, and a compiler's token table, AST and symbol table do not fit in either. `SELFHOSTING-PLAN.md` M1 names the fix and its two shapes: a bump allocator over a global arena (zero compiler changes) or the two system calls that grow the address space — this round ships the second as `mmap`(222) and `brk`(214), and `examples/arena.ast` shows the first riding on top of it.
- **These are the first intrinsics whose *result* is an address, and that is the whole story of the round.** Every earlier syscall intrinsic answered an `i32` — a byte count, a descriptor, an `-errno` — so `syscallInsn` could always read its answer with `MOV Wd, W0`. `mmap` answers a pointer: under qemu the guest mapping lives around `0x4000_0081_2000`, far above 4 GiB, and reading it through `W` hands back `0x00000000_7fxxxxxx` — a plausible-looking address that faults on the first store. The helper now takes the answer's width from the intrinsic (`Ty::Ptr`/`Ty::I64` → `MOV Xd, X0`) and the two heap intrinsics are the only callers that ask for it. `m_mmap_result` narrows the read back to 32 bits on purpose and asserts the suite notices.
- **An i64 argument is the other half, and it was a silent bug waiting to happen.** `mmap(addr, length, prot, flags, fd, offset)` carries `length` and `offset` as 64-bit values — the first i64 arguments any intrinsic has taken — and the staging loop chose its `MOV` by "is it a ptr?". An i64 argument staged through `W` truncates, and the case that makes it visible is exactly the one the suite ships: a length of **`0x1_0000_0000` (4 GiB)** maps fine at full width (demand-zero, lazy) but truncates to **0**, and the kernel rejects a zero length with `EINVAL`. `mmap_huge` pins that value; `m_mmap_argwidth` reverts the staging to the ptr-only test and asserts the case diverges.
- **Six arguments is the widest any intrinsic carries, and the discipline did not change.** `mmap` needs x0..x5 — more than `write`'s three — but the hazards are the ones v0.23.40-dev already survived: all six destination temporaries are allocated up front and distinct, loaded, then released top-down (`releaseTemp` is strict LIFO). The pool is sixteen wide and `temp()` throws a clean "expression too complex" rather than aliasing, so the only way to exhaust it is an adversarially nested call.
- **The contract is the one the round before established, in both passes.** `checkCall` types all six arguments (a ptr address; i64 length and offset, where a literal widens exactly as `store_i64`'s value does and a ptr or bool is refused; i32 prot/flags/fd) and answers `ptr`; `gen` re-checks with `exprIsPtr` and funnels through `syscallInsn`. Codegen's `callType` — the lightweight "what does this call yield" that `exprType` reads — learns both names too, because without it a `let heap: ptr = mmap(…)` is typed `i32` and refused as a mismatch: the first time a non-`load_ptr` intrinsic has ever had to be registered there. A user `fn mmap`/`fn brk` still shadows the intrinsic, and both shadow cases assert the user function wins. A failing call answers **`-errno` as a pointer** (`mmap` with a bad offset returns `-22`, and poking through it faults) — the honest libc-free contract, same as `close`'s `-9`.
- **The allocator is not a builtin, and that is deliberate.** No `malloc`/`free` intrinsics ship: the bump allocator is *written in Aster* — `examples/arena.ast` maps one page, hands out `heap + cursor` with a moving cursor, and reclaims the whole arena with `cursor = 0`, which is exactly the shape a parse-emit-exit compiler wants. `ptr + i32` is a byte offset and `store_u8`/`load_u8`/`store_i64`/`load_i64` are the accessors, so everything above the mapping is ordinary language. `munmap` is not shipped either — the process exiting is the deallocator, which is the zero-dependency answer.
- **What it does *not* do.** It adds no `argc`/`argv` (`main` stays `() -> i32`, still `_start`), no `mprotect`/`munmap`, and no allocator of its own. It touches no existing path: for every pre-existing intrinsic the widened tests make the same decision the old ones did, so a program that never names `mmap`/`brk` compiles to the bytes it always did — **the 324-row baseline reproduces byte for byte** (see `tests/rebuild-baseline-v0.23.41.sh`; only `arena`'s three rows are new, total 327).
- **Coverage: `tests/heap-checks.sh`, registered in `ci.sh`, with the shipped example `examples/arena.ast` and its C twin `tests/arena-twin.c`.** (1) a strict `-Werror` rebuild; (2) **5 gcc-differential cases** — a 4 KiB anonymous page poked at five offsets (the page's last byte included), a 4 GiB mapping (the i64-width tripwire), the bootstrap's read-source pattern (`openat`→`fstat`→`mmap` a file at a page-aligned offset→`close`→read it back through `load_u8`), the `sbrk` shape (`brk(0)` to query, grow by a page, use and read back the fresh memory), and the arena example against its twin; (3) **10 rejections** — the argument contract for both intrinsics, including the bool-in-an-i64-slot refusal; (4) **2 shadow cases** — a user-defined `mmap`/`brk` wins, observed through a counter in the exit status; (5) **5 mutation probes** — renumber `mmap` 222→223 and `brk` 214→215, narrow the result read back to 32 bits, revert the i64 argument width, and delete the `mmap` dispatch; each asserts both the break and the "before" fact.

## v0.23.40-dev — raw system calls: `read`, `openat`, `close`, `fstat` (the input side of self-hosting)

- **Where this came from.** v0.23.39-dev shipped and closed its own record by naming the one thing Aster could not do: *"编译器距离自举还差什么？"* — the answer was a language that was **~80% of the way there in principle** but had two hard blockers, and the first was blunt: **there was no way to read a byte in.** `write` (x8=64) and `main`'s `exit` (x8=93) were the *only* system calls the compiler emitted; a program could print and quit but never receive. The answer's own minimal path — *"加一个 `read` syscall 内建… 保持零依赖"* — is this round, widened just enough to matter: the four system calls a self-hosting compiler needs to pull a source file in and push an ELF out, all still `SVC #0`, all still with no libc. `SELFHOSTING-PLAN.md` is written alongside it as the roadmap this is milestone M0 of.
- **The numbers are the AArch64 ones, and getting them wrong is the bug that shipped.** `read`=63, `close`=57, and `fstat`=**80** are asm-generic; but AArch64 has **no plain `open`** — 56 is `openat(dirfd, path, flags, mode)` (four arguments, `AT_FDCWD=-100` for the current directory), and **79 is `newfstatat`**, not `fstat`. The first cut used 79 for `fstat` and a three-argument `open`; the differential suite caught both — `fstat` silently stopped filling the stat buffer and `open` failed outright — and the resident `m_fstat` mutation probe renumbers 80→79 to prove the suite still notices. `exit`(93)/`write`(64) had already pinned this numbering; this round completes it.
- **`syscallInsn` is `write`'s staging, factored once.** Every intrinsic funnels through one helper — park every live temporary in the frame, generate each argument, stage it through the scratch grid, load x0..xn, `MOV X8, #num; SVC #0`, read the i32 result from w0, restore — the exact register dance `write` performs because x0/x1/x2 are simultaneously the syscall argument registers and the first three temporaries of the w0–w15 pool. Two of the hazards `write` had already survived were re-learned here: the destination temporaries must be **allocated up front and distinct** (releasing between arguments makes `temp()` hand back the same register, so loading argument *i* clobbers x0 already staged for an earlier one — the `read(256, buf, 256)` strace), and they must be **released top-down** (`releaseTemp` is strict LIFO — an out-of-order release is an internal error). Both are pinned in the suite.
- **The contract is the same as `write`'s, in Sema and in codegen.** `callType` types each argument and answers `i32`; `gen` re-checks with `exprIsPtr` (codegen has no `typeOf` — it reads the syntax) and dispatches only when the program has not defined a same-named function, so a user `fn read` still shadows the intrinsic exactly as it shadows `write`. `read(fd, buf, len)` / `openat(fd, path, flags, mode)` / `close(fd)` / `fstat(fd, buf)` are all refused on the wrong argument count or the wrong argument kind, naming the offending argument.
- **These are the raw kernel returns, not libc's.** A failing call answers **`-errno`** (a `close` of a never-open fd returns `-9`, which lands 247 in the exit status), because the intrinsic *is* the system call — there is no libc wrapper normalising it to `-1`. That is the honest libc-free contract, and it is why the `close_badfd` case's C oracle uses the same raw `SVC` in inline asm: every glibc entry point, `close()` and even `syscall()`, converts `-9` to `-1` and would diverge for a reason that is not a bug.
- **What it does *not* do.** It adds no heap — `SELFHOSTING-PLAN.md` M1 gets the bootstrap its memory from a global arena over `u8[1<<20]` buffers (or, later, `mmap`/`brk` intrinsics), never from an external allocator. It adds no `argc`/`argv` — `main` stays `() -> i32` and is still `_start`; the bootstrap reads its source from stdin (`read(0, …)`) and writes its ELF to stdout (`write(1, …)`), so the shell redirect supplies the filename. It touches no existing path: a program that never names `read`/`openat`/`close`/`fstat` compiles to the bytes it always did.
- **Coverage: `tests/syscall-io-checks.sh`, registered in `ci.sh`, with the shipped example `examples/copy.ast` and its C twin `tests/copy-twin.c` (both a stdin→stdout cat).** (1) a strict `-Werror` rebuild; (2) **5 gcc-differential cases** — a 256-byte stdin cat, a 7-byte-chunked cat (the count carried across loop iterations is what a clobbered x0 corrupts), `openat`+`read`+`write`+`close` of a real file, `fstat` dumping the 8 raw bytes of `st_size` (offset 48 on AArch64), and `close` of a bad fd returning raw `-EBADF`; (3) **7 rejections** — the argument contract for all four intrinsics; (4) **2 mutation probes** — renumber `fstat` 80→79 (must diverge from the C oracle) and delete the `read` dispatch (must stop compiling); each asserts both the break and the "before" fact.
- **Baseline 321 → 324 lines, the other 321 byte-identical.** `tests/rebuild-baseline-v0.23.40.sh` filters this round's `copy` rows (and `fn_type`/`cb_table`/`fn_ret`/`call-stmt`'s, which the 309-row comparison has always filtered), asserts the 309 pre-existing rows reproduce before it writes anything, and lands the total at 324. The new branches are keyed on the intrinsic names and reached only by a program that calls one, so no existing example moves a byte. One resident tripwire moved with the source: `narrow-int-abi-checks.sh`'s `loadStackOff(`/`storeStackOff(` call-site counts (16 → 17, 11 → 12), because `syscallInsn`'s staging loops add one of each — the same increment the suite recorded for `write` in v0.23.21-dev, now carrying both entries in its comment.

## v0.23.39-dev — a discarded-call statement: `foo();` and `h.onClick(41);`

- **Where this came from.** v0.23.38-dev shipped a callback table and was asked, three times, whether a function still needed a variable to catch its return: *"现在的函数执行还需要一个变量来接收吗？"* — the answer was yes, because Aster has **no expression statement at all**, and the follow-up *"那没返回值的函数也需要吗？"* surfaced the deeper fact — Aster has no `void` and no bare `ptr`'s way to mean "I don't care" — and *"有什么优劣？"* named the trade-off and the narrow form worth taking: allow a call written for its side effect alone, but only a call. This is that round: `foo();` and `h.onClick(41);` discard the result and run the callee; `x;`, `a+b;` and the assignment forms keep their readings, so the language gains no meaningless expression statement.
- **It is deliberately narrow — only a Call or a FieldCall reaches it.** `Statement()` gains a `Stmt::Expr` kind and a new branch that runs *before* the field-assign dispatch, gated by `isCallStatement()` — a peek that walks `h.x.y(` to the `(` without consuming a token, so `h.onClick(41)` (a discarded field call) is told from `h.field = v` (a field store) and from `h.field` (a bare read). `x;` and `a+b;` are not Calls, so they fall through to the existing dispatch and are still refused (*"expected let, assignment, return, if, while, for, break, or continue"*); the assignment forms (`=`, `+=`, `a[i].f = v`) are untouched. The narrowing is pinned in the source guard and in the rejection matrix — a bare variable and an arithmetic expression are still refused, and a discarded call is still checked for argument count (both forms) and for being a function field.
- **The type checker runs first, exactly as a kept call.** `Sema::checkStmt`'s `Stmt::Expr` case calls `typeOf(*s.expr)` and nothing else, so a discarded `f(1, 2);` is still refused (*"wrong argument count calling f: expected 1, got 2"*) and a discarded `h.tag(1);` is still refused (*"cannot call field 'tag': its type is i32, not a function type"*) — the argument-count check lives independently in codegen too, which is why the mutation layer (m2) proves the Sema check by removing the field-kind refusal, not the count. The "fire-and-forget" value of the feature is that the side effect runs; the dropped answer is never bound.
- **Codegen generates the call, then releases the result register.** `gen`'s `Stmt::Expr` arm is `int d=gen(*s.expr); releaseTemp(d);` — the call is emitted at the widths its own type declares (a discarded `i64` call still stages at full width and writes its global), and the temporary `gen` returned (the top of the X0–X15 pool) is released the same way an assignment releases the value it just stored, so the pool cannot leak across statements. The callee's side effects all run; only the answer is dropped.
- **What it does *not* relax.** It does not make `x;` or `a+b;` statements, it does not weaken the assignment/`++`/`+=` positions, and it does not touch the "expressions are side-effect-free" design for anything but a call (a call already runs its body, whether or not the caller keeps the answer — `let r = h.onClick(41) + 0;` was always legal). A discarded call is checked as hard as a kept one, so the only thing the old rule actually blocked was writing the discard explicitly.
- **Coverage: `tests/discard-call-checks.sh` [8/8], registered in `ci.sh`, with the shipped example `examples/call-stmt.ast` and its C twin `tests/call-stmt-twin.c` (both exit 56).** (1) a strict `-Werror` rebuild; (2) **9 source guards** — the `Stmt::Expr` kind, the `isCallStatement()` peek, the parser branch and the statement construction, the Sema case and its `typeOf`, the codegen arm and its `gen`/`releaseTemp`, and the narrowing comment; (3) **9 gcc-differential cases** — a direct call, a struct-field call, an `i64` result, zero arguments, a `bool` result, a `ptr` result, ten arguments with the last two on the stack, a loop, and a "harmless drop" case whose exit depends only on a nearby computation (proof the drop corrupts no register); (4) the example against its twin; (5) **4 rejections** — a bare variable, an arithmetic expression, and wrong argument count on a direct and a field discarded call; (6) a 6-row coverage map; (7) **4 mutants** — the parser branch (a discarded field call must stop compiling), the Sema check (a non-function field call must become accepted), the codegen emit (the side-effect-laden direct call must diverge from C), and the `isCallStatement()` peek (a discarded field call must stop compiling).
- **Baseline 318 → 321 lines, the other 318 byte-identical.** `tests/rebuild-baseline-v0.23.39.sh` filters this round's `call-stmt` rows (and `fn_type`/`cb_table`/`fn_ret`'s, which the 309-row comparison has always filtered), asserts the 309 pre-existing rows reproduce before it writes anything, and lands the total at 321. The discard is pure codegen on top of a normal call, so no existing program reaches a new branch: a kept call compiles to the same bytes, a bare `ptr` still stages at 64-bit width, and a struct's layout is unchanged.

## v0.23.38-dev — a callback table: `fn` fields in a struct, and a function that returns one

- **Where this came from.** v0.23.37-dev gave a function pointer a signature — `let fp: fn(i32) -> i32 = inc;` — and closed its own record by naming what it had deliberately left out: *"Two positions are refused with positioned diagnostics: a struct field of function type … and a function returning a function type"*. Those two refusals were the round's own scope line, not a verdict on the design, and they left the shape a callback table actually has unwritable: a `struct` of handlers (`struct Handlers { onClick: fn(i32) -> i32, onMove: fn(i32, i32) -> i32 }`) had to be a `struct` of bare `ptr`s, so every call site was unchecked and staged at the legacy 64-bit width. This round opens both positions, and adds the one call form that makes the field worth declaring — `h.onClick(x)`, a call written where the field is.
- **A field carries a signature beside its eight bytes, and the layout is unchanged.** `StructField` gains `FuncSig funcSig; bool isFunc=false;` — the same parallel-signature pattern `Binding`, the slot table and the globals already use — and `structDeclaration()` captures it where it used to throw. The field's storage type is still `Ty::Ptr` and its length is still 8, so every existing struct's layout, offset table and byte-for-byte codegen is untouched; what the signature buys is the **check** at every store into the field (a struct literal and a field assignment) and the **widths** at a call through it. An `fn` field may not be an array (*"an array of function pointers is not supported: Handlers.onClick"*) — a `cb[4]` would be a second, separate feature and a bare `ptr[4]` field is unaffected.
- **`h.onClick(x)` is its own expression kind, and that is the whole reason it works.** `Expr::FieldCall` is new, not `Expr::Call` with a flag, because every call site — `gen`, `checkCall`, `callType` — keys on `e.name`, and v0.23.24-dev's "the function always wins" rule would silently reroute a field whose name matches a function in scope: with `fn onClick` beside `Handlers.onClick`, `h.onClick(41)` would call the *function* and answer 100 instead of 42. The suite pins that case (`ct_clash`) and the mutation layer pins the parser branch that builds the kind. A `Field` node is reused as the callee (`e.a`), `e.name` stays empty, and `postfix()`'s loop admits a `(` after a field.
- **A call through a field is checked and staged by the field's own signature.** `Sema::checkFieldCall` looks the field up from the host struct's type: a field that is not `fn` is refused (*"cannot call field 'tag': its type is i32, not a function type"*), the **argument count** is checked (*"wrong argument count calling field 'cb': expected 1, got 2"*), each argument runs through the shared `checkArgument`, and the call's type is the signature's **return type**. The staging sequence already knew how to read a signature (v0.23.37-dev), so `gen`'s `FieldCall` arm is one line — `emitCallSeq(e, fs, 0, 0, true)` — and an `i64` return is read at full width, a `bool` one as a bool, a `ptr` one as eight bytes.
- **The callee is read *before* the arguments take X0–X7, and this is the one codegen decision the round had to get right.** `emitStructAddr` — the address of `h.cb`, whether `h` is a local, a `ptr<S>` parameter or a nested field — needs a temporary register, and the temporary pool **is** X0–X15. Reading the callee after the argument loads (the position an ordinary indirect call reads its own, from a symbol) would hand the field's address to a register that already holds argument 1, so the field callee is read into X9 immediately after `--callDepth` and before the register loop, and the ordinary path keeps its old instruction exactly (`if(indirect&&e.k!=Expr::FieldCall)emitCalleeAddr(9,e.name);` — a bare-ptr indirect call emits the same bytes it always did). The suite pins both arms and the mutation layer puts the read back in the wrong place.
- **A function may now return a function pointer, with the signature in the return slot.** `fn pick(which: i32) -> fn(i32) -> i32 { … return inc; }` captures the signature in `Function::returnFuncSig`/`returnIsFunc` where the parser used to refuse; the driver collects it into a `returnFuncSigMap` that Sema reads per function (`retIsFunc`/`retFuncSig`), and `Stmt::Return` checks a returned pointer against it — so `return wide;` where the function promised `fn(i32) -> i32` is a compile error at the `return`, not a mismatch discovered at the first call. The value a call hands back **answers** that signature too (`funcSigOfExpr` consults `returnFuncSig`), which is what makes `apply(pick(1), 20)` one check spanning two functions: the getter's promise and the callee's parameter, compared where the argument is written.
- **The parameter side is keyed by function name, because a callee's declaration has to be visible at every call site.** v0.23.37-dev carried parameter signatures *inside* the `Function` being checked, which is enough for the call sites in that function's own body and no others — a call in a different function could not see what `apply`'s parameter asked for. `ParamSig { bool isFunc; FuncSig sig; }` plus a `paramFuncSigMap` collected by the driver fixes that, and the record's `isFunc` flag is what distinguishes `fn() -> i32` (an empty parameter list, a real signature) from "no signature was declared".
- **What a returned address still cannot do.** `pick(0)(20)` is refused (*"cannot call the result of this expression: assign it to a fn(...) variable or a fn(...) field first"*), as is a call on a subscripted field's result — a call's result is not a value you can take an address of, and the refusal says where to put it instead. Assign it to a `fn(...)` variable, to a `fn(...)` field, or hand it to a `fn(...)` parameter: all three are covered, and all three carry the signature with them.
- **Coverage: `tests/callback-table-checks.sh` and `tests/function-return-fn-checks.sh`, both [8/8], both registered in `ci.sh`, with `examples/cb_table.ast` (exit 87, twin `tests/callback-table-twin.c`) and `examples/fn_ret.ast` (exit 108, twin `tests/function-return-fn-twin.c`).** The callback-table suite: (1) a strict `-Werror` rebuild; (2) **22 source guards** — the field's signature member, its capture and its arrival in the layout table, the array refusal, the `FieldCall` kind and the two parser branches, `funcSigOfExpr`/`sigEqual`/`checkPtrSig`, the field-call type check, the parameter map, and the three codegen arms including both sides of the callee-read position; (3) **12 gcc-differential cases** — a local host, a `ptr<H>` parameter called through and reassigned inside a helper, the **name-clash** case (42, not 100), the `i64` return width, zero arguments, ten with the last two on the stack, a nested host, a read-out into a `fn` variable, reassignment, a loop, and `bool`/`ptr` returns; (4) the example against its twin; (5) **8 rejections** — a literal and an assignment of the wrong signature, the wrong argument count, a non-`fn` field, an array of fn-pointers, a call on a call's result, a subscripted field, and a global struct constant holding one; (6) a 7-row coverage map; (7) **6 mutants** — the value resolver, the comparison, the field capture, `gen`'s signature hand-off (the `i64` case must diverge), the callee-read position (the `ptr<H>` case must diverge), and the parser's `FieldCall`. The function-return suite: (1)–(2) **16 source guards** pinning the return slot, its capture, sema's seed and check, the resolver that answers a call's result, the `ParamSig` record and the parameter map, the two driver collections, and the on-the-spot refusal; (3) **12 gcc-differential cases** — the getter itself, the **hand-off** (`apply(pick(1), 12)`, the case the return slot exists for), the `i64` width, the empty parameter list, `bool`/`ptr` returns, two getters of different signature, reassignment, a loop, ten arguments, a global `fn` slot, and a value landing in a `fn` **field** (both halves of the round in one line); (4) the example against its twin; (5) **6 rejections** — a returned fn-pointer of the wrong signature, wrong argument count, wrong return type only, handed to a parameter wanting another signature, stored into a field wanting another signature, and called on the spot; (6) a 7-row coverage map; (7) **6 mutants** — the return check, sema's seed, the parser's capture, the driver's collection, the parameter side, and the on-the-spot refusal.
- **Eight pre-existing suites moved a pin, and the full CI caught every one.** The round's first full run came back 8 red, and none of them was a behaviour: six suites count baseline rows and had to be told about the two new examples (each answered 294 instead of 288); `indirect-call-checks.sh` pinned `if(indirect)emitCalleeAddr(9,e.name);` in both a guard and its mutant m3; `wide-int-literal-checks.sh` counts SXTW sites and this round's field call added the eleventh (an `i32` result read at the field's declared return width); and `struct-array-field-checks.sh`, `multi-dim-array-checks.sh` and `sizeof-len-checks.sh` spelled a `StructField`, an `s.fields.push_back` and `Expr::Kind`'s tail that this round widened — the last of which is now pinned *without* the enum's tail, so a later round adding a kind does not have to move it again. `struct-array-field`'s m2 mutant spelled `postfix()`'s loop before it gained a `(` arm.
- **The v0.23.37 suite is re-pointed, not replaced.** `tests/function-type-checks.sh` pinned the two refusals this round retires, in its guard layer and in its rejection matrix, so both are now inverted: the guards assert the retired text **stays retired** (a refusal nobody watches for is worse than no refusal), the matrix refuses what the opened positions now allow *inside* them (a returned fn-pointer of the wrong signature, an array of fn-pointers), and its 8 mutants were re-pointed at the new lines (m7 is the return-slot check, m8 the array refusal). Its 15 differential cases and its 7-row coverage map are unchanged and still pass — the proof, from the other side, that opening a refused position did not disturb what the refusal was protecting.
- **Baseline 312 → 318 lines, the other 312 byte-identical.** `tests/rebuild-baseline-v0.23.38.sh` filters this round's `cb_table` and `fn_ret` rows (and `fn_type`'s, which the 309-row comparison has always filtered), asserts the 309 pre-existing rows reproduce before it writes anything, and lands the total at 318. Nothing that does not declare a function type reaches a new branch: a bare `ptr` still stages every scalar argument at 64-bit width and still answers `i32`, a bare-`ptr` indirect call walks exactly the instructions it always did, and a struct with no `fn` field lays out as it always has.
- **What this deliberately does *not* do.** It does not add an array of fn-pointers (`cb[4]`), a fn-pointer **parameter of a fn-pointer parameter**, a nested function type as an *argument* type (`fn(fn(i32) -> i32) -> i32`), or a call on a call's result; it does not make the signature part of `Ty` (it still rides beside the value, which is why every pre-existing row reproduces); and it does not change what a bare `ptr` does anywhere — the round widens where a signature may be *declared*, not what an undeclared pointer means.

## v0.23.37-dev — a function pointer that carries its signature: `fn(P1,..,Pn) -> R`

- **Where this came from.** The v0.23.24-dev round shipped *"the round the candidate queue had named as a language-design round of its own"* — `op(x)`, a call through a `ptr` variable — and closed it with one design decision stated outright: *"There is no function type, so there is no signature"*, hence every scalar argument rode at 64-bit width and the call answered `i32`, and its own "deliberately not included" line named the follow-up: *"a function type (typed variables, signature checking — the larger round that would retire the 64-bit staging rule)"*. This is that round. A function pointer may now be declared **with its signature** — `let fp: fn(i32) -> i32 = inc;` — and an indirect call through it is checked and code-generated exactly like the direct call it stands for.
- **The signature rides *beside* the ptr, never inside `Ty`.** The storage type of a typed fn-pointer is still `Ty::Ptr` — an address is an address — so `struct FuncSig { std::vector<Ty> params; Ty ret=Ty::I32; };` is carried in parallel to the binding, the slot and the global, following the exact pattern `paramDims`/`slotDims` set for array dimensions: `Stmt`/`Global`/`Binding` gain `FuncSig funcSig; bool isFunc=false;`, `Function` gains `paramFuncSig`/`paramIsFunc` for the parameter form, and Codegen gains `slotFuncSig`/`slotIsFunc` (frame slot → signature) plus a `globalFuncSig` map. Because the type itself is untouched, **a bare `ptr` behaves exactly as it did**: its `funcSigOf` answers null, the legacy 64-bit staging and `i32` answer stand, and every program written before this round compiles to the same bytes — 309 of 309 baseline rows reproduce.
- **The parser learns one type spelling, captured at three declaration sites.** `parseType`'s new `case TK::Fn:` branch reads `fn (` params `) -> R`, stashes the result in `pendingFuncType`/`pendingFuncSig` and answers `Ty::Ptr`; the local `let`, the parameter list and the global declaration each capture the pending signature into their record and reset the flags (`needType` clears them at entry so a stray pending value cannot leak into an unrelated type). Two positions are **refused with positioned diagnostics**: a struct field of function type (*"a struct field cannot have a function type (use a pointer instead)"*) and a function returning a function type (*"a function cannot return a function type"*). A global fn-pointer **may be left uninitialized** (`let gop: fn(i32) -> i32;` zero-fills into `.bss`, the one spelling the new branch had to add) — exactly as a global `ptr` always could.
- **Sema checks a typed call like the direct call it stands for.** `Sema` gained a `globalFuncSig` map (file-scope signatures) and a `funcSigOf` resolver (local binding, then global), and `checkCall`'s indirect branch now consults it: the **argument count** is checked, each argument runs through the same `checkArgument` a direct call uses (so an `i32` argument to an `i64` parameter is the same legal widening it always was, and a `bool` to an `i32` parameter is the same refusal), and the call's type is the signature's **return type**. A bare `ptr` callee keeps the v0.23.24 rule unchanged: a struct argument is still refused (*"a composite needs a signature to lay it out"*) and the answer is still `i32`.
- **Codegen stages by the signature and reads the result by it.** `emitCallSeq` takes `const FuncSig* sig` and its `at()` lambda answers `sig->params[i]` when there is one — which is what retires the 64-bit staging rule for typed callees: an `i32` argument rides in a `W` register, an `i64` one in a full `X` register, and a **struct argument is laid out as a composite** (the 12-byte `Tri` case splits across two registers) instead of being refused. The result type `rt` is the signature's `ret` for an indirect call, so an `i64` return is read at full width (the old rule collapsed it to `i32` — the suite pins this with a case nested inside an addition, because `emitCallSeq` only MOVs its result when the destination temporary register is non-zero), and `callType` answers the signature's return type so `let r: i64 = fp(x);` type-checks. The `at()` priority is load-bearing **only** for the struct case: for scalars the 64-bit stage already served both widths (the v0.23.24 design), which is why the mutant layer pins the struct witness rather than a scalar one.
- **Two independent resolvers, one contract.** Sema's `funcSigOf` reads `Binding`/`globalFuncSig` (`find`), Codegen's reads `slotFuncSig`/`globalFuncSig` (`lookup`) — the same split every other type property has, so the two passes cannot disagree about a signature any more than they can about a stride. `compileProgram` builds the file-scope map once (`if(g.isFunc) globalFuncSigMap[g.name]=g.funcSig;`) and hands it to both passes.
- **Coverage: `tests/function-type-checks.sh` [8/8], registered in `ci.sh`, with the shipped example `examples/fn_type.ast` and its C twin `tests/function-type-twin.c` (both exit 115).** (1) a strict `-Werror` rebuild; (2) **source guards** pinning every piece — the `FuncSig` record, the parser's `fn` branch and its capture at all three declaration sites, the two refusals, the uninitialized-global branch, `Binding`'s fields, both `funcSigOf` resolvers (and that there are exactly two), `checkCall`'s signature check, the `gen` call that hands the signature over, `emitCallSeq`'s signature-first `at()` and signature `rt`, `callType`'s typed answer, and the `globalFuncSigMap` wiring; (3) **15 gcc-differential cases** — a local, reassignment to two functions, the `i64`-argument width centrepiece, an `i64` return, a negative `i64`, `bool`/`u32` returns, a global (assigned at run time, and called at `i64` width), a fn-pointer **parameter** applied inside a helper, zero arguments, three mixed-class arguments, ten arguments with the ninth/tenth on the stack, a **struct by value** (12 bytes, two registers), a loop and nesting; (4) the shipped example against its twin; (5) **9 rejections** — wrong count (local and global), a `ptr`/`bool` argument to an `i32` parameter, a non-`bool` to a `bool` parameter, the two type misspellings (no arrow, no parentheses), and the two position refusals; (6) a 7-row coverage map; (7) **8 mutants** — sema's resolver (a struct argument must stop compiling), the staging width (a struct must stop compiling), the result width (the `i64` return must diverge), the `gen` hand-off (diverge), the parser's `fn` branch (a typed declaration must stop compiling), the global wiring (diverge), and the two refusals (each must lose its diagnostic).
- **The v0.23.24 suite is re-pointed, not replaced.** `tests/indirect-call-checks.sh`'s source guards followed `emitCallSeq`'s new signature and its `at()`/`rt` guards pin both arms — the signature-first width **and** the bare-`ptr` fallback (`return indirect?Ty::I64:…` stays pinned, because the fallback *is* the contract) — and its whole battery, differential cases and 8 mutants still pass unchanged. That is the proof of the round's central claim from the other side: the new feature did not disturb the old one.
- **Baseline 309 → 312 lines, the other 309 byte-identical.** `tests/rebuild-baseline-v0.23.37.sh` filters this round's `fn_type` rows, asserts the 309 pre-existing rows reproduce before it writes anything, and lands the total at 312. This closes the v0.23.24-dev *"no function type"* design decision — the 64-bit staging rule is retired for typed callees and stands, untouched, for bare `ptr`.

## v0.23.36-dev — `--bounds-check` now guards array *parameter* subscripts too

- **Where this came from.** Two rounds left this exact gap open on purpose: v0.23.34-dev noted *"the array length is not part of the parameter type … so there is no bounds check on a parameter — same as C"*, and v0.23.35-dev closed the length into the parameter only for `sizeof`/`len` — *"the dimensions answer these two operators and nothing else: a parameter subscript stays unchecked"* — and its validation report named the next round outright (*"a bounds-checked array parameter is the round that would close that"*). This is that round. It is **not** a language change: `--bounds-check` already existed and already trapped local and global subscripts; a parameter was the one array shape it could not see, because the two length tables everything else reads from — `localArrayLength[slot]` and `arrayLengths[name]` — were never filled for a parameter.
- **The fix is a third, narrow length table that nothing else reads.** `Codegen` gained `paramArrayLength[slot]` — frame slot → total element count of an **array parameter** — filled in the parameter-setup pass from the dimensions `f.paramDims[i]` the v0.23.35 round already carries (`paramArrayLength[slot] = dimsProduct(f.paramDims[i])`), and consulted **only** by the two bounds-check sites. It is deliberately *not* `localArrayLength`, because `gen(Var)` keys off `localArrayLength.count(slot)` to decide whether a bare `a` resolves to the frame slot or to the pointer value stored in the slot (v0.23.34-dev): handing the parameter a `localArrayLength` entry would make `a` silently resolve to the frame slot and silently break every call that relies on the decayed pointer. The existing `arrayLengthOf`/`gen(Var)`/`AddrOf` type machinery is untouched, which is exactly why a default build moves not a byte.
- **One read path and one write path, both guarded, identically to local/global.** A 1-D read `a[i]` routes through `emitElemAddr`'s pointer branch; after the index register is generated, the round calls `checkArrayBounds(paramArrayLength[slot], index)` then `emitBoundsCheck(paramArrayLength[slot], index, r)` — the same `CMP widx, #len` + `B.LO` over `BRK #1` (encoding `0xd4200020`) that local and global arrays emit, so an out-of-range parameter read raises **SIGTRAP, exit 133**, under exactly the same flag. A write `a[i] = v` routes through `gen(Stmt::Assign)`; the round adds the same pair of calls when the assigned name resolves to a slot holding a parameter length. A compile-time-constant out-of-range subscript (`a[4]` on an `i32[4]`) is still rejected by the always-on `checkArrayBounds` named-parameter overload, **with or without** the flag — same message, `array index out of bounds for 'a': index 4, length 4`.
- **Multi-dimensional parameters check per dimension for free.** A 2-D parameter `a[i][j]` already feeds `slotDims[slot]` from `f.paramDims[i]` (the same line that fills `paramArrayLength`), so the existing `emitIndexAddr` per-dimension walk (`indexChainOf` → one `emitBoundsCheck` per level) applies automatically — no second code path. The round's own suite proves a row OOB and a column OOB each trap under the flag while a valid `a[1][2]` runs clean.
- **Opt-in, and silent by default — structurally.** `emitBoundsCheck` opens with `if(!boundsCheck) return;`, so a default build emits no test and a parameter subscript behaves exactly as before (and exactly as C). The feature adds no example, no syntax and no change to the two length tables that codegen reads for addressing, so the byte baseline is unchanged: **309 of 309** rows reproduce, and the round adds no `examples/*.ast` row.
- **Coverage: `tests/array-param-bounds-checks.sh` [8/9], registered in `ci.sh` beside `array-address-checks.sh`, with `tests/array-param-bounds-twin.c` (the gcc twin `pick(int a[4], int k){return a[k];}`, both exiting 6).** (1) a strict `-Werror` rebuild; (2) **source guards** pinning the new member (`paramArrayLength`), its init fill (`slotDims[slot]=f.paramDims[i]` and `paramArrayLength[slot]=dimsProduct(...)`), the read-side guard (`if(paramArrayLength.count(slot)){`), the write-side guard (`if(!s.name.empty()){int aslot=lookup(s.name);`), and that `arrayLengthOf` still consults `localArrayLength` (the separation the design depends on); (3) **acceptance under qemu** — in-range read and write through a parameter return the same value with and without `--bounds-check`; (4) **trap acceptance** — out-of-range read, write and negative parameter subscripts raise SIGTRAP (133) under the flag and do not trap by default; (5) **multi-dimensional parameter** checked per dimension (valid index runs; row/col OOB trap); (6) **compile-time rejection** — a constant out-of-bounds parameter read is refused at compile time, flag or not; (7) **a gcc-differential pair** — Aster (`--bounds-check`) against the cross-compiled twin for in-range parameter reads; (8) **a 2-mutant layer** — dropping the `paramArrayLength` init must make the trap go away (the mutant no longer traps), and dropping the two read-side check calls must make the out-of-range read fall through without trapping.
- **What this deliberately does *not* do.** It does not change `gen(Var)`'s frame-slot/pointer resolution for a parameter, so `return a;` stays refused and a parameter subscript's *stride* is unchanged — only its *run-time bounds* gain a guard. It does not make a parameter subscript checked by default; the `--bounds-check` opt-in rule the v0.23.1 round set stands. And it does not re-record the byte baseline: `tests/rebuild-baseline-v0.23.36.sh` is unnecessary because there is no new example. This closes the v0.23.34-dev/v0.23.35-dev *"parameter bounds check absent"* line — the one item the previous two array rounds named as out of scope — for the run-time guard, while the compile-time `sizeof`/`len` answers were already closed in v0.23.35-dev.

## v0.23.35-dev — `sizeof` and `len`, and an array parameter that remembers its length

- **Where this came from.** The roadmap carried two "ask the compiler" questions a program could not ask: *"sizeof, and a way to ask an array for its length in an expression"* — the very item v0.23.31-dev had listed as out of scope. Both are small, both fix a real gap (a program had to hard-code `4`, `16`, `4` and re-derive them at every use), and they turned out to share almost all their machinery, so they are one round.
- **`sizeof` answers bytes; `len` answers the element count.** `sizeof(i32)` is 4, `sizeof(i64)` is 8, `sizeof(ptr)` is 8 (a pointer is an address, exactly as in C), `sizeof(i32[4])` is 16, `sizeof(S)` is the struct's laid-out size, and `sizeof(row.cells)` is an array field's. `len(i32[4])` is 4 and — the point that separates it from the row count — `len(i64[2][3])` is **6**, the product of the dimensions, not 2. Both spellings work: a **type** (`sizeof(i32[4])`, `len(P)`) or an **expression** (`sizeof(w)`, `sizeof(row)`, `len(a)`, `len(s.xs)`).
