- **Coverage: `tests/condition-checks.sh` [9/9], registered in `ci.sh` after `comment-checks.sh`.** (1) a strict `-Werror` rebuild; (2) **source guards** — the new tokens, the lexer mapping, the `boolLit` flag, both `typeOf` twins, the `constantInteger` guard, both new `genBool` forms, `cmpZeroX`, the integer condition path, the new diagnostic message (both copies), all 16 `rejectBool` sites, and both initializer gates; (3) **21 behavioural cases under qemu** — every literal branch shape, `i32`/`i64`/`u8`/`u16`/`u32` truthiness, `while(0)`, negative values, `while(true)` with an early return, a call as a condition, a `bool` struct field, `?:` arms, `&&`/`||`/`!`; (4) **4 gcc-differential cases** (wide zero, high-bit `u32`, literal branches, loop + `?:` conditions — exit statuses compared, no hand-computed expectations); (5) **31 rejections**; (6) a 17-row coverage map; (7) a **7-mutant layer** (drop integer conditions, narrow the 64-bit zero test, fold the literal into `constantInteger`, drop an operator's `rejectBool`, bypass the global gate, void the struct field's value — each must turn a battery red); (8) **285/285 of the A/B baseline reproduces** — 282 pre-existing rows byte-identical; (9) the shipped example's exit status.
- **Three resident suites were updated on the record, not quietly broken.** `bool-checks.sh` and `for-loop-checks.sh` each pinned the *old* rejection of a bare integer condition (`if (x)`, `for (…; i + 1; …)`) — assertions of the very restriction this round lifts. Both were inverted into acceptance checks, and their ptr-condition cases were rewritten to keep the "not a truthy type" boundary pinned. `struct-checks.sh`'s struct-condition rejection message was updated to the new wording. The old byte-hash assertions were never weakened: they passed with the round's compiler before any edit.
- **Byte-level: 282 of 282 pre-existing outputs reproduce byte for byte** — asserted by `struct-checks.sh` [4/8] with the round's compiler — and the 3 new rows are `examples/condition.ast`'s three output modes. A rebuild script (`tests/rebuild-baseline-v0.23.25.sh`) refuses to write the baseline unless every pre-existing row reproduces, so a silent re-recording is impossible.
- **What this deliberately does *not* do.** It does not make **`bool` a numeric type**: `bool` is not implicitly an `i32` anywhere (no `let x: i32 = true`), integers are not implicitly `bool` outside conditions, and the truthiness rule does not spread to `?:`/`&&`/`||`/`!` — those remain strictly typed, so `a || b` still requires both sides to be `bool`. It does not add a `bool`-typed `while`-else, `elif`/`elsif` keyword sugar, or a `truthy`/`falsy` intrinsic. It does not touch the comparison fast path's codegen, which is why the byte baseline holds.

## v0.23.24-dev — the missing half of function values: `op(x)`, a call through a `ptr` variable

- **Where this came from.** Taking a function's address has worked since the callback-table work: a function name in a value position *is* its address, and that `ptr` could be stored, passed as an argument and handed to C. What it could not do was be **called**. Every call site the compiler emitted branched through a relocated `BL`, and a `BLR` did not exist in the emitter — so the callback shape (`fn apply(op: ptr, x: i32) -> i32 { return op(x); }`, a table of operations, a dispatcher, a comparator) was one instruction away and unreachable. The roadmap's candidate queue named it explicitly: *"function-pointer calls (a language-design round of its own — taking a function's address shipped long ago, but Aster never emits a `BLR`, so it cannot call through one)."*
- **The language surface is one sentence: `fp(args)` where `fp` is a `ptr` variable — local, global, or parameter.** The parser needed **no change at all**: `primary()` has always produced `Call{name}` for `Ident(`, so the entire feature is dispatch in the three call sites that already exist. Codegen's `Call` case asks `functionArities` first (a function name in call position is still a **direct** call — the function always wins, exactly the rule by which a user function shadows an intrinsic) and, failing that, the new `indirectCallee()` asks whether the name is a `ptr` local (not an array) or a `ptr` global (not an array); only then does the call branch. `emitCallSeq` gained one parameter, `bool indirect=false`, and the direct path is byte-for-byte what it was.
- **There is no function type, so there is no signature — and that is the round's one real design decision.** *(Closed in v0.23.37-dev: a function pointer may now be declared `fn(P1,..,Pn) -> R`, its signature is checked and staged by, and the call answers its return type. A bare `ptr` keeps the rule below, unchanged.)* A `ptr` variable carries an address, not a signature, so no arity check, no parameter types, no return type beyond the fixed answer. The consequence for arguments: **every scalar argument rides at 64-bit width** (`at(i)` answers `Ty::I64` for an indirect call, whatever the expression's own type). One width serves both kinds of callee: one declaring `i32` reads the low half — exactly the bits a 32-bit stage would have put there — and one declaring `i64` reads a *properly extended* full register, because AAPCS64 leaves a sub-64-bit argument's upper half unspecified and `gen()` already extends each source type the right way (`SXTW` for an `i32`, free zero-extension for the unsigned widths through their load form, the pointer's own eight bytes). The call answers **`i32`**: a callee returning `i64` has its low half delivered, and a 64-bit consumer sign-extends it — the same contract a direct call's `i32` return has always had. The first cut of this rule staged only *over-wide literals* at 64 bits; that leaves an ABI hole the round's own probes found — an `i32` variable or a small negative literal into an `i64` parameter would ride with an unspecified upper half — so the rule is uniform, and the `call_var64`/`call_neg64` differential cases pin it (194=194, 47=47).
- **The callee's address lives in X9, and the window it needs is free.** `emitCalleeAddr` materializes it **after** the arguments are in their registers and **before** SP moves: X0–X7 hold the arguments, X8 is the hidden result pointer when there is one, and X9–X15 hold nothing live — every live temporary is parked in the frame and the staging's own temporaries have been released. The stack copies below touch only X16/X17, so X9 survives to the `BLR X9` (`0xd63f0000|(Rn<<5)`). No park/restore change, and therefore no risk to the direct path. The local case loads the slot (`LDR Xreg, [SP, #slot*8]`); the **global case must load what the global *holds***, not the global's own address — the first cut of this round shipped the `movX(reg,kGlobalBase)` form and segfaulted (branching into `.data`), caught by a smoke test before the suite existed and now pinned by mutation m6, which restores exactly that line and requires `call_global` to diverge.
- **The second half of the round is the static linker, and it was a reachability precondition, not an optimization.** `let fp: ptr = f;` at global scope failed to link with *"global address relocation references a function symbol 'f'"* — a restriction written for `.so` output, whose text cannot carry load-time relocations and whose function addresses therefore always travel through GOT slots. The restriction had never been visible because sema refused the *call* before the linker ever saw the program. Static images (ET_EXEC, static PIE) know function addresses at link time and `ADRP`/`ADD` is PC-relative, so `resolveRelocations` gained a `funcAddrDirect` parameter that `linkObjects` (and therefore `build-elf`/`build-pie`/`link-obj`/`link-pie`) passes as `true`; the `.so` path passes it as `false` and is untouched.
- **Sema's half of the contract is a rejection matrix, because a wrong callee is a branch to nowhere.** A non-`ptr` local (`i32`/`bool`/struct), a local or global *array*, an `i32` global, an undeclared name and a **struct argument** (a composite needs a signature to lay it out — register class, copy area, hidden result pointer — and a `ptr` variable has none) are each refused with a diagnostic that names the offending thing; a `bool` consumed from the result is refused by the existing assignment rule; and an **extern function's address** is refused explicitly ("its location is another object's business") rather than falling through to "unknown variable". The struct-argument refusal is a *diagnostic* layer, not the only guard — codegen's struct-as-value rule refuses the same program with a worse message — and mutation m8 pins exactly that: remove sema's line and the specific diagnostic must vanish (the program stays refused, with the worse message).
- **Coverage: `tests/indirect-call-checks.sh` [7/7], registered in `ci.sh` after `icon-text-demo-checks.sh`.** (1) a strict `-Werror` rebuild; (2) 16 source guards pinning `indirectCallee`, the `emitCallSeq(e,0,0,true)` dispatch, `emitCalleeAddr(9,e.name)`, the `BLR` encoding, the 64-bit staging line, the global load form, both sema messages, the extern diagnostic and the linker flag *including its call site*; (3) a probe that SKIPs cleanly without an AArch64 C compiler or a runner; (4) a **19-case differential battery**, every case an Aster source and a C twin compiled by `aarch64-linux-gnu-gcc -O2 -fwrapv` and run under qemu, exit statuses compared — never a hand-computed expectation: the five callee forms (local, reassignment, trampoline parameter, runtime-initialized global, and `load_ptr(&struct)` — the dlsym shape), argument forms (none, one, ten with the ninth/tenth on the stack, a wide literal and an `i32` variable into `i64` parameters, a small negative literal, a `ptr`, a `bool`, mixed widths, negatives), call forms (in a loop, nested as an argument, from a helper that calls through the pointer twice), and the precedence case where a function wins over a same-named `ptr` variable in call position; (4b) a shared-object structural check — the `.so` builds, contains a register branch, and its text carries **no** load-time relocation; (4c) the shipped example run against its C twin (`examples/indirect_call.ast` and `tests/indirect-call-example-twin.c` both exit 144 — the byte baseline records the example's build modes, this records that it still *runs* to the number its header claims); (5) the **10-rejection matrix** above; (6) a **coverage map** naming the ten contract regressions that must stay resident; (7) an **8-mutant layer** — dispatch dropped, `BLR` demoted to `BR`, callee not materialized, sema resolution dropped, 64-bit staging reverted to 32-bit, the global callee loading its own address, the linker's function-address permission withdrawn, and the sema struct-argument diagnostic removed.
- **Two mutation-layer corrections are on the record, because both were the layer doing its job.** m5 (staging reverted to 32-bit) initially pointed at `call_wide_literal` and **survived**: the probe's observable is the callee's `i64` result read at `i32` width, and addition is congruence-preserving mod 2^32, so the low half of the sum cannot see a truncated argument — a blind probe, not a weak mutant. The battery gained `call_wide_shift` (`a >> 32` — the answer *is* the argument's upper half, 232 vs 0) and m5 now diverges on it. m4's sed replacement (`if(const Binding* b=…)` → `if(false)`) also failed to build, because the block's body uses `b`; it now uses the C++17 if-init form (`if(const Binding* b=find(e.name);false)`), which keeps the declaration and skips the block.
- **Byte-level: 279 of 279 pre-existing outputs reproduce byte for byte** — asserted by `struct-checks.sh` [4/8] with the round's compiler — and 3 new rows record `examples/indirect_call.ast` across `build-so` / `build-elf` / `build-obj --pic`, taking the baseline from 279 to 282 lines with zero changes to the old 279. This holds *by construction*, not by re-measurement: the direct-call path is the `indirect=false` branch and is untouched, the `.so` path is untouched, the linker change only *permits* programs that were previously rejected, and no pre-existing example can contain an indirect call (it used to be a compile error).
- **The first full CI run failed one suite, and it was a count guard doing its job.** `wide-int-literal-checks.sh` [2/7] pins "total SXTW sites: expected 8 occurrences of `0x93407c00u`, found 9" — the round added a ninth: an indirect call's result is `i32` by contract, so a 64-bit consumer gets the same sign-extension the direct call's leaf has always given. That is a *leaf* widening itself from its own type, which is rule (2), not a post-hoc consumer widening — but the guard's comment said "eight leaf sites and nowhere else", and the letter of that claim was now false. The count is 9 with the reason recorded at the guard, and the mechanical re-evaluation of every other count guard in the tree found no second casualty. It is the third round in a row where a bulk-ish addition invalidated someone else's pin, which is why the re-evaluation is mechanical rather than remembered.
- **What this deliberately does *not* do.** It does not add a **function type** — `fn`-typed variables, typed parameters, signature checking — which is a larger language-design round and the thing that would make the 64-bit staging rule unnecessary. It does not support calling through a **computed** pointer: `tbl[0] = inc;` still meets the pre-existing "pointer indexing/assignment is not supported yet" limitation (recorded, not lifted — the memory-resident shape via `load_ptr(&struct)` works and is the documented route, exactly the dlsym shape). It does not deliver an `i64` result (the call answers `i32`; wrap the low half). And it does not touch the trampoline's *own* calling convention: `apply(op: ptr, x: i32)` is an ordinary direct call whose body contains the indirect one.

## v0.23.23-dev — the raw-memory width ladder is complete: `load_u16`/`store_u16` and `load_i64`/`store_i64`

- **Where this came from.** The round-4 difficulty audit ranked **narrow memory intrinsics** first, and for an honest reason: the byte pair (`load_u8`/`store_u8`, v0.22.6-dev) made raw memory reachable one byte at a time, and `load_ptr` (v0.22.9-dev's family) reads pointers — but the two widths real data actually arrives in, 16-bit and 64-bit, had no spelling. Reading an `i64` out of a buffer meant eight `load_u8`s, seven shifts and seven ors, and that assembly is **wrong in a way a self-comparison cannot see**: `<< 32` on an `i32` is a 32-bit shift whose hardware count wraps (`1 << 32` is `1`, measured this round: the isolated probe returns `(1<<32)%251 = 1`), so the high word comes back as the low word. In C the same read is one line. This is an "API-missing" difficulty rather than a deep one — the LDRH/STRH/LDR X/STR X *encodings* had existed since the stack-path work (`loadVar64`/`storeVar64`, the `u16` global and stack paths all emit them) — which is exactly why it survived: nothing was broken, four names simply did not exist.
- **The feature is four intrinsics in the established shape, dispatched at three sites each.** `checkCall` (sema), `callType` (codegen's type answer) and `gen` (the `Call` case) each gained a branch per intrinsic, every one behind the v0.23.21-dev shadow guard `&&!arities.count(e.name)` / `&&!functionArities.count(e.name)` — **the user's function wins, always**, now proven for eight intrinsic names. The contracts mirror the byte pair exactly: `load_u16(p) -> i32` zero-extends one halfword (0..65535, `LDRH Wa, [Xa]`); `store_u16(p, v) -> i32` writes `v & 0xFFFF` (`STRH Wv, [Xn]`) and answers the **original** `v` — the byte pair's documented contract, *not* C's assignment-expression value; `load_i64(p) -> i64` answers the full 64 bits (`LDR Xa, [Xa]`); `store_i64(p, v) -> i64` writes eight bytes (`STR Xv, [Xn]`) and answers the original `v`. `callType` answers `Ty::I64` for the two quadword names (the halfword pair shares the existing `i32` fallthrough, like the byte pair); the halfword and byte pairs need no `pointerReturnFunctions` registration because they answer integers, and `exprYieldsPtr` is untouched. No new encoding, no frame-layout change, no ABI change: the four edits are pure dispatch, which is why the A/B baseline holds by construction.
- **The one genuinely new rule: `store_i64` widens an `i32` by sign-extension.** `gen(*e.args[1], Ty::I64)` is what applies it — the same rule that stores an `i32` into an `i64` local (`Var`'s SXTW, `Num`'s `emitMov64`) — so `store_i64(p, -1)` lands eight `0xFF` bytes and reads back as `-1`. Without it the value is stored zero-extended (`0x00000000FFFFFFFF`) and the round's showcase case silently disagrees with C. `store_i64` also **rejects a `bool` second argument** (sema and codegen both): no `i64` slot accepts a bool anywhere else in the language, and this store is not where that changes. `store_u16` keeps the byte store's exact contract (a `ptr` second argument is rejected; an `i64` one truncates to its low 16 bits, the language's documented narrowing).
- **Coverage: `tests/narrow-memory-checks.sh` [7/7], registered in `ci.sh` after `syntax-relief-checks.sh`.** (1) a strict `-Werror` rebuild; (2) **source guards** pinning each of the four names in all three dispatch sites *with its shadow guard*, the `callType` i64 answer, the four encodings (including "`// LDR Xa, [Xa]` must now appear at least twice" — `load_ptr`'s and `load_i64`'s), and the exact widening call `int v=gen(*e.args[1],Ty::I64);…`; (3) a probe that SKIPs cleanly without an AArch64 C compiler or a runner; (4) a **24-case differential battery**, every case an Aster source and a C twin compiled by `aarch64-linux-gnu-gcc -O2 -fwrapv` and run under qemu, exit statuses compared — never a hand-computed expectation: `u16` round trip, truncation past 16 bits (`0x1BEEF`), zero-extension read as `i32` *and* as `i64`, an **unaligned** `blob+3` halfword, a loop, a helper; `i64` round trip, the **`0x100000000` trap value** the old shift-and-or lost, the negative and positive `i32` widenings, narrowing back to `i32`, a string literal's bytes at quadword width, read-modify-write, a loop at 1e9 scale, a helper with a `ptr` parameter, and a call result feeding arithmetic directly at `i32` width (the path `callType`'s i64 answer feeds); cross-width aliasing (a halfword store observed through a quadword load — endianness); and six **shadowing** cases — each intrinsic shadowed, a same-signature `load_i64`, and a *partial* shadow where `store_u16` is the user's function while `load_u16` stays intrinsic, proving the guard is per name and not a global switch; (5) an **8-rejection matrix** (an integer where a `ptr` belongs, wrong arity, a string or a `bool` value for `store_i64`, …), each demanding the diagnostic that names the offending argument; (6) a **coverage map** of the twelve contract regressions that must stay resident; (7) a **7-mutant layer** — each intrinsic's dispatch disabled (its case must stop compiling), the `callType` i64 answer removed, the widening call reverted to `gen(*e.args[1])`, and the `load_u16` shadow guard removed — each requiring the battery to go red. The battery earned its keep during authoring: the first `u16_trunc` twin used C's assignment-expression value and "failed" — the twin was wrong, not the compiler, because Aster's store answers the *original* value; the corrected twin (store and capture separated) is what pins the contract now.
- **Byte-level: 279 of 279 of the A/B baseline reproduces** — asserted by `struct-checks.sh` [4/8] with the round's compiler. The 276 pre-existing rows are byte-identical (the new code is reachable only through the four new names; the version constant is not embedded in generated objects), and 3 new rows record `examples/narrow_mem.ast` across `build-so` / `build-elf` / `build-obj --pic` — an example that demonstrates all four intrinsics, the truncation, and the negative-widening case the old assembly got wrong. Baseline file grew 276 → 279 lines; the diff against the previous recording is three additions and zero changes.
- **What this deliberately does *not* do.** It does not add `load_i16`/`load_i8` (a signed narrow load composes from the unsigned one plus the language's arithmetic shift, and the signed-16 spelling was never the felt gap), `load_u64` (there is no `u64` type to answer with), or `u16`-typed intrinsics generally — the byte pair's precedent is "the intrinsic answers `i32`, the width is the access", and expanding the narrow *types* into the expression lattice is a separate, larger round. It does not touch `*p` (the `i32` dereference) or pointer indexing, and it does not change any existing intrinsic's contract by one byte.

## v0.23.22-dev — the grammar's two most-felt gaps: `else` is optional, and literals may be hexadecimal

- **Where this came from.** Two pieces of friction the round-4 difficulty audit had already ranked first, and one the user named directly when approving this round: **`else` was mandatory**. `ifStatement()` ended in `need(TK::Else,"expected else branch")`, so the most common shape in C — `if (c) { … }` with no fallback — was a compile error, and shipped code carried filler: `examples/icon_text_demo.ast` contains an `} else { }` whose only purpose is to satisfy the grammar. Worse, `else { if … }` — the explicit spelling of what `else if` desugars to — did not parse at all unless the inner `if` dragged an else along too, so the desugaring's target was itself illegal. And the lexer only ever accepted decimal digits (the number scan was `while(std::isdigit(...))`, and `unsignedLiteral` was `stoull(…,10)`), so the bit patterns this compiler exists to emit — masks, addresses, wide integers — had to be written in base 10 or not at all; `0x0102030405060708` failed with `expected ';'`, which is how the gap was found in the first place.
- **The feature is two edits and no more.** Optional `else`: the parser's `need(TK::Else,…)` became `if(eat(TK::Else))`. That is the whole change — an absent else leaves `elseBody` empty, codegen's `block({})` emits nothing, and sema's `definitelyReturns` already demanded *both* branches, so a then-only return correctly stops proving that the function returns (the "may fall through without returning" diagnostic still fires, and a rejection case pins that). No codegen path is touched, which is what makes the byte baseline hold *by construction* rather than by re-measurement. Hexadecimal: the number scan takes an optional `0x`/`0X` prefix followed by at least one hex digit — a bare `0x` is a lexer error carrying a position, not a confusing parser error three tokens later — and `unsignedLiteral` selects base 16 for a prefixed token, so every caller (integer literals, array lengths) inherits hex for free. A hex literal's value rides the exact same paths a decimal one does: over-i32 values still make the operation 64-bit (v0.23.18-dev's rule) and still truncate into an `i32` slot exactly as the decimal spelling always did.
- **Coverage: `tests/syntax-relief-checks.sh` [7/7], registered in `ci.sh` after `write-output-checks.sh`.** (1) a strict `-Werror` rebuild; (2) source guards pinning the new parser and lexer shapes *and the absence of the two old ones* (`need(TK::Else,"expected else branch")` and `stoull(tok.s,&used,10)` must never come back); (3) a probe that SKIPs cleanly without an AArch64 C compiler or a runner; (4) an **if/else shape battery — 13 shapes**, each an Aster source and a C twin, both run under qemu, exit statuses compared: no else at all (condition true and false), else-if chains with and without a tail else, the explicit `else { if … }` spelling alone and nested two deep, the `} else { }` filler the icon demo carries, if-without-else nested inside a then branch / a `while` / a `for`, the early-return guard, and the both-branches-return form sema accepts with no trailing return; (5) a **hex battery — 14 values**, each reduced through arithmetic the C oracle computes (`% 251`, a mask, a shift, a call argument), so a mis-lexed nibble or a wrong base fails a case: zero, small, uppercase `0X`, negative, sums, masks, `0x7FFFFFFF`, the `0x80000000` truncation edge, `0x0102030405060708` and `0x7FFFFFFFFFFFFFFF` on the 64-bit path, hex array lengths in both cases, hex as a call argument, hex as a shift count; (6) a **rejection matrix** — a bare `0x` (as a literal and as an array length), a hex value past i64, `0x1G` adjacency, and the fall-through that optional else must *not* excuse — each demanding the diagnostic that names the offending thing; (7) a **mutation layer** that puts each old restriction back (else re-mandated; the hex branch disabled) and requires the batteries to go red. No expected value is hand-computed anywhere: gcc is the oracle.
- **Byte-level: 276 of 276 of the A/B baseline reproduces** — asserted by `struct-checks.sh` [4/8], which passed with the round's compiler. This is the round's structural claim: neither edit is reachable from any pre-existing example (no example has an else-less `if` — the parser used to reject it — and no example has a hex literal — the lexer used to reject it), and the codegen paths are literally untouched.
- **The first full CI run of this release failed one suite, and it was the right suite.** `bool-checks.sh`'s rejection matrix carried `if without else not rejected` — a case asserting that `if (a == 1) { return 0; } return 1;` must fail with `expected else branch`. That is not a regression; it is a test pinning the very restriction this round lifts, written when the restriction was the rule. The case was inverted into an acceptance check in the same suite's `[7/8]` (the early-return guard now compiles and exits 0), with a comment recording that the *remaining* half of the contract — the fall-through that optional else must not excuse — lives in `syntax-relief-checks.sh` [6/7]. A suite that pins a lifted rule is the system working: the round had to argue with the past on the record, not quietly break it.

## v0.23.21-dev — an ELF program prints without a host: `write(fd, buf, len)` is a system call, like `main`'s return

- **Where this came from.** The user asked the obvious question after the hello-world walkthrough — *"the ELF is already a process, why must printing go through a `.so` and a `dlopen` host?"* — and the answer was that it must not. `build-elf` emits static, libc-free binaries *by design* (that is what keeps them tiny and what the whole byte baseline pins), and its libc story is the Android one: `build-so --needed libc.so`, loaded by a NativeActivity host. But the process model was never the obstacle: `main` **is** `_start` (`elfNameOf`, `src/aster.cpp`), and `main`'s `return` already emits `MOV X8, #93; SVC #0` — `exit`. Output was the one capability outsourced to libc for no reason deeper than "there was no intrinsic for it yet".
- **The feature.** `write(fd: i32, buf: ptr, len: i32) -> i32` — one `write(2)` system call, `x8 = 64`, arguments in `x0/x1/x2`, the byte count returned in `x0`. It is the fourth intrinsic (after `load_ptr`/`load_u8`/`store_u8`), the first one that is a *system* primitive rather than a memory one, and the first that lets a single-file program speak: `examples/write_hello.ast` builds to a static ELF that prints **Hello, World!** and exits 0. The type system needed no change at all — `callType` already answers `Ty::I32` for any name not in `functionArities`, and the two checks (Sema's `checkCall`, codegen's `Call` case) follow the `store_u8` pattern exactly.
- **The round's real lesson: `x0/x1/x2` are two things at once.** They are the syscall argument registers *and* the first three temporaries of the `w0–w15` pool. The first implementation moved each argument into its syscall register as it was generated and freed the temporary — and the next argument's `gen` reused that very register: the fd sat in `x0`, the buffer's `gen` took `w0` for its own, and the syscall ran with the buffer as the fd (`EBADF`, exit 247). The second implementation staged all three through the frame's scratch grid (`4160 + callDepth*256`, as `emitCallSeq` does) but then **loaded straight into `x0`**, clobbering the surrounding expression's own `w0`: in `total = total + write(1,"ab",2)` the `gen(total)` result lived in `w0`, the argument load overwrote it, and the following `add w0, w0, w1` summed the byte count with itself — 4 instead of 6, three times in a row, and the same shape inside a loop where the corrupted value is carried across iterations. The shipped implementation parks every live temporary in the frame (`storeLocal(r, 1020+r)`, `emitCallSeq`'s own save area), stages and moves the arguments, runs the syscall, and restores (`loadLocal`) before handing out the result. A syscall preserves every register but `x0`, so the argument loading is the only thing that can hurt — but that is exactly what an expression's live temporaries are exposed to.
- **Coverage: `tests/write-output-checks.sh` [4/4], registered in `ci.sh` after `narrow-value-coverage.sh`.** (1) a strict `-Werror` rebuild; (2) a probe that locates an AArch64 C compiler and SKIPs cleanly without one; (3) **16 differential cases**, each an Aster source and a C twin compiled by `aarch64-linux-gnu-gcc -O2 -fwrapv` (the C twins of the shadowing cases with `-fno-builtin`, so gcc does not fold their calls), both run under qemu, compared on **three channels** — stdout bytes, stderr bytes and the exit status — so a wrong fd, a wrong length, a wrong buffer or a wrong count each fail a case: `hello_stdout`, `hello_stderr` (fd 2), `zero_len` (write of 0 bytes returns 0), `bss_bytes` (the buffer is a run-time-filled global array, not a literal), `offset_write` (`p + 2` is a byte offset — ptr+int does not scale), `rodata_two` (two literals, two bases), `i64_count` (the count consumed at 64-bit width), the three clobber regressions — `accumulate`, `loop_multi`, `nested_expr` — which are also the [4/4] **coverage map**: if any of the three is ever deleted from the case list, the map itself fails — and five **shadowing** cases (`shadow_write`, `shadow_write_same_sig`, `shadow_load_u8`, `shadow_load_ptr`, `shadow_store_u8`) proving a user-defined function of an intrinsic's name is an ordinary call, not the intrinsic. [3/4b] pins the argument contract with four rejections (a string length, a string fd, an integer buffer, two arguments), each demanding the diagnostic that names the offending argument. No expected value is hand-computed anywhere: gcc is the oracle, on all three channels.
- **The defect the first full CI run caught: an intrinsic used to swallow a user's function of the same name.** With the suite written and the baseline rebased, `ci.sh` came back **76 passed, 2 failed** — and one failure was not the new feature at all: `runtime-bounds-checks.sh`, whose helper function is literally named `write`, died with `error: write expects (fd: i32, buf: ptr, len: i32)`. The intrinsic dispatch (`callType`, Sema's `checkCall`, codegen's `gen` `Call` case) matched on the *name alone*, so any program defining `fn write(...)` — or `load_u8`, `load_ptr`, `store_u8` — had its own function silently replaced by the intrinsic. Four releases of the intrinsic family had never met this because nothing had ever called one `write`; the new primitive created the collision, and the CI was what found it. The fix is the same rule at all three sites: `&&!arities.count(e.name)` — **the user's function wins, always** — and `load_ptr`'s pointer-return registration in `compileProgram` became conditional on the same question instead of unconditional. The five shadowing cases in the new suite keep it fixed; the other failure was `narrow-int-abi-checks.sh`'s source-guard counts (`loadStackOff(` 13→16, `storeStackOff(` 10→11 — the write path adds three load sites and one stage site), which is the guard doing its job.
- **Byte-level: 276/276 of the A/B baseline reproduces** — the 273 pre-existing rows byte-identical (the version constant is not embedded in generated objects, so bumping it moves no byte; the new code path is only reachable through the new `write` name), and 3 new rows record `examples/write_hello.ast` across `build-so` / `build-elf` / `build-obj --pic`. `struct-checks.sh` [4/8] asserts the baseline; it passed.
- **What this deliberately does *not* do.** It does not link libc into `build-elf`. That road runs through `__libc_start_main` — TLS, stdio buffers, the atexit chain — and would trade the static-self-contained contract (and every existing ELF's structure) for one function. It does not add an `exit_group` (94) either: `exit` (93) is what `main`'s return has always emitted, and changing it would re-record every ELF row in the baseline for zero benefit while the language has no threads. And it does not touch the `.so` path: `--needed libc.so` + a host remains the Android story, unchanged.

## v0.23.20-dev — the A/B baseline could not see the narrow-argument path, so the v0.23.19-dev defect survived five releases

- **Where this came from.** Round 3 fixed the `u8`-parameter defect (`stackOffFits` + `loadStackOff`/`storeStackOff`, per `tests/narrow-int-abi-checks.sh` [7/7]). Round 4 then asked a harder question: *why did that defect survive five releases in the first place?* The 82-probe diagnosis answered it — and the answer was not another bug. **No example in the tree ever passed a `u8`/`u16`/`i64` argument.** Every one of the 87 `examples/*.ast` passes `i32`/`ptr` arguments; the narrow types appear only as locals, globals, array elements and struct fields. So the 261-line A/B byte baseline (`tests/baseline/v0.23.5-dev/example-hashes.txt`, asserted by `tests/struct-checks.sh` [4/8]) was **blind to the argument-passing path**: a regression there could not have been caught, because nothing in the baseline exercises it. The defect was invisible by construction, not by oversight.
- **The two-part repair is to make the blind spot visible and permanent.** (1) Four new examples now carry narrow values through the exact path the baseline could not see: `examples/narrow_args.ast` (narrow arguments in the eight registers), `examples/narrow_return.ast` (narrow return values), `examples/narrow_stack_arg.ast` (narrow arguments on the 9th and 10th stack slots — the `4160 + callDepth*256` scratch area that is past the 4095 unscaled-byte limit and is where the v0.23.19-dev defect actually lived), and `examples/narrow_so.ast` (a shared object exporting `u8`/`u16`/`i64` parameter and return functions, for cross-language calls). Re-running the baseline generator over all examples now records **273 lines (was 261); the other 261 are byte-identical**. (2) A new resident suite `tests/narrow-value-coverage.sh` [4/4] freezes the round-4 probes so the gap can never reopen.
- **The discipline that matters: expected values are generated by the compiler, never by hand.** `tests/narrow-value-coverage.sh` has, for each of its 16 cases, an Aster source *and* an equivalent C source. The C source is compiled by `aarch64-linux-gnu-gcc -O2 -fwrapv` and run under qemu; its 8-bit exit code is the expected value. Aster's exit code must equal it. No number is written down by the author. This is a direct response to round 4 itself: three of its five "failures" were the author's own miscalculated expectations (`4000000000 % 7` taken as 6, `load_u8(p)` read as `a[0]`, and a couple of syntax slips) — the suite would have reported a defect that did not exist. A gcc-generated oracle cannot make that mistake, because it has no expectation to get wrong: if Aster and gcc agree, the case passes; if they disagree, the case is a real defect.
- **Coverage: `tests/narrow-value-coverage.sh` [4/4], registered in `ci.sh` after `narrow-int-abi-checks.sh`.** (1) a strict `-Werror` rebuild; (2) a probe that locates an AArch64 C compiler and SKIPs cleanly without one; (3) **16 differential cases** — narrow arguments in registers, a second narrow argument, `u16`/`i64` passthrough, mixed narrow arguments returning `i64`, a narrow argument through a nested call, an `i64`+`u8` combination, the 9th/10th stack-slot narrow arguments, a narrow return consumed in `i32`, a `u8` struct field, a `u8` `while` loop, `u8` recursion, and `u8`/`u16` global arrays — each asserted equal to its C twin; (4) a **real cross-language `.so`**: Aster's `narrow_so.ast` is built into a shared object and loaded from a C host through `dlopen`/`dlsym`, which calls `add_u8(100,50)`, `add_u16(40000,25535)`, `scale_i64(1e6,7)`, `ninth_u8(0..0,200)`, `ret_u8()`, `ret_u16()` and asserts all six. That [4/4] test is the *only* one that proves Aster's narrow-argument **stack layout** — how far the 9th argument sits from the 10th, the register-to-stack split, the high bits of a returned `u8` — matches the platform's; an Aster-calls-Aster link would pass on a layout nothing else uses.
- **The other faces round 4 probed are clean and stay covered by their own suites.** The 82-probe sweep also exercised `bool` storage/passing, string/rodata boundaries, run-time global-initializer order, struct-array fields and multi-file link signature consistency; it found **zero real defects**. Those faces were already owned by `bool-checks.sh`, `c-string-literals-*` + `e2e-c-string-literals.sh`, `e2e-global-deep.sh`, `struct-checks.sh`, and `linker-checks.sh`/`symtab-checks.sh` respectively. The gap was narrow *values* and only narrow values, so this release closes exactly that and leaves the rest alone.
- **Byte-level: 273/273 of the A/B baseline reproduces** — the 261 pre-existing rows are byte-identical to v0.23.19-dev (the version constant is not embedded in generated objects, so bumping it moves no byte), and 12 new rows record the four new examples across `build-so` / `build-elf` / `build-obj --pic`. `struct-checks.sh` [4/8] asserts the baseline; it passed. No compiler codegen change was needed this round — the fix in round 3 already produced correct narrow-argument output, and this round makes that correctness *observable*.
- **The parallel-lineage note in the v0.23.18-dev entry refers to a *different* `v0.23.20-dev`** — the uploaded builds cut from this tree's ancestor. This entry is this tree's own v0.23.20-dev, whose only source delta beyond round 3 is the version constant, four example files, and the `tests/narrow-value-coverage.sh` suite.
- **Segment B — the "other faces" deep-dig (closure audit, no compiler delta).** The user's second instruction was to keep digging into the compiler's *other* faces. Reconnaissance found those faces were already owned by dedicated suites: `bool-checks.sh` (bool is type-strict — `bool<->i32` assignment is *rejected*, and a `bool` local array is unsupported by design, so no silent bool-width path exists), `c-string-literals-*` + `e2e-c-string-literals.sh` (strlen/memcmp across the C boundary, embedded NUL, escapes), `global-deep-checks.sh` + `runtime_init_order_*` (single- and cross-file runtime init), `narrow-value-coverage.sh` [3/4] `u8_struct_field` (struct `{i32,u8}` laid out + read, gcc-differential), and `linker-checks.sh` + `c_struct_abi` (i32/ptr and struct across link/cross-language). The only residual edges the existing suites had *not* exercised were: byte-indexing a string pointer passed to an Aster-defined function (`load_u8(s+i)`), two distinct rodata literals addressed independently by offset, and a narrow `u8` argument carried across a pure-Aster `link-obj` (segment A had proven the narrow-argument ABI only against gcc via a `.so`). A new resident suite `tests/compiler-other-faces-audit.sh` [4/4] closes exactly those, every case gcc-differential (no hand-computed expectation), plus a [4/4] coverage-map assertion that the seven dedicated suites and the two failure-mode-mirroring edges (`u8_struct_field`, cross-file `runtime_init_order_*`) still exist. The audit passes; full `tests/ci.sh` is **77 passed / 0 skipped / 0 failed (of 77 checks)**, up from 76, the one new check being the audit suite. The five "other faces" are now closed by evidence, not assertion.

## v0.23.19-dev — a `u8` argument never arrived: an unscaled byte offset above 4095 turned a store into a load

- **Where this came from.** v0.23.18-dev closed the wide-integer holes, and every one of its 54 cases used `i32` or `i64` values. What none of them did was *pass a narrow value across a call*. The moment that was tried, `fn g(a: u8) -> u8 { return a; }` called as `g(200)` returned **0** — not a wrong-width answer, nothing at all. A `u8` parameter had not worked since `u8` was introduced in v0.23.2-dev, five releases earlier, and no suite had ever called a function with one.
- **Why it did not look like an ABI problem.** `u8` locals, globals, array elements and struct fields were all correct, and `u16`, `u32` and `i64` parameters were all correct. What differed was not the type but the **offset**: AArch64's unsigned immediate is width-scaled, so its *range* differs per width. `LDRB`/`STRB` take the byte offset **unscaled** in 12 bits (0..4095); `LDRH`/`STRH` scale by 2 (0..8190, even); `LDR`/`STR W` by 4 (0..16380, 4-aligned); `LDR`/`STR X` by 8 (0..32760, 8-aligned). `loadStackT`/`storeStackT` shifted the byte offset left by 10 for *every* width, so a `u8` offset past 4095 was neither rejected nor wrapped onto a neighbour: its **bit 12 landed on bit 22**, the one bit that separates `STRB` (`0x39000000`) from `LDRB` (`0x39400000`).
- **The mechanism.** Every call argument is staged in the scratch area at `4160 + callDepth*256`, past 4095 for *every* call, so `storeStackOff(v, 4160, U8)` emitted `LDRB w0, [sp, #64]`: the caller **stored the argument by loading it**, and the callee read whatever the uninitialized stack held. `u16` survived on arithmetic alone (`4160/2 == 2080` fits; even the deepest scratch, `4160 + 14*256 + 31*8 == 7992`, is 3996 after the shift). Struct fields cannot reach it — a struct is capped at 1024 bytes. Locals and globals sit at small offsets. So the truthful statement is "unscaled byte offsets above 4095 are broken", not "u8 parameters are broken", and the two differ in where the fix belongs.
- **The repair.** `stackOffFits(off,t)` decides **per width** whether an offset is expressible. `loadStackOff`/`storeStackOff` emit the encoding that always was when it fits, and otherwise stage the address through `x16` — or `x17`, when `x16` is itself the operand, because `emitAddOff` would otherwise overwrite the value about to be stored — and use the `[x16]`/`[x17]` forms. All 23 previous direct `emit(loadStackT(...))` / `emit(storeStackT(...))` sites now go through the two helpers. A fitting offset keeps the encoding it always had, so nothing pre-existing moves.
- **Coverage: `tests/narrow-int-abi-checks.sh` [7/7], registered in `ci.sh`.** (1) a strict `-Werror` rebuild; (2) **15 source guards** — the four range tests, both fallback helpers, both `x16`-is-the-target branches, the counted call sites (13 loads, 10 stores) and the absence of any bare `emit()` of the raw encodings; (3) **2 rejections** — an `i64` argument into a `u8` or `u16` parameter; (4) **21 qemu-run cases in four groups** — narrow arguments in registers (A), on the stack from the 9th onward (B), mixed widths / nesting / recursion (C), and forward guards at small offsets that must *stay* correct (D); (5) **8 differential comparisons against cross-compiled C** plus **a real cross-language link** — Aster's object linked against an `aarch64-linux-gnu-gcc` object, stripped to `.text` because Aster's linker accepts only the sections it emits itself — which is the only witness that the *stack layout* of a narrow argument (how far the 9th sits from the 10th) agrees with the platform's; (6) **6 mutations**, each caught by a named case; (7) seven documented blind spots. The suite runs in 41 s.
- **Three of the first six mutants survived, and that was the informative part.** Removing the load-side `x16`-is-the-target branch is an *equivalent* mutant: `emitAddOff` leaves the address in `x16` and the very next instruction consumes `x16` as its base, so `LDRB W16, [X16]` is correct without `x17`. (The store-side branch is a different matter and is kept: there, staging through `x16` would destroy the value.) Changing `emitAddOff`'s chunk split slides the whole scratch area, and because every access to a slot goes through the same helper, the store and the load move together and the value still round-trips — visible in the object bytes, invisible in an exit code. And `g(0) == 0` passes under the defect too, because uninitialized stack usually reads as zero, so it is a forward guard and never a discriminator. All three are now stated in [7], and the two replaceable mutants were replaced with ones observable at the same sites: a wrong offset inside `loadAtT`, and a load side that skips the range test.
- **One regression this round introduced, caught by the full CI.** Routing all 23 call sites through the two helpers rewrote two lines `struct-checks.sh` had pinned by literal text — the prologue that saves the hidden result pointer and the reload that brings it back for a nested `sret` call — so the first full run was **74 passed / 1 failed**. Both guards now pin the new spelling. That is the second round in a row where a bulk rewrite invalidated someone else's pin, so the check is now mechanical rather than remembered: all 181 literal `grep -F` guards and 37 count guards in `tests/*.sh` were extracted and re-evaluated against the new source, and those two were the only casualties. It is also why [2] of the new suite pins the *absence* of a direct `emit()` of the raw encodings: a helper that is correct while one call site bypasses it is exactly the shape of this defect.
- **Byte-level: 261/261 outputs byte-identical to v0.23.18-dev** across `build-elf`, `build-so` and `build-obj --pic`. No example in the tree passes a `u8` or `u16` argument — which is exactly why the defect lasted five releases, and also why the fix moves no byte. The only source delta beyond the fix is the version constant.

## v0.23.18-dev — wide-integer correctness: the operators were right, the operands were being cut in half

- **Where this came from.** v0.23.17-dev shipped `%`, the shifts and the bitwise operators, and its suite proved the encodings with 30 qemu checks and 11 differential comparisons against C. What none of them tested was an operand wider than 32 bits, because every one of them used `i32` values. The moment the new operators met the `i64` type v0.23.2-dev had added, three defects surfaced at once — and one of them (`let x: i64 = 4294967297; return x % 7;` returning **1** instead of **5**) was a silent wrong answer in a plain program. This release fixes all three, and the fix is a set of rules rather than three patches, because the three defects had the same root: *width was decided at the wrong place*.
- **Defect A — a wide literal was chopped back to 32 bits.** `exprType(Num)` reported every numeric literal as `i32`, so the five arithmetic sites unconditionally sign-extended the operand *from i32* before operating on it. A literal reaching an `i64` slot was already materialized at full width (`emitMov64`, `MOVZ`+`MOVK`), so the extension was pure damage: `return 4294967296 / 1000;` computed `0 / 1000` and returned **0** instead of 4294967.
- **Defect B — an operation's width was decided by its consumer.** `const bool wide = tyIs64(want)`: an `i64` operand met by an `i32` context was computed in 32 bits. `let x: i64 = 4294967297; return x % 7;` truncated `x` to 1, and `1 % 7` is 1 — a wrong answer that looks plausible, which is the worst kind. The width of an operation is a property of the *values* flowing through it, not of whoever wants the result.
- **Defect C — local array element types were never recorded.** `slotElemTypes` was declared and read but never written, so `elementTypeOf` fell back to `i32` for every local array. A local `i64[2]` stored its elements 8 bytes wide while *addressing* them at 4-byte stride: `a[1]` read the top half of `a[0]`, and with one slot covering the whole array, `c[1]` even spilled into the neighbouring local. Global arrays were typed correctly; only locals lied.
- **The repair is three rules.** **(1)** A constant operand whose value exceeds `i32` makes the operation 64-bit (`constExceedsI32` — magnitude tested after peeling `Neg`, so `-4294967297` counts, with thresholds `0x7fffffff` / `0x80000000`). **(2)** Widening is the operand's own job, not the consumer's: every post-hoc `SXTW` is deleted, and each leaf form widens itself from its own type — eight sites in all (`Num`, `Neg`, local and global variables, a field, three index forms, `Deref`, a call, and a conditional arm). The now-dead `extendTo64` helper is removed, and a source guard pins its absence. **(3)** An operation is 64-bit if either operand is `i64` or over-wide, whatever the consumer asks for: `tyIs64(want) || tyIs64(ta) || tyIs64(tb) || constExceedsI32(e.a) || constExceedsI32(e.b)`. `Cond` takes its arm type from the expression itself when the consumer is `i32`, so `c ? 4294967297 : small` keeps both arms wide instead of truncating one.
- **Coverage: `tests/wide-int-literal-checks.sh` [7/7], registered in `ci.sh`.** (1) a strict `-Werror` rebuild; (2) **22 source guards** — each rule's helper and threshold, the wide-operation call sites and their shift/`BitNot` variants, each of the eight leaf widening sites (counted), `Cond`'s arm type, `Neg`'s `want` pass-through, `slotElemTypes` being *written* at array declaration, the element-width store bases, and the dead helper's absence; (3) **2 rejections** — an `i64` argument into an `i32` parameter, and a literal that overflows `i64`; (4) **54 qemu-run cases in eight groups** — wide literals consumed as `i32` (A), `i64` locals across every operator (B), `i64` operands with `i32` consumers (C), negative constants and `INT64_MIN` (D), each widening leaf (E), conditional arms (F), local array element types (G), and six cases that must *stay* 32-bit (H), where a spurious widening is as much a bug as a missing one; (5) **14 differential comparisons against cross-compiled C** (`aarch64-linux-gnu-gcc -O2 -fwrapv`), which is what catches an expectation that is wrong in the same way the compiler is; (6) a **16-mutation layer** that rebuilds the compiler with each rule broken in turn — drop the constant test from `wideOp`, drop it from the comparison paths (two: the materialized and the conditional), remove each leaf's widening, stop writing `slotElemTypes`, break `Cond`'s arm type, break the array element width, break `Neg`'s pass-through, raise the positive threshold, remove the `Neg` peel — and asserts a *named* case goes red for each; (7) the three blind spots, written down.
- **Three blind spots are documented rather than hidden.** A consistently-truncated expectation passes: `8589934593 == 8589934593` returns equal when both sides lose their high bits, so every expectation in the suite is a large *positive* constant compared for value, or compares against a small one. `&` cannot see a sign extension — `x & 7` discards the high half whichever way it is extended, so the bitwise cases lean on the other operators. And a `u32` return must *not* be widened, which is why case 52 sits in group H rather than being a "fix".
- **One test-infrastructure lesson is in the file too.** The mutation layer runs 16 full rebuilds of the compiler; launched all at once they starved the machine (all `cc1plus` processes in D state, 20 minutes without output). The suite now drains them four at a time and completes in 1:30.
- **Byte-level note: 259 of 261 outputs byte-identical to v0.23.17-dev, and the two that differ are provably equivalent.** Both are `c_struct_abi` (`build-so` and `build-obj --pic`), where the array-layout fix reaches a struct's `i64` field array: the stride is now the element width, which is the correction itself. `tests/e2e-c-struct.sh` links that example against a C host in both directions with 15 assertions and passes, so the change is behaviour-preserving; the two affected rows of the 261-line baseline in `tests/baseline/v0.23.5-dev/example-hashes.txt` were re-recorded, and the other 259 reproduce exactly (`struct-checks.sh` [8/8]). The i32 code paths are untouched: no example that does not use a wide value changed a single byte.
- **Parallel-lineage note.** The uploaded builds named v0.23.18-dev, v0.23.19-dev and v0.23.20-dev are a *separate lineage*: they were cut from this tree's ancestor and carry their own audit fixes, which this tree does not contain and does not claim. The working comparison is `/workspace/Aster v0.23.17-dev 与上传版 v0.23.20-dev 差异分析与下一步完善计划.md`; where the two lineages touch the same code the conflict is resolved in favour of this tree's rules above, and the divergence is recorded there rather than silently merged.

## v0.23.17-dev — the operators the language was missing (`% << >> & | ^ ~`, `OP=`, `++`/`--`, `?:`)

- **Where this came from.** The gap analysis that paused the touch line ranked the missing operators first, and the reason is not aesthetics: `%` cannot be written at all without division, a bit mask is unreachable, and `glyph_bit` in `examples/icon_text_demo.ast` is division-and-modulo *because the language had no `&`*. A language whose arithmetic vocabulary is `+ - * /` forces every user program to re-derive the rest, and every re-derivation is a place to get it wrong. This release adds `%`, `<<`, `>>`, `&`, `|`, `^`, `~`, the ten compound assignments, `++` / `--`, and the conditional operator.
- **The encodings were measured, not invented.** For every new operator the equivalent C expression was compiled with `aarch64-linux-gnu-gcc -O2` and the encoding read off the disassembly: `%` is `SDIV` followed by `MSUB` (`a - (a/b)*b`; there is no remainder instruction), `<<` is `LSL` (register form), `>>` is `ASR` for a signed left operand and `LSR` for an unsigned one, `& | ^` are `AND` / `ORR` / `EOR`, and `~x` is `ORN x, xzr` — which is what `mvn` is. The signed/unsigned choice in `>>` is decided from the *left* operand's type, because whether the sign survives is a property of the value being shifted, not of the count.
- **Precedence is a real chain now, not a flat list.** `primary → postfix → product(*,/,%) → sum(+,-) → shift(<<,>>) → relational → equality → bitand(&) → bitxor(^) → bitor(|) → logical(&&,||) → expr(?:)`, matching C's own order. `&` keeps its second job as the address-of operator without ambiguity: `primary()` takes `&x` only where an operand starts, and the `bitand_()` level consumes `&` only between operands. The lexer decides multi-character forms by lookahead, so `<<=` is one token (never `<<` followed by `=`) and `&&` never becomes two `&`. The rule that made `a < b < c` an error is intact.
- **Compound assignment is desugared in the parser, not in codegen.** `x OP= e` becomes `x = x OP e`, so no code-generation path changes and every target form (`x`, `a[i]`, `s.f`, `*p`) inherits the new operators for free. The desugaring has exactly one observable consequence: the target's address expression is evaluated twice. Rather than ship a silent miscompilation, `containsCall()` rejects the form that would show it — `a[f()] += 1` is a compile error telling the user to write the assignment out in full. A plain `a[f()] = 1` is still accepted, because it evaluates the subscript once.
- **`++` / `--` are statements, never expressions.** Both spellings compile to the same store (`name = name ± 1`), so `i++` and `++i` are the same instruction — but neither is allowed in expression position. That is a deliberate restriction: an Aster expression is side-effect free, which is what lets `containsCall()` be a sufficient guard above, and what keeps the constant folder honest. `return d++;` is rejected.
- **The constant folder had a silent bug, and it is fixed.** Folding a global initializer masked the shift count with `& 63`, so `let g: i32 = 1 << -1;` quietly became `1 << 63` — a value nobody wrote. A shift count outside `[0,63]`, and a left shift whose result leaves `i64`, are now compile errors, the same rule `constantInteger()` already applied everywhere else, so a shift cannot mean two different things depending on where it is written.
- **Coverage: `tests/operator-checks.sh` [8/8], registered in `ci.sh`.** (1) strict build; (2) source guards pinning every new token, AST kind, grammar level and encoding; (3) **14 rejections** — a pointer or struct in a bitwise operator, `++` in expression position, a non-bool `?:` condition, `?:` arms of different types, a compound assignment with a call in the subscript, `%` by zero, the three bad shift counts, and the chained comparison that must stay rejected; (4) **30 qemu-run semantic checks**, including one aggregate program whose value is the sum of nine independent sub-results so a wrong operator cannot be masked by another's accident; (5) precedence, every compound-assignment target form, both `++`/`--` spellings and a `for` step, `?:` nesting and right-associativity; (6) constant folding; (7) **11 differential comparisons against cross-compiled C**, the layer that would catch a precedence mistake that happens to agree with a hand-written expectation; (8) a **mutation layer** that rebuilds the compiler three times — `MSUB`→`MADD`, `ASR`→`LSR`, `ORN`→`ORR` — and asserts the checks now *fail*, which is what separates "the parser accepts `%`" from "the code is right".
- **One pre-existing defect found and left alone, on purpose.** `fn g(a: u8) -> u8 { return a; }` called as `g(200)` returns **0**: a `u8` *parameter* is not delivered, while a `u8` local variable, array element and struct field all work. It reproduces identically under v0.23.16-dev, so it is not a regression from this round, and it is not an operator bug — fixing it means changing the narrow-argument ABI, which is its own theme. It is recorded in the gap analysis as the next narrow-type item rather than folded into a release whose subject is operators. One more is recorded but not fixed: `let g: i32 = -9223372036854775808 / -1;` traps inside the compiler, because the folder divides before it checks.
- **Byte-level note:** the 261-line byte-identity baseline in `tests/baseline/v0.23.5-dev/example-hashes.txt` reproduces exactly (asserted by `struct-checks.sh` [4/8]), and a direct v0.23.16-vs-v0.23.17 A/B over every example in three modes is **214/214 byte-identical**. The new operators are additive: a program that does not use them compiles to exactly the bytes it did before.

## v0.23.16-dev — the e2e suite runs on the development machine too (test infrastructure)

- **The question this release answers: "what was CI actually proving on x86?"** Less than it looked. `ci.sh` picked its C host compiler with "what can execute on this machine", so on a non-AArch64 host the host-arch probe reported SKIP for **23 of 72 checks** — and the skipped set was not a random sample. It was the `e2e-c-*` family: the suites that link Aster's output against a C compiler and check the calling convention in both directions, the libc-string and rodata suites, the ABI edge cases. Those are precisely the suites a codegen change can break, and precisely the ones that were not running where the codegen gets developed. A green CI on the main machine meant "46 of the 69 suites ran and 3 APK gates passed; 23 suites did not run", and the report did not say which kind of green it was.
- **The contract changed, not the compiler.** A test host is now always compiled **for the target architecture**: `ci.sh` selects `aarch64-linux-gnu-gcc` whenever it exists, on any host, and the binaries are executed through the router `tests/run-aarch64.sh` — natively on an AArch64 machine, under `qemu-aarch64 -L /usr/aarch64-linux-gnu` everywhere else. "Must run on this machine" becomes "must run on the target", which is what an e2e suite for an AArch64 toolchain always should have meant. The router already existed (it detects an `EM_AARCH64` ELF and picks the path); what was missing was every suite using it — 11 of the 23 called the host binary directly, and `ci.sh` itself refused to cross-compile. Both are fixed; the `RUNNER=${RUNNER:-tests/run-aarch64.sh}` convention the other 12 suites already used is now universal.
- **One real defect surfaced, and it was not in the compiler.** Four suites died with `dlopen: /lib/libc.so: invalid ELF header`. Aster-emitted shared objects carry the **Android** spelling `libc.so` in `DT_NEEDED` — written explicitly with `--needed`, or added automatically by `ndkLibraryFor`'s fallback for libc imports — and on a glibc host `libc.so` is a **linker script**, not an ELF image, so the guest loader rejects it. The first fix attempt rewrote the spelling to the host's real `libc.so.6`; it failed, because `ndkLibraryFor` adds its own `libc.so` entry regardless, and changing the spelling would also have meant testing a different `.so` than the one that ships to the device. The fix that works touches neither the compiler nor the emitted bytes: `tests/guest-libc-alias.sh` — the symlink farm `touch-checks.sh` already used to satisfy the same Android spelling for `libm.so` under qemu — is now sourced by five suites, symlinking the guest's real `libc.so.6` to `libc.so` in the output directory and putting it on `LD_LIBRARY_PATH`. On a device the paths do not exist and the farm is a no-op; on a glibc host the loader finds a real ELF under the name the guest asks for.
- **The number that matters is the delta, not the total.** v0.23.15-dev on this machine: **49 passed, 23 skipped, 0 failed**. v0.23.16-dev: **72 passed, 0 skipped, 0 failed** (the first run reported 71/0/1, the one failure being `version-checks.sh` demanding the documentation this entry is part of — a gate doing its job). Twenty-three suites that reported SKIP now execute the guest and assert on its real output (22 `e2e-*` suites plus `icon-text-demo-checks.sh`), including `e2e-c-abi`, the suite that checks Aster's AAPCS64 conformance against a C compiler in both directions.
- **What this buys the next release.** The compiler gap analysis (kept beside this file) ranks the missing operators first — `%`, shifts, bitwise ops, compound assignment, `++`/`--`, `?:` — and every one of them lands in the arithmetic codegen path: `SDIV`+`MSUB` encoding, `LSL`/`LSR`/`ASR`, `AND`/`ORR`/`EOR`/`ORN`, register allocation, the i64 ABI. Before this release, changing any of that on the main development machine meant editing the C-ABI suites blind; now each operator ships with the Aster side executed under qemu **and** the C side as a differential oracle, in the same run.
- **Byte-level note:** no code-generation path is touched — the only source delta is the version constant, and the version string is embedded nowhere in any ELF/`.so`/`.o` Aster emits (it reaches stdout and the APK JAR manifest only). Two independent witnesses agree: the 261-line byte-identity baseline in `tests/baseline/v0.23.5-dev/example-hashes.txt` reproduces exactly (asserted by `struct-checks.sh` [4/8]), and a direct old-vs-new compiler A/B over every example in three modes is **212/212 byte-identical** (40/40 `build-elf`, 86/86 `build-obj`, 86/86 `build-so`).

## v0.23.15-dev — the touch example actually touches (P2.3 hotfix)

- **What happened.** v0.23.14-dev shipped `examples/native_touch.ast` as "the first interactive native app". On a real device it installed, launched and drew the gradient — and then ignored the finger completely: no dot, no response, nothing in logcat. Every off-device check had been green: the qemu suite, the APK gates, the byte-identity A/B. Three independent defects were stacked in the example, and each one alone would have produced exactly the reported symptom. This entry is the post-mortem; the release is the fix.
- **Defect 1 — the callbacks were written to the wrong slots.** `onInputQueueCreated` was wired at offset **80** and `onInputQueueDestroyed` at **88**, on the theory that the input-queue slots continue the `+8` cadence after the device-verified window slots at 56/64/72. They do not. `android/native_activity.h` declares `onNativeWindowDestroyed` *between* `onNativeWindowRedrawNeeded` and `onInputQueueCreated`, so the real layout is window 56/64/72, **`onNativeWindowDestroyed` 80, `onInputQueueCreated` 88, `onInputQueueDestroyed` 96**. The framework therefore called the "destroyed" handler when the queue appeared — it detached the looper and cleared the queue — so the attach never survived a single event. This is the v0.22.43-dev `callbacks`-offset bug one struct further along, and it is the whole argument for why the window slots were called "device-verified": a struct layout can be read from the header, never inferred from a cadence.
- **Defect 2 — the drain condition tested the wrong return value.** The event loop ran `while (got == 1)`, but `AInputQueue_getEvent` returns **0** when it filled in an event and a **negative** value when the queue is dry; the 1/0 convention belongs to `AInputQueue_hasEvents`. Even with the callback firing, the loop body would never have executed. The condition is now `got >= 0`, and `AInputQueue_preDispatchEvent`'s non-zero result — "the IME may consume this; abandon it *without* `finishEvent`, it will be reported again later" — is handled as its own branch instead of being ignored.
- **Defect 3 — the coordinate capture read the wrong register file.** `AMotionEvent_getX`/`getY` return a C `float`, which AAPCS64 delivers in **s0**. An `extern fn … -> i32` declaration makes Aster read the call result with `MOV Wd, W0` — **w0**, a general-purpose register. s0 and w0 are different physical register files with no aliasing between them, so the v0.23.14-dev code captured whatever happened to sit in w0 and decoded *that* as IEEE-754. The old qemu test fed the decoder hand-written bit patterns and never executed the capture path, which is exactly why it was green: it tested the half that was not broken.
- **The fix is a bridge, not a compiler feature.** Aster emits no SIMD/FP instruction of its own and the PLT is eagerly bound, so the s0 a C float-returning call leaves behind survives until the next call. `lrintf` takes its argument in **s0** and returns a `long` in **x0** — so a zero-argument `extern fn lrintf() -> i32;` issued *immediately* after `getX`/`getY` hands Aster the integer (x0's low 32 bits, exact for every screen coordinate). `event_x`/`event_y` in the example are two statements each, and `tests/touch-checks.sh` guards the adjacency structurally (an awk check that each float call is followed on the next line by its `lrintf`). The one real compiler edit: `ndkLibraryFor` now maps `lrintf` and the `lrint`/`llrint` family to **libm.so** — without that `DT_NEEDED` the on-device loader aborts `dlopen` with "cannot locate symbol". `lrintf` is exported by Android's `libm.so` with no API-level restriction (checked against bionic's `libm.map.txt`), so `--min-sdk 24` is safe.
- **The qemu test now tests the capture, not the decoder.** `tests/touch-coord-test.ast` is built as a shared object (extern calls go through the PLT, which only that path emits), dlopened by the new `tests/touch-coord-host.c`, and run under qemu with the `tests/guest-libc-alias.sh` aliases extended to `libm.so`. `strtof` — also a C function that returns a float into s0 — stands in for `getX`, and the chain must convert `0.0`, `1042.0`, `768.0`, `3000.0`, `123.0`, `1042.5`, `1919.5` and `-12.0` exactly (round-half-to-even included). The mutation layer now breaks the *bridge* — `return lrintf();` → `return junk;`, which is literally the v0.23.14-dev bug — and requires the test to go red.
- **The example now talks.** `tlog` wraps `__android_log_print` with fixed strings (Aster cannot format numbers): the input-queue creation, the looper attach, every motion action, every repaint and the pre-dispatch skip each log their own line, so a future "it does nothing" report comes back with evidence instead of a guess.
- **One latent bug fixed on the way.** The touch overlay's format test was inverted relative to the gradient's: it drew nothing in the two 4-byte formats it *could* draw, and drew 4-byte pixels into a 2-byte-per-pixel RGB_565 buffer otherwise (running off the end of the row). It now follows the same bytes-per-pixel rule as the gradient.
- **Byte-identity is unchanged for everything that already worked.** With both compilers building the v0.23.14-dev sources: **212/212** outputs byte-identical (40/40 `build-elf`, 86/86 `build-obj`, 86/86 `build-so`). On the current sources the single delta is `native_touch.build-so`: **23256 bytes on both sides, 670 bytes differing** — the added `libm.so` `DT_NEEDED` entry, its `.dynstr` string and the `DT_STRSZ` update, all absorbed by the alignment padding between the dynamic sections, so the file does not grow and no `.text` byte moves. The struct baseline's two `native_touch` lines were re-recorded; the other 259 lines are byte-identical.
- **Still device-only, and now said plainly.** The bridge is proven under qemu with real libc/libm; what remains unprovable off-device is the visible dot under a finger — the same boundary `native_render.ast` ships under, and the one this release exists to cross.

## v0.23.14-dev — touch input: an Aster-built app answers a finger (P2.3) — superseded by v0.23.15-dev

> **Corrigendum (v0.23.15-dev).** This release shipped `native_touch.ast` with three stacked defects — the input-queue callbacks wired at 80/88 instead of 88/96, an event drain that tested `got == 1` instead of `got >= 0`, and a coordinate capture that read `w0` where the C `float` actually sits in `s0` — plus an inverted pixel-format test in the touch overlay. The release installed and drew the gradient but ignored every touch. The corrected facts: the offsets are 88/96 with `onNativeWindowDestroyed` at 80; the drain condition is `got >= 0`; the capture is the `lrintf` s0→x0 bridge, not an `s0`/`w0` alias (there is no alias — they are different register files); `motion_coord` no longer exists. See the v0.23.15-dev entry above for the full post-mortem. The claims below are kept as written because that is what was believed and shipped; every one of them that touches those three points was wrong.

- **The question this release answers: "can an Aster program respond to input?"** Until now, no. `native_render.ast` (v0.22.38-dev) draws a gradient into an `ANativeWindow` — later an icon and text — but it is *display-only*: the `ANativeActivityCallbacks` slots it wires are the window ones at offsets 56/64/72, and the input-queue slots right behind them were never filled, so a tap on the screen did nothing at all. `examples/native_touch.ast` is the first interactive native app Aster can build: it fills the two remaining slots — `onInputQueueCreated` at **80** and `onInputQueueDestroyed` at **88** — attaches the `AInputQueue` to Android's main looper with `AInputQueue_attachLooper(queue, ALooper_forThread(), …)`, and on every motion event repaints a white 40×40 dot at the finger.
- **The offsets were not guessed.** The window callbacks at 56/64/72 are device-verified (`native_render.ast` renders on a real phone), and the callbacks struct is a plain array of function pointers in declaration order — `onNativeWindowCreated`, `onNativeWindowResized`, `onNativeWindowRedrawNeeded`, `onInputQueueCreated`, `onInputQueueDestroyed`, … — so the input-queue slots follow at 80/88 by the same +8 cadence. The looper model is the NDK's own: `attachLooper` registers the queue with the thread's `ALooper`, and the callback drains events with `getEvent`/`preDispatchEvent`/`finishEvent` until the queue is empty, which is the one shape that cannot miss an event between polls.
- **Aster has no `float`, so the example does not read a float.** `AMotionEvent_getX`/`getY` return a C `float`, and Aster has no floating-point type at all. The trick is that the float comes back in `s0`, which **aliases the low 32 bits of the `w0`** an `extern fn … -> i32` call reads — so declaring the NDK function `-> i32` captures the raw IEEE-754 bit pattern, and `motion_coord(bits)` decodes it back to an integer with division alone: sign and exponent from the top bits, the mantissa scaled down by a divide loop. `1042.0` arrives as `0x447C0000` and decodes to `1042`; the all-zero pattern is special-cased rather than divided by 2¹²⁷. There is no shift operator either, which is why the scaling is a loop of `v = v / 2`.
- **No compiler change is involved — and that is the point.** The version string is the only edit to `src/aster.cpp` this release. The `extern fn … -> i32` return path already reads `w0` (`MOV Wd, W0`); the NDK symbol map already routes `AInputQueue_*`, `AMotionEvent_*` and `ALooper_*` to `libandroid.so` with no manual `--needed`; and the callback offsets were anchored from the device-verified window slots. Everything the feature needs was already in the toolchain — which is itself a statement about the ABI slice: a pointer-argument, i32-returning C call into a system library is the whole surface.
- **The decode is pure integer math, so it is provable off-device.** The risky part of the feature is not the plumbing but the coordinate conversion, and because it is integer-only it can be executed here: `tests/touch-coord-test.ast` runs six known IEEE-754 bit patterns (`0.0`, `1042.0`, `768.0`, `3000.0`, `123.0`, `1042.5`) through an independent copy of `motion_coord` under qemu and exits non-zero on any disagreement. `tests/touch-checks.sh` [4/4] layers on it: source guards for the three externs, the 80/88 wiring and the looper attach; an APK gate that requires the `.so` to carry `libandroid.so` in `DT_NEEDED` (the auto-map, asserted rather than assumed); and a **mutation** — shifting the decode by one (`150 - exp` → `151 - exp`) — that the qemu test must catch. The suite is registered in `tests/ci.sh`, and `native_touch` joins the APK verification gate.
- **The one boundary that stays open is the on-screen response itself.** The decode, the wiring, the signing and the linking are all verified here; the *visible* dot under a finger is only observable on a real arm64 device — exactly the boundary `native_render.ast` ships under.
- **Code generation for pre-existing code is untouched: 212/212** outputs are byte-identical to v0.23.13-dev (40/40 `build-elf`, 86/86 `build-obj`, 86/86 `build-so`). One new example ships with it — `examples/native_touch.ast` — which the baseline now enumerates in all three modes (261 recorded outputs), and which the previous release builds identically.

## v0.23.13-dev — `include`: a multi-file program stops retyping its own signatures (P3.4)

- **The question this release answers: "who checks that my `extern` matches the function I actually wrote?"** Nobody. Before this release, a two-file Aster program shared a signature by *retyping* it — `extern fn add2(n: i32) -> i32;` in `main.ast`, `fn add2(n: i32) -> i32 { … }` in `defs.ast` — and nothing in the toolchain ever compared the two. Get the arity wrong, or the return type, or the name, and the linker is silent, because it matches by name: the program links, runs, and returns garbage. The README said as much ("imports are resolved by name only"). `include "PATH";` at the top level now brings another file's declarations into this one, and what crosses is **the signature read out of the file that holds the body** — so it cannot drift, because it is not a second copy.
- **Declarations only, and that is the whole design.** A body and an initializer stay in the file that owns them: an included function becomes `external` with its body dropped, and an included global arrives as a name and a type with its initializer left behind. This is C's header model rather than C's "`#include` a `.c` file" trick, and the reason is the one above — a textual copy can drift, a declaration read from the definition cannot. It also means the two files still compile to two objects and still link, exactly as they do today; nothing about the object format, the linkers or the ABI changes.
- **The proof is byte-identity, not "the build succeeded".** `tests/include-checks.sh` builds the same program two ways — one file that writes `include "sub/defs.ast";` and one that hand-writes the two `extern` lines the include stands for — and requires the two objects to be **`cmp`-identical**. An `include` that did anything more or less than supply those declarations cannot pass. The suite also checks the symbol table directly: `add2` and `total` must be `UND` in `main.o`, which is what "the body stayed in the other file" looks like.
- **Paths resolve against the file that wrote them** (C's `#include` rule), not against the working directory, with `--include-dir DIR` overriding and an absolute path ignoring both. The test is adversarial about it: the build runs from a directory holding a **decoy** `sub/defs.ast` that is not valid Aster source, so a cwd-based resolution fails loudly instead of quietly compiling the wrong file. A path that resolves to nothing is an error that names the path it looked for, in the v0.23.9-dev diagnostic format.
- **A cycle is an error that names the chain; a repeat is a no-op.** Those two look identical if you only track "have I seen this file": a file reached through a cycle is *part-way through being parsed*, while one reached twice through a diamond is finished. So the parser keeps both a `done` set and a **stack**, shared by every sub-parser through an `IncludeState` that outlives them — and the cycle check runs **before** the `done` check, because checking `done` first makes every cycle look like a repeat (it did, during development: a self-include was silently accepted). The message prints the chain: `include cycle: "cyc-b.ast" -> "cyc-c.ast" -> "cyc-a.ast" -> "cyc-b.ast"`. A diamond — two files that both include a third — still compiles, which is the case the `done` set exists for.
- **A definition in this file supersedes the included declaration**, which is what makes "include the header, then define the functions" legal. Only an *included* declaration gives way: two definitions or two declarations this file wrote itself are still an error. An included file's `@embed` bytes are that file's business and do not come across, so including a file that embeds a resource does not silently copy the resource.
- **`-g` is unaffected, and the suite asserts it.** An included file contributes declarations and therefore no code, so it contributes no line rows either: linking the two `-g` objects yields one unit per file, each naming only its own lines, and `-g` still does not move a single byte of `.text`. That is also the check that would catch a future change letting a body — and with it another file's line numbers — across.
- **A version string with two homes is a version string that drifts.** The usage banner carried its own copy of the version and still read `v0.23.12-dev` while the binary was v0.23.13-dev; nothing caught it, because nothing looked. Both places now expand one macro, and a new `tests/version-checks.sh` asserts the banner, the README title, the newest `VERSION_HISTORY` entry and the presence of `VALIDATION-v<version>.md` all agree with `kVersion` — and that the version literal appears exactly **once** in the source.
- **Code generation for pre-existing code is untouched: 210/210** outputs are byte-identical to v0.23.12-dev (40/40 `build-elf`, 85/85 `build-obj`, 85/85 `build-so`). Two new examples ship with it — `examples/include_demo_defs.ast` (the definitions) and `examples/include_demo_main.ast` (the file that includes them) — which the old compiler cannot parse at all and which are therefore outside the comparison, and `include` is new syntax, so no existing source takes any of these paths.

## v0.23.12-dev — `.debug_line`: the debugger can find your source (P4.2)
