- **The question this release answers: "I can read the disassembly — so which `.ast` line produced this instruction?"** Until now the answer was "count backwards by hand". Every executable Aster emitted carried no line information at all, so `lldb`, `gdb`, `addr2line` and a crash reporter could name an address but never a line, and `b main.ast:12` was not expressible. `-g` / `--debug-line` now emits a real **DWARF v4 `.debug_line`** table: one row per statement, at the instruction that statement begins at, naming the source file that produced it.
- **Where the rows come from.** v0.23.10-dev gave every `Expr` and `Stmt` a `line`/`col`, so the raw material was already there. `Codegen` gains a `lineRows` vector and one line at the top of `execStmt`: if line info is on and the statement has a line, record `{current instruction index, statement line}`. Because every nested body goes through `execStmt`, a row exists for every statement at every depth. `Function` gains a declaration position (the `fn` token, or the `extern` in front of it) so the **prologue** is attributed to the line that declares the function rather than to its first statement — without that, stepping into a function lands you on the body's first line while the frame setup has already run. Rows live beside the instructions and never influence instruction selection.
- **Why the encoder, and not a byte-copy of someone else's table.** A line-number program is a small state machine (`set_address`, `advance_pc`, `advance_line`, `copy`, `end_sequence`) over ULEB/SLEB operands, and the header carries `line_base`/`line_range`/`opcode_base`, `include_directories` and `file_names`. It is ~120 lines to write and it is the one part of the object file where hand-rolling is *safer* than copying: Aster knows its own statement boundaries, and the header's file entry points at the `.ast` source by name and directory, so `path_of()` needs nothing from `.debug_info` to resolve. Rows with `line==0` or an address past `.text` are dropped, and repeated addresses are collapsed — a zero-length statement must not produce a row that claims the next one's line.
- **The one genuinely new problem: an object file does not know its own address.** An executable can write final vaddrs directly; a relocatable `.o` cannot, because only the linker knows where its `.text` will land. So each `set_address` operand is written as **0** and its offset recorded as a patch site, and `.rela.debug_line` carries one `R_AARCH64_ABS64` (257 — a relocation type Aster had never emitted before) per site against a local `STT_SECTION` symbol for `.text`. Both linkers then raise every site to `textVaddr + objectTextOffset`. This is also what makes `link-obj` of two `-g` objects come out as **one unit per object**, each covering its own address range — a unit's rows are relative to a text base that only exists after the merge.
- **Additive only, and proven so.** Every new section (`.debug_line`, `.rela.debug_line`) is laid out *after* everything that already existed, and their section indices are allocated last, so no existing offset or index moves. Without `-g` the compiler takes none of these paths and emits the bytes it always did. The A/B agrees: **40/40** `build-elf`, **84/84** `build-obj` and **84/84** `build-so` outputs are byte-identical to v0.23.11-dev. Inside a single build, `-g` cannot change the code either, and the suite asserts it: `readelf -x .text` of a `-g` build is `cmp`-identical to the same build without `-g`.
- **Two independent readers, because a standard format is only worth emitting if something else can read it.** `tests/debugline-decode.py` is written for this suite — it walks the ELF64 section table by hand, runs the state machine, and prints `addr line path` with no pyelftools and no objdump in the loop. Where `llvm-dwarfdump` is installed it decodes the same file and the two must agree **row for row**; the suite decoder was separately validated against `gcc -g -gdwarf-4` output as an external control. The end-sequence address is additionally required to equal the end of `.text` exactly.
- **The assertion that matters is the line set, not the presence of a section.** For a source whose statement lines are known by inspection (`1 2 3 6 7 8 9 10 12` — blank lines, closing braces and non-statement declarations excluded), the decoded set of lines must be **exactly** that: every statement line appears, and no line that holds no statement does. A table that is merely non-empty, or one that attributes a statement to a neighbouring line, fails. Addresses must ascend and every one must land inside `.text`.
- **Coverage: `tests/debugline-checks.sh` [9/9], registered in `ci.sh`.** (1) off by default — no `.debug_line` in an elf, a PIE, an object or a shared object; (2) `-g` adds it and `.text` does not move; (3) every row names our file and a line in range, the line set is exact, and addresses ascend inside `.text`; (4) `llvm-dwarfdump` agrees row for row and the sequence ends at `text_end`; (5) PIE rows carry small, image-relative addresses with the same line set; (6) the object carries both `.debug_line` and `.rela.debug_line` with `R_AARCH64_ABS64` against `.text`; (7) a two-object link yields one unit per object, the second starting at or after the first ends, with per-file line assertions; (8) `.so` keeps the table; (9) **the suite must still be able to fail** — it `sed`-injects `s.line+1` into a *copy* of the source, builds that, and requires the line-set assertion to notice (`table has [1 3 4 6 8 9 10 11 13], source has [1 2 3 6 7 8 9 10 12]`). Step 9 also fails if the injection no longer applies, so the guard cannot quietly rot.
- **Cost.** 34 s per run (two compiler builds, one of them the mutant). Plumbing verified on every path that emits an ELF: `build-elf`, `build-pie`, `build-obj`, `link-obj`, `build-so` (both the static and the dynamic shared-object writer) and `link-so`.
- **Byte-level note:** all new sections and all section indices for them are appended after the existing layout, and the flag is off by default; every recorded output of v0.23.11-dev is byte-identical.

## v0.23.11-dev — a syntax fuzzer, and proof that it can still fail (P4.4)

- **The question this release answers: "the compiler crashed on a legal program and nothing in the suite caught it — how do we make that impossible to repeat?"** v0.23.10-dev's move-then-deref bug (six sites reading a moved-from `unique_ptr`) segfaulted the compiler on a negative literal, a `!`, and a `*p = v`. The strict build was clean, and the focused diagnostics suite passed, because neither exercised a unary prefix; only the full `ci.sh` caught it, across nine suites. This release adds the guard for that whole class of bug: `tests/fuzz-checks.sh`, registered in `ci.sh`.
- **One property, asserted over 2,000 inputs:** every input must either **compile** (exit 0) or be **rejected cleanly** (exit 1). Anything else — a signal, an abort, a timeout — is a crash. The corpus is generated by `tests/fuzz-gen.py` from a fixed seed, so a run is reproducible, and it is four kinds deep rather than a stream of random bytes: **fixed seeds** spelling every unary/assignment path that has crashed; **mutations** of real examples, half of them a *single* edit (nearly valid, which is what gets past the parser into Sema and codegen); **token soup** for the lexer; and **valid frames** filled with random statements — the kind that actually reaches code generation. On this release: **0 crashes, 76 inputs compiled**, and a 300-input subset also run through `build-obj --pic` and `build-so` (different codegen: PLT/GOT entries, relocations, a different ELF header) with 0 crashes.
- **Two guards keep the suite from becoming decorative.** (a) It asserts a **nonzero share of the corpus compiles** — a corpus where everything is rejected at the first token would report "0 crashes" while testing nothing past the lexer; the light-mutation kind is what lifted that share from 33 to 76. (b) Step 6 **re-injects the v0.23.10-dev bug into a copy of `src/aster.cpp`** (never the real source), builds it, and requires the fuzzer to crash it — 44 inputs do. A fuzzer that cannot fail is not evidence, so the zero in step 4 only means something because step 6 is red.
- **One thing the fuzzer had to be taught about itself.** Step 6 crashes a compiler on purpose, and a crashing process leaves a core file behind: the first runs dropped **139 core dumps (~39 MB) into the project root**, once per run, including every CI run — the suite was polluting the very workspace it was guarding. `ulimit -c 0` at the top of the script fixes it (verified: a full run now leaves 0 core files, and the crash detection is unaffected because that reads the exit status, not the core). Worth recording precisely because it is the failure mode of a *successful* test: the green run was the thing making the mess.
- **Cost, and why it is acceptable in the gate.** 34 s per run, which takes `ci.sh` from ~8 to ~8.5 minutes. `FUZZ_COUNT` / `FUZZ_SUBSET` / `FUZZ_SEED` are environment-overridable, so a developer can run 300 inputs locally or dial the seed to a different region of the space.
- **Byte-level note:** no code-generation path was touched — the only source delta is the version constant. This release adds tests, not behavior.

## v0.23.10-dev — semantic errors point at the line too (P4.1 completion)

- **The question this release answers: "v0.23.9-dev draws a caret under parser errors — so why does `unknown variable: missing at 2:12` still print one bare line?"** Because only the parser had a source position to give. The AST nodes carried none, so the semantic pass — which is where a type error is actually detected — could only fall back to a single-line message. This release threads source positions through the AST itself, and every semantic diagnostic now renders the same way a parse error does: `file:L:C: error: <message>`, the source line, and a `^` under the exact column.
- **How the threading works, and what it deliberately did not do.** Every `Expr` and `Stmt` node gained two unsigned fields (`line`, `col`); the parser fills them at construction time from the token that *introduces* the node — for a binary expression that is its left operand, for a unary prefix it is the operand, for a call or index it is the callee/array token, for a statement it is the statement's leading token. A single helper `loc(line, col, msg)` appends ` at L:C` to a message — or returns it unchanged when `line==0`, which is how a genuinely location-less error stays compact. Every throw site in the semantic pass and in the global-declaration path was wrapped with it. **No diagnostic string was edited**: the suffix is appended, never substituted, so the message text a `grep -F` assertion matches (e.g. `type mismatch initializing 'b': expected bool`) is printed verbatim, exactly as in v0.23.9-dev.
- **The regression this release survived, and what caught it.** The first cut of the parser changes moved each child into its parent (`z->a = std::move(x)`) and *then* read the child's position (`z->line = x->line`) — reading a moved-from `unique_ptr`, i.e. a null dereference. Six sites (deref `*`, address-of `&`, negate `-`, not `!`, and the two assignment-target statements) crashed the compiler with SIGSEGV, and they crashed it on *valid programs* — a negative literal, a `!`, a `*p = v`. The strict rebuild was clean and the diagnostics suite passed, because neither exercises a unary prefix; **it was `tests/ci.sh` that caught it**, as nine suites segfaulted (bool, struct, sema, wide-int, array-bounds, local-array, runtime-bounds, linker, object, plus both APK gates). The fix was to read the position before the move (or from the new owner afterwards). This is the third release in a row where the one-command CI gate earned its keep: a change can compile warning-free, pass its own focused suite, and still be broken.
- **Coverage.** `tests/diagnostics-checks.sh` now has 6 checks: the original four (parse-error caret, message text preserved, non-first-line realignment, one-line fallback for location-less errors) plus a new **semantic caret** check — `let ok: bool = 5;` must print the `type mismatch initializing 'ok': expected bool` text, echo line 2, and align `^` to the column Aster itself reports — and a check that the fall-through diagnostic for `let x: i32 = 1;` (no return) stays exactly one line.
- **Byte-level note:** only the version constant and the JAR-manifest `Created-By` string changed; semantic errors abort compilation, so successful-compilation artifacts (`.text`, `.so`, APK payload) are unaffected and byte-identical to v0.23.9-dev.

## v0.23.9-dev — errors now point at the line (P4.1)

- **The question this release answers: "the compiler said `at 1:27` — but which `1:27`, and what does the code actually look like there?"** Previously every parser/lexer diagnostic printed only `L:C` and a message; you had to open the file yourself to see the offending text. Now those diagnostics also echo the source line and draw a `^` caret under the exact column, so the mistake is visible in the terminal. Semantic errors that did *not* already carry a location stay compact (a single line) — threading source positions through the AST is a deliberate follow-up, not part of this change.
- **Design, and why existing tests did not move:** the location was already encoded as a trailing ` at L:C` suffix on every message. The top-level handler now parses that suffix and renders `file:L:C: error: <message>` followed by the source line and caret. The message text itself is printed **verbatim** (suffix included), so every `grep -F` assertion in the suite (e.g. `error-checks.sh`, `bool-checks.sh`) keeps passing unchanged. This is a zero-intrusion change: no diagnostic string was edited, no throw site touched.
- **Coverage:** new `tests/diagnostics-checks.sh` verifies (a) the original message text is preserved, (b) the offending source line is echoed, (c) the `^` is aligned to the column Aster itself reports (parsed back out of the `file:L:C:` prefix, so the test is column-agnostic), (d) a non-first-line error still reports the right line and realigns, and (e) a non-located semantic error prints exactly one line.
- **What changed in `src/aster.cpp`:** added a source-line cache (`setSourceCache`) populated at the two AST compile entry points (`build-elf`/`build-pie`/`build-obj`/`build-so` and the `build-apk` `.ast` path), a `reportError` renderer, and rewired the single top-level `catch` to call it. Strict `-Werror` rebuild is clean.
- **Byte-level note:** only the version constant and the JAR-manifest `Created-By` string changed; generated code (`.text`, `.so`, APK payload) is byte-identical to v0.23.8-dev.

## v0.23.8-dev — one command proves the whole thing still works (P4.3)

- **The question this release answers: "did my change just break something, and how do I find out without running thirty shell scripts by hand?"** Aster's delivery discipline is already strict — a warning-free `-Werror` build, three layers of per-feature tests (negative / structural / qemu), byte-level A/B against the previous release, v1+v2 APK verification, and a written validation report. The gap was that *running* all of it was a manual ritual. `tests/ci.sh` is the one command that performs it end to end and exits non-zero the moment anything slips.
- **`tests/ci.sh` is the gate; it is not a new feature with its own tests.** It orchestrates what already exists: a strict compiler build, every test suite as a black box, and an APK validation gate. Nothing about code generation changes in this release — the only source edits are the version constant (see below) and the added script — so this version is, byte for byte, the same compiler v0.23.7-dev was, now with a button to prove it.
- **Step 1 — the build is the first thing that can fail, and it fails loud.** `ci.sh` compiles `src/aster.cpp` with `-std=c++17 -O2 -Wall -Wextra -pedantic -Werror`. A single warning is a hard error under `-Werror`, so "compiles cleanly" is enforced by the script, not hoped for. If the build fails, `ci.sh` prints the error and exits 1 before any suite runs.
- **Step 2 — every suite runs, and every suite gets a verdict.** `ci.sh` walks a fixed list of `*-checks.sh`, `*-structural.sh`, and `e2e-*.sh` suites. Each is invoked as a black box: exit 0 with a `SKIP` line in its output is counted as **SKIP** (a real, reported skip, not a hidden pass); exit 0 otherwise is **PASS**; non-zero is **FAIL**, and `ci.sh` keeps going so *all* suites are judged before it reports — one failing suite never hides behind another. The per-suite log is kept, and a FAIL prints the tail so the cause is visible without re-running.
- **The e2e suites are architecture-aware, and that is deliberate.** The end-to-end C-ABI suites compile a C host program that links Aster's AArch64 output and then run that host **natively**. So `HOSTCC` must emit binaries runnable on the machine running CI. `ci.sh` picks `HOSTCC` from the host architecture: on an AArch64 runner it uses the AArch64 toolchain so the e2e suites actually execute; on any other architecture (e.g. the x86_64 sandbox here) it defaults to `cc`, and the suites' own probe then **skips them cleanly**. The result is honest on both: full e2e coverage on a real AArch64 runner, no false red on a foreign one. (`HOSTCC=aarch64-linux-gnu-gcc` is honoured if you pass it; it only "fails" on a non-AArch64 box because those particular suites exec the AArch64 host directly — which is exactly the case the probe is meant to skip.)
- **Step 3 — an APK gate that does not need HOSTCC.** Independent of the suites, `ci.sh` builds two demo APKs (`examples/native_render.ast` and `examples/icon_text_demo.ast`) and verifies each two ways: `python3 tests/validate_apk.py … --label-literal` (manifest/cross-checks against `resources.arsc`, no `openssl` required) and `./aster verify-apk …` (the full v1+v2 chain: ZIP CRCs, JAR digests, PKCS#7, Signing Block, chunked content digest, RSA over signed-data). Neither step touches `HOSTCC`, so this gate runs on **every** runner, including the x86_64 sandbox — it is the part of "validate_apk" the plan calls for that needs no device or cross toolchain.
- **One small design note on the gate.** The gate is a shell function `gate_apk(name, src, pkg, lib, label, …extra)` so adding another demo later is a one-line call; it builds into the run's scratch dir with a per-run debug key (`--keystore-dir`), validates, verifies, and reports PASS/FAIL with the relevant log tail on failure.
- **Coverage / result on this runner.** `sh tests/ci.sh` here (x86_64) ends with **42 passed, 23 skipped, 0 failed (65 checks)** and `CI_EXIT=0`. The 23 skips are the e2e C-ABI suites (expected on a non-AArch64 box — they run on an AArch64 runner). The 42 passes cover the full AArch64-only suite set (compiled with `g++`, executed under `qemu-aarch64` via `tests/run-aarch64.sh`) **plus both APK gates**. `tests/smoke.sh` is untouched and remains the curated quick gate; `ci.sh` is the comprehensive one.
- **Regression.** Strict build (`-std=c++17 -O2 -Wall -Wextra -Wpedantic -Werror`): zero warnings. `tests/ci.sh`: 42 passed / 23 skipped / 0 failed. **Code generation is unchanged** from v0.23.7-dev: the only source delta is the version constant — `kVersion` (line 23) and the `kUsage` string (line 3810) — plus the new `tests/ci.sh`; no emitted ELF/SO/APK byte is affected, so the v0.23.7-dev artifacts remain byte-identical and the existing `apk-checks` / `signing-checks` / `resource-table-checks` suites still pass. The shipped `icon-text-demo.apk` is therefore byte-for-byte the same APK v0.23.7-dev produced.

## v0.23.7-dev — a real resource table, and `@embed` for everything else (P2.2 follow-up)

- **The question this release answers: "can we skip `resources.arsc` and put the resources in one `.so`?"** No — for the resources the *Android framework* reads. `android:icon` and `android:label` are **resource ids** (`0xpptteeee`), not paths and not strings, and the id → value mapping lives in exactly one place. A resource the table does not hold resolves to 0: the app installs, `logcat` stays quiet, nothing crashes, and the launcher shows a generic icon under a blank name. There is no signal to debug from. Packing bytes into the `.so` does not help, because nothing looks there. **The way out is to make the table real, not to delete it** — which is what this release does — and to open a *second* path for the resources the program reads itself.
- **`resources.arsc` stops being "the icon's table" and becomes a table.** `ARSC::Table` holds entries (`type`, `name`, `value`, `isFile`) in insertion order plus the distinct type names in type-id order, and `Table::resId(type, name)` computes `(0x7f << 24) | ((typeId + 1) << 16) | entryIndex`. `build()` emits one `ResTable_typeSpec` and one `ResTable_type` per type. So the manifest no longer *agrees* with the table about what `0x7f020000` means — it **asks** the table. The delivered APK now carries two types and two entries; a build with `--string greeting=Hello --string app_subtitle="Two words"` carries four, at `0x7f010000 drawable/ic_launcher`, `0x7f020000 string/app_name`, `0x7f020001 string/greeting`, `0x7f020002 string/app_subtitle`.
- **The two kinds of entry are the same bytes on the wire, and that is the point.** Both are a `Res_value` with `dataType TYPE_STRING (0x03)` and a `data` that indexes the global string pool. The difference is only what that string *is*: a `drawable`'s value is a path inside the APK that `AssetManager` opens; a `string`'s value **is** the value, and there is nothing to open. Arsc is not "a table of files" — it is a table of values, and a file path is just one kind of value it can hold.
- **`android:label` is no longer a literal in the manifest.** It is a `TYPE_REFERENCE` to `string/app_name`, and the `--label` text lives in the resource table only. The bytes prove it: the manifest shrank from 1,560 to **1,544** (the literal left the string pool) while `resources.arsc` grew from 580 to **740**. `AXMLPrinter` renders `android:label="@7F020000"` on both `<application>` and `<activity>`, and the string "Aster Native" does not appear in `AndroidManifest.xml` at all. One name, one place — which is the whole reason resource tables exist.
- **`--string NAME=VALUE` (repeatable)** adds inline string resources. Names are validated (letters, digits, `_`, `.`; not starting with a digit) and duplicates are rejected at the command line, so the table cannot be built with two entries fighting over one id.
- **`@embed("path")` / `@embed_len("path")` — the second path.** This closes a genuine gap: there was no syntax that puts arbitrary bytes into a program. `@embed` reads the file **at compile time** and yields the address of its bytes in `.rodata` (a `ptr`); `@embed_len` folds to the byte count **as a compile-time constant**, so it works as an array bound or a global initializer. Relative paths resolve against the **source file's own directory** — C `#include` rules — so a source file finds its neighbours no matter where the compiler is invoked from; `--embed-dir` overrides the base, and an absolute path ignores both. The same path embedded twice is embedded **once** (blobs are interned in a `std::map`, deliberately not a `vector`, because a reference has already been handed out and a vector would move it). Errors are specific: a missing file names both the path written and the path it resolved to, a directory and an empty file get their own messages (a zero-byte resource is almost always the wrong path), and >16 MiB is refused.
- **One consequence worth calling out: `let g: ptr = @embed(...)` needed a rule relaxed.** The run-time initializer rejected any pointer-yielding initializer outright, which made a global embed impossible even though the same expression worked inside a function. The rejection is now conditional — a pointer-yielding initializer is refused only when the target is **not** a `ptr`. A global embed is initialized by `__aster_init` before `main`, which the qemu run proves (checksum 159, matching python).
- **A `ptr` is not a byte pointer.** `p[i]` is `*(p + i*8)`, so walking embedded bytes is `load_u8(p + i)`. Getting this wrong costs an afternoon: the first global-embed checksum came out 95 instead of 71.
- **Coverage.** Two new suites, both registered in `smoke.sh`. `tests/embed-checks.sh` [6/6]: 9 source guards, 10 rejections, byte-level `.rodata` comparison against the source file, dedup evidence (three `@embed` of two files → 15 rodata bytes, one copy each), a build from `cwd=/`, `--embed-dir`, `build-so`/`build-obj --pic`, qemu runs (global checksum 159, a function-local embed agreeing, `@embed_len` as a constant = 114, a NUL/`0xFF` round-trip = 255, an asset inside a `.so`), and four compiler mutations (off-by-one length, no dedup, an appended NUL, ignoring `--embed-dir`). `tests/resource-table-checks.sh` [5/5]: 9 guards, a 2-type / 4-entry APK re-derived by **an independently written arsc walker** (every typeSpec/type chunk, every entry, id order, a drawable value that must be a path in the APK, a string value that must not be, no duplicate pool strings), 8 `--string` rejections, and three compiler mutations — label as a literal, label pointing at the icon, one type instead of two. `validate_apk.py` gains **[4d]**, which resolves `android:label` through the table.
- **External witnesses.** androguard's `ARSCParser` resolves all four ids of the test APK by name (`0x7f010000 → drawable/ic_launcher`, `0x7f020000 → string/app_name`, `0x7f020001 → string/greeting`, `0x7f020002 → string/app_subtitle`); `AXMLPrinter` renders the label as a reference. No aapt, no apksigner, no NDK.
- **Sizes.** Payload 31,785 → **31,929** bytes (+144: +160 in `resources.arsc`, −16 in the manifest); the APK on disk is still **34,554** bytes, because the growth landed in alignment padding that was already there. `MANIFEST.MF` still covers all 7 entries, so the v1 signature is unaffected.
- **Regression.** Strict build (`-std=c++17 -O2 -Wall -Wextra -Wpedantic -Werror`): zero warnings. `tests/smoke.sh`: **255 PASS / 0 FAIL** (215 for v0.23.6-dev, 251 before the demo suite). Code generation untouched: **249/249** recorded outputs byte-identical to the v0.23.5-dev baseline (`icon_text_demo.ast` adds three more, also byte-identical by construction). `example-nativeapp.apk` rebuilt and re-validated end to end (`validate_apk.py` ALL CHECKS PASSED, `aster verify-apk` OK v1 + v2).
- **A demo that puts both halves on screen at once (v0.23.7-dev, same release).** `examples/icon_text_demo.ast` is a NativeActivity that draws a **48×48 RGBA icon** and **three lines of bitmap text**, every byte of which comes from `@embed`: `demo_assets/icon48.bin` (9216 bytes), `demo_assets/font8x8.bin` (760 bytes = 95 glyphs of 8 rows × 8 bits), and three `.txt` files whose lengths arrive as `@embed_len` constants so the text can be centred. Two design decisions are worth recording. **Aster has no shift and no bit operators**, so "is bit `col` of this glyph row set?" is spelled with division and modulo — `p = 128 / 2^col`, then `(byte / p) mod 2` — and that four-line `glyph_bit` is the whole font renderer. **The drawing is a pure function**, `render_rgba(pixels, w, h, stride)`, with the NDK kept to a thin glue layer; that is what makes the frame checkable with no device in the loop. `tests/icon-text-host.c` `dlopen`s the `.so`, `calloc`s a buffer, **pre-fills it with magenta** so a pixel Aster forgets is counted instead of reading as a dark background, calls `render_rgba` under qemu, and writes a PPM; the pixel census is asserted (magenta 0, icon plate > 12000 px, white title > 1500 px, grey body > 1500 px), and `PREVIEW_OUT=…` turns the frame into a PNG. `tests/icon-text-demo-checks.sh` [5/5] wraps it: strict build, `build-so`, byte-level confirmation that all five assets are in the object **exactly once**, the render census, and `build-apk` → `validate_apk.py` → `verify-apk` with the assets re-found inside `lib/arm64-v8a/libicontext.so` and the app name proven absent from `AndroidManifest.xml`. The APK carries 4 resources at **876 bytes** of `resources.arsc` (`drawable/ic_launcher`, `string/app_name` = "Aster 图标文字", `string/app_subtitle`, `string/greeting`) — the name in the table, the picture in the `.so`, and nothing duplicated.
- **The app name is spelled out in the manifest by default; `--label-as-reference` asks for the aapt spelling.** Reported from use: a third-party APK viewer showed a **blank app name** for v0.23.7-dev’s APKs while the name displayed fine once installed, and the v0.23.6-dev ones were fine. The cause is the release’s own change — `android:label` was a `TYPE_REFERENCE` to `string/app_name`, and resolving it means opening `resources.arsc`. The framework does that on install; a viewer that parses the binary manifest and stops there does not, and shows nothing. Both spellings are valid AXML and both resolve on a device, so the default is now the one **every** consumer can read — the `TYPE_STRING` — and `--label-as-reference` writes the reference for anyone who wants the name to live in exactly one place. `res/` is untouched either way: **`resources.arsc` comes out byte-identical**, `string/app_name` is still there, and `validate_apk.py` [4d] now accepts either spelling and then fails if the manifest and the table disagree — so the two cannot drift apart. `--label-literal` / `--label-reference` pin the check to one spelling, which is how the suite proves the flag really changes the bytes. The mutation that used to be "label as a literal" is now **the literal drifting away from the table**, which is the failure mode the one-place argument actually warns about. Covered by `tests/resource-table-checks.sh` [6/6].
- **Building it also exposed a clang-only build failure, now fixed.** Termux's `c++` is clang++, and clang's `-Wunused-function` fires on an **inline** function that nothing calls, where gcc's does not: four of the seven `Ty` comparison operators were unused in this release and `-Werror` turned that into four errors, so the compiler would not build there at all. They are marked `[[maybe_unused]]` rather than deleted — the comment above them says both argument orders are spelled out deliberately, and which ones a release uses varies. Proof that the fix is inert: a **clang-built `aster` produces 252/252 outputs byte-identical** to the gcc-built one and to the v0.23.5-dev baseline.
- **`validate_apk.py` no longer fails when `openssl` is missing — and, more to the point, no longer needs it.** The v1 PKCS#7 signature was verified *only* by shelling out to `openssl smime -verify`, so on a machine without it (a bare Termux install) a perfectly good APK was reported as `FAIL: openssl smime -verify failed` plus `FAIL: unexpected cert subject: ''`. That was the validator confusing a missing witness for a broken artifact. The check now runs **in-process**: the signer's certificate rides inside `CERT.RSA`, so its RSA public key is lifted out of the DER and `pow(sig, e, n)` verifies the signature with nothing but the stdlib — over `signedAttrs`, whose `messageDigest` attribute is checked against `SHA-256(CERT.SF)`, which is what PKCS#7 actually signs. The certificate subject is read out of the same DER instead of being asked from `openssl` (this also fixed a latent bug: `AttributeTypeAndValue`'s value is a bare `UTF8String`, not a `SET` — the `SET` is the RDN one level up — so the first attempt read a subject shifted by two bytes). `openssl` stays, as a second opinion; when it is absent the run reports `SKIP` and still ends in ALL CHECKS PASSED. `signing-checks.sh` [9b/10] keeps the in-process path honest: the real signature verifies, a flipped byte in `encryptedDigest` is rejected, and a modified `CERT.SF` is rejected via the `messageDigest` attribute.
- **Two usability changes fell out of the same exercise.** `PREVIEW_OUT` no longer needs Pillow — the PNG is written with `zlib` and `struct` alone, so the preview works on a bare `pkg install python`. And `APK_OUT=…` copies the APK out of the scratch directory before it is cleaned up, which is how you get a file you can tap. `docs/TERMUX.md` is the phone-side walkthrough: the sources, every command, installing, and a troubleshooting table.

## v0.23.6-dev — launcher icon: a self-encoded PNG and a generated resource table (P2.2)

- **Backlog item P2.2 read "launcher icon in the manifest + a minimal embedded
  PNG", which undersells it by one file.** `android:icon` is not a path, it is a
  **resource id** (`0xpptteeee`: package, type, entry). The mapping from that id
  to a file exists in exactly one place — `resources.arsc` — and `AssetManager`
  reads that table, not the ZIP directory. Ship the PNG without the table and
  the id resolves to 0: the app installs, `logcat` stays quiet, nothing crashes,
  and the launcher shows the generic Android icon. It is the most expensive
  class of bug there is, because there is no signal to debug from. So this
  release is really **two generators Aster did not previously have** (a PNG
  encoder and a binary resource table) plus one attribute whose data type has to
  be right.
- **`res/drawable-nodpi/ic_launcher.png` is Aster's own PNG.** 192×192,
  **bit depth 2 / colour type 3** — a three-entry palette (indigo plate `283593`,
  white glyph, transparent) with a `tRNS` chunk carrying the alpha. IHDR, PLTE,
  tRNS, IDAT and IEND, each with the standard CRC-32, and an IDAT holding
  `78 01` + one **stored** (uncompressed) deflate block + the adler32 trailer.
  Every scanline uses filter 0 (None), which is the one filter with no neighbour
  arithmetic to get subtly wrong. **9,512 bytes.** The first version wrote
  32-bit RGBA — the same picture cost **147,726 bytes** and pushed the APK from
  26 KB to 170 KB. Two bits per pixel is enough for a three-state image and it
  keeps a real alpha channel, which is what a launcher icon actually needs.
- **`resources.arsc` is a from-scratch `ResTable`.** One `ResTable_header`
  (type `0x0002`, `packageCount` 1), a global UTF-8 string pool, one
  `ResTable_package` (`0x0200`, id `0x7f`, 288-byte header), a type pool
  (`"drawable"`), a key pool (`"ic_launcher"`), a `ResTable_typeSpec` (`0x0202`)
  and a `ResTable_type` (`0x0201`) whose `Res_value` is `TYPE_STRING` (`0x03`)
  pointing at `res/drawable-nodpi/ic_launcher.png`. Three details decide whether
  a reader accepts it: `ResTable_type.headerSize` is `20 + config.size` and
  aapt writes **`config.size = 56`** for the default configuration; `entriesStart`
  must point *past* the per-entry offset array (`headerSize + 4 * entryCount`),
  not at the chunk header; and the string-pool length is a two-byte varint read
  as `((first & 0x7F) << 8) | second`, so the high bits go in the *first* byte.
- **`android:icon` is written as `TYPE_REFERENCE` (0x01), and this matters more
  than it looks.** `TYPE_INT_HEX` (0x11) stores the identical 32-bit value, so a
  hex dump of the manifest cannot tell them apart — but only `0x01` makes the
  framework resolve the id. Wrong choice, and you get the silent-default-icon
  failure again, this time with a manifest that looks right byte for byte. The
  validator asserts the data type explicitly, and one of the five mutations
  flips it to `0x11` to prove the assertion has teeth.
- **The icon is part of the package, not an option.** There is no `--icon` flag:
  a launcher entry with no icon is the bug this release exists to remove, and
  `res/drawable-nodpi` keeps the bitmap exact rather than letting Android
  pre-scale it for a density. Adding two entries grew the deliverable APK from
  25,838 to **31,785 bytes** (7 entries), and `MANIFEST.MF` from 2 to 4
  entries so the v1 signature still covers everything.
- **One real bug found by rendering the picture instead of measuring it.** The
  glyph grid mapped a pixel to a cell with `(x - pad) / cell`, and C++ truncates
  towards zero — so every `x` in `[pad - cell + 1, pad]` produced cell **0**,
  stretching the leftmost column 17px further left than the rightmost column
  reaches right. The letter sat off-centre in an otherwise perfectly square
  icon, and every structural check passed. Fixed with an explicit `x >= pad`
  guard, and locked down by asserting the white pixels' bounding box is
  symmetric (`minX + maxX + 1 == w`): the old code gives `x[7,167]` in a
  192-wide image, the new one `x[24,167]`.
- **Coverage.** New `tests/icon-checks.sh` [6/6], registered in `smoke.sh`:
  source guards; a build validated end to end; a second, independently written
  byte-level reader of both new files (its own `resources.arsc` walk, its own
  zlib PNG decode, the rendered 16×16 glyph compared against a literal, and the
  centring invariant above); **five compiler mutations**, each a different way to
  ship an invisible icon — no `resources.arsc` at all, a `Res_value` that is not
  `TYPE_STRING`, the attribute as `TYPE_INT_HEX`, broken chunk CRC-32, and a
  table pointing at a file that was never added; and two external witnesses.
  Mutating the *compiler* rather than a finished APK is the only mutation that
  says anything here, since every APK entry is covered by both signatures.
  `validate_apk.py` gains sections **[4a]** (the reference attribute, including
  the data type), **[4b]** (a self-contained `resources.arsc` parser that
  resolves the id to a path) and **[4c]** (a self-contained PNG decoder: chunk
  CRCs, IHDR, palette/`tRNS`, and the inflated scanlines).
- **Independent witnesses, none of them involved in producing anything.**
  androguard 4.1.4's `ARSCParser` resolves `0x7f010000` to
  `drawable/ic_launcher` in the right package; Pillow decodes the PNG as
  `mode P`, `192×192`, three distinct colours, corner alpha 0. `openssl`,
  `aarch64-linux-gnu-gcc` and `qemu-aarch64` keep their usual roles. No aapt,
  no d8, no apksigner, no NDK.
- **Byte identity.** Code generation is untouched: **249/249** outputs of the 83
  examples (build-so, build-elf, build-obj --pic) are byte-identical to
  v0.23.5-dev, checked compiler binary against compiler binary. Only the APK
  changes, and only as described: the packaged `.so` is the same 17,824 bytes,
  the manifest grows by 36 (the one attribute), and the two new entries appear.

## v0.23.5-dev — structs: nominal composite types with a real AAPCS64 ABI (P3.3)

- **Aster can now name a composite.** `struct Point { x: i32, y: i32 }` declares a
  type; `let p: Point = Point { 3, 4 };` makes one, field by field; `p.x` reads a
  field and `p.x = 5;` writes one; `let q: Point = p;` copies the whole value.
  Four questions were settled up front and the answers drive the whole design:
  structs travel by value under the **full AAPCS64** rules (including the hidden
  X8 result pointer), **nesting is supported** (a field may be another struct,
  declared earlier), pointers to structs are a distinct type **`ptr<S>`** reached
  with **`&p`**, and **aggregate literals** (`Point { x: 1, y: 2 }`) exist.
- **Types are nominal.** Two structs with identical layouts are different types:
  `fn g(q: Q)` rejects a `P`, and `let q: Q = mk()` where `mk` returns `P` is an
  error. A struct is a value but deliberately not an integer — `p + 1`, `p == q`
  and `if (p)` are all rejected, each with its own message, rather than being
  given a meaning nobody asked for.
- **Layout is AAPCS64, computed once and shared.** `layoutStruct()` walks the
  fields, aligning each to its own alignment and padding the struct to its
  widest; a nested struct contributes its own size and alignment, computed
  recursively. The result is checked **byte for byte against a C compiler**:
  `tests/struct-checks.sh` [5/8] gives Aster and C the same struct and the same
  initializer and compares the images, including the padding bytes.
- **Three size classes cross a call, and both sides agree because they share one
  function.** `layoutArgs()` produces the assignment of arguments to registers
  and stack slots, and the caller and the callee both call it — the two halves
  cannot drift apart the way two hand-written loops would:
  - **≤ 8 bytes** — one general-purpose register.
  - **9..16 bytes** — two consecutive registers; returned in `x0`/`x1`.
  - **> 16 bytes** — the caller makes a copy in the outgoing area and passes its
    **address** (an 8-byte slot, even when the argument itself lands on the
    stack); a >16-byte result comes back through the buffer whose address the
    caller puts in **X8**, which the callee parks in its frame immediately
    because X8 is caller-saved scratch and a nested sret call would clobber it.
- **Two things the ABI does *not* do, both settled by asking GCC rather than
  remembering.** Composites are **not** padded to an even-numbered register —
  not even when the struct itself is 16-byte aligned. `aarch64-linux-gnu-gcc`
  13.3 at `-O0` puts `s1` in `x1/x2` and `s2` in `x3/x4` for
  `f4(int a, struct T s1, struct T s2)` (`struct T` = 12 bytes, align 4), and
  puts an `__attribute__((aligned(16)))` 16-byte `A16` in `x1/x2` for
  `h(char c, struct A16 s)` — straight from the next free register in both
  cases, with no rounding. Aster does the same and `tests/struct-checks.sh` runs
  the `f4` signature (`pairalloc.ast`); the `A16` half is GCC-only evidence,
  since Aster has no way to declare a 16-byte-aligned struct, and it is the case
  where a reader of the AAPCS64 text would most expect rounding. And the stack
  begin at the stack pointer **as the callee sees it**, so the caller's copies
  and the sret buffer — which have no fixed home, since their addresses travel
  in registers — sit *above* the stack area, not below it.
- **A struct is a memory value, so it moves through the frame.** A local struct
  takes `ceil(size/8)` eight-byte slots; a parameter arrives either in registers
  or as a copy the prologue makes from the address it was handed; a >16-byte call
  result with no address of its own (`use(mk(5))`) is staged in the scratch grid
  and copied out with the other composites. `emitStructCopy` does the byte
  movement, `emitStructStore` materializes a value into memory (literal field by
  field, anything else by copy), and `emitStructAddr` is the single place that
  answers "where does this struct live".
- **Global structs** are laid out like any other object: a constant initializer
  lands in `.data`, an all-zero one in `.bss`, and a nested aggregate is folded
  field by field into the bytes its own layout owns. A `ptr` field cannot be
  given a constant value and is rejected rather than silently zero-filled.
- **Four bugs found and fixed while testing, not by reading.** (1) `addImm` only
  reaches 4095, and the argument scratch area and the return buffer both sit
  above it — `emitAddOff` now takes the offset in chunks. (2) An aggregate
  literal stored its **destination register's old value** into the save slot
  instead of the destination, so a nested literal wrote through a garbage
  pointer (SIGSEGV). (3) Stack arguments were placed below the composite copies
  instead of at SP+0. (4) `use(mk(5))` — a >16-byte call result passed by value —
  had no address to hand over, so its bytes are now staged in the scratch grid.
- **`tests/run-aarch64.sh` had a latent detection bug.** Its ELF sniff assumed
  `EI_OSABI == 0`, which is true of Aster's own output and false of anything
  glibc linked (3), so an AArch64 host program was exec'd natively and failed
  with "Exec format error" on any other host. Only the class, endianness,
  version and `e_machine` are read now.
- **Coverage.** New `tests/struct-checks.sh` [8/8], registered in `smoke.sh`:
  source guards, a 20-case rejection matrix, the proof that every pre-existing
  example still compiles byte for byte, the C layout comparison, qemu runs of all
  three size classes in both directions plus nesting / `ptr<S>` / `&` /
  whole-struct copy / globals / a 9th-argument composite / call-result arguments,
  and a bit-level check that a >16-byte return really goes through X8 while a
  12-byte one really comes back in `x0`/`x1`. New `tests/e2e-c-struct.sh` links
  `examples/c_struct_abi.ast` against a C host **statically** and runs 15
  assertions in both directions; it skips (exit 0) without an AArch64-capable
  `$HOSTCC`.
- **Byte identity.** The 246 outputs the 82 pre-existing examples produced before
  this release are **unchanged**, one sha256 at a time; the golden file grows to
  249 lines only because the new `examples/c_struct_abi.ast` was added.

## v0.23.4-dev — signing keys as input: `aster keygen`, debug/release modes, `aster verify-apk`, self-verification (P2.5)

- **The signing key is no longer a constant.** Until now every APK Aster produced was signed with one RSA key compiled into `src/aster_apk_key.h`, so two developers' APKs carried the same identity and nobody could ship under their own. Signing now takes a `SigningKey` chosen at build time, and Aster ships with **no key of its own**: `src/aster_apk_key.h` is deleted.
- **`--sign-mode debug` (default)** uses the project's own key: `aster keygen` generates an RSA 2048/3072/4096 key with a self-signed X.509 v3 certificate into `<project>/.aster/debug.key.pem` + `debug.crt.pem` (plus a `.gitignore` holding `*`), and a build generates one on first use. **One key per project**, not per machine and not per build — two projects get two identities, and rebuilding a project does not rotate its key (which would break upgrades).
- **`--sign-mode release --keystore <key.pem> --cert <cert.pem>`** uses your key and **fails closed**: without both options the build stops with "a release key must be yours; Aster will not invent one for you" instead of quietly falling back to a debug key. A release build that silently used a generated key would be indistinguishable from a correctly signed one.
- **Key material is read, not assumed.** PEM or raw DER, PKCS#8 or traditional PKCS#1 private keys, X.509 certificates — so anything `openssl` or `keytool` wrote is used directly. An encrypted PEM is rejected with a message telling you to decrypt it first. `--keystore-dir` picks where debug keys live (default: `$ASTER_KEY_DIR`, else the nearest ancestor of the working directory holding `.git/` or `.aster/`, else the working directory).
- **Every loaded key is self-checked.** `keySelfCheck()` proves the certificate carries the private key's public half and then signs and verifies a probe, so a mismatched key/certificate pair fails immediately ("the certificate's public key does not match the private key") instead of producing an APK that installs nowhere. This needed `minimalBE()`: the same modulus is a 257-byte DER INTEGER (a `00` prefix because its top bit is set) and a 256-byte PKCS#1 value, and comparing them raw reports a false mismatch.
- **Aster can now verify, not only sign.** `rsaVerify()` is the mirror of `rsaSign()` — left-pads to the modulus width, rejects a signature of the wrong length or `>= N`, and compares the whole recovered block.
- **`aster verify-apk <app.apk>`** re-derives the entire v1 + v2 chain: ZIP structure and every entry CRC, MANIFEST.MF and CERT.SF digests, the PKCS#7 signature over `signedAttrs`, the Signing Block's framing and ID-value pairs, the chunked content digest over entries + Central Directory + EOCD, and the RSA signature over signed-data.
- **`build-apk` verifies the APK in memory before writing it.** The same `Verify::verify()` used by `verify-apk` runs on the bytes about to be written; on failure nothing reaches the disk and the failure log is printed. `--no-verify` bypasses only the check — never the signing.
- **Two encoding bugs found by making Aster read what it writes.** `b64()` dropped the final byte whenever `len % 3 == 1`, which truncated the generated private-key PEM by one byte (1215 instead of 1216) — invisible to `openssl rsa` but fatal to `openssl asn1parse` and to Aster's own loader. The APK Signing Block's ID-value pairs use a **64-bit** length prefix while the pairs nested inside a v2 block use a **32-bit** one; the reader used 32 bits for both, and each pair's value is itself length-prefixed and needed one layer unwrapped.
- **Coverage.** New `tests/signing-checks.sh` [10/10], registered in `smoke.sh`: `openssl` reads back what `keygen` wrote, `keygen` refuses to overwrite without `--force`, debug keys are per-project and distinct, release fails closed three ways, a release APK carries the developer's own certificate (checked with `openssl pkcs7`), mismatched pairs are rejected, PKCS#1 + raw DER load, `verify-apk` accepts good and rejects tampered APKs, and bad options are refused. `tests/apk-checks.sh` gains guards that no key material is compiled in and that the compiler mutation is caught by Aster's own verifier before the file is written. Full smoke: **152 PASS / 0 FAIL**. Codegen untouched: **204/204** outputs byte-identical to v0.23.3-dev.
- Structs (P3.3) move to **v0.23.5-dev**.

## v0.23.3-dev — APK v2 (APK Signing Block) signatures, v1 retained (P2.1)

- **`build-apk` now dual-signs.** An `APK Sig Block 42` is inserted between the
  last ZIP local entry and the ZIP Central Directory, carrying one APK Signature
  Scheme v2 signer. v1 (JAR/PKCS#7) signing is unchanged and kept, so the same
  APK installs and verifies on every Android version: pre-7.0 reads only v1,
  and 7.0+ prefers v2 and ignores v1.
- **Signing Block framing (all little-endian).** `uint64 size` (excluding
  itself), a sequence of `uint64`-length-prefixed ID-value pairs (`uint32` ID
  plus value), the same `uint64 size` again, then the 16-byte magic. v2's ID is
  `0x7109871a`. The size field is `pairs + 24`, so a reader can walk backwards
  from the Central Directory — which is exactly how Android finds it.
- **V2 block contents.** One signer, matching apksig byte for byte: `lp(lp(signer))`
  wrapping `lp(signedData) lp(signatures) lp(publicKey)`, where `signedData` is
  `lp(digests) lp(certificates) lp(attributes) lp(<empty>)` and each
  digest/signature is a `uint32` algorithm ID plus length-prefixed bytes.
- **Algorithm `0x0103`** = RSASSA-PKCS1-v1_5 with SHA2-256 over chunked SHA-256
  content. Chosen over `0x0101`/`0x0102` (PSS) because PKCS#1 v1.5 is
  deterministic — no salt, no nonce — so the same inputs produce the same APK
  twice, which `tests/apk-checks.sh` [6/7] asserts with `cmp`. It also reuses
  the existing `rsaSign()` path directly.
- **Content digest.** The ZIP entry region, the Central Directory and the EOCD
  are split into 1 MiB chunks; each chunk is hashed as
  `0xa5 || uint32 chunkLen || chunk`, and the result as
  `0x5a || uint32 chunkCount || chunkDigests...`.
- **The one subtle rule.** For digesting only, the EOCD's "offset of start of
  central directory" is rewritten to the offset at which the Signing Block
  starts; the record actually written into the file keeps the real Central
  Directory offset. Both records are the same size, so there is no ordering
  problem — but getting this wrong makes every verifier reject the APK while
  the file still looks like a perfectly good ZIP.
- **Anti-stripping.** `CERT.SF` now carries `X-Android-APK-Signed: 2`. Without
  it, deleting the v2 block leaves a valid v1-only APK and the downgrade is
  invisible; with it, Android and apksig both refuse the stripped APK
  ("no such signature was found. Signature stripped?").
- **Public key lifted from the certificate.** A minimal DER walker finds
  `SubjectPublicKeyInfo` inside the embedded X.509 certificate instead of
  storing a second copy, so the v2 public key cannot drift from the cert the
  signature is made with.
- **v3 deliberately not implemented.** v3 adds key rotation (plus per-signer
  `minSdk`/`maxSdk` attributes) over v2. Aster signs with a fixed embedded dev
  key, so there is nothing to rotate, and v3 is not a compliance gate — v1 + v2
  is. `tests/apk-checks.sh` [2/7] fails if any v3 code appears.
- **Verification.** Cross-checked against Google's own `apksig` 8.7.3
  `ApkVerifier`: at `minSdk 23` both v1 and v2 verify with 0 errors and
  0 warnings; at `minSdk 24`/`28`/`33` v2 verifies and v1 is (correctly)
  skipped. Five tampered APKs — a flipped entry byte, a flipped byte in the
  alignment padding that **no v1 digest covers**, the v2 block stripped, the
  signature corrupted, and a Central Directory field flipped — are all rejected
  with the expected reason. `tests/validate_apk.py` [6] re-derives all of it
  from scratch with the standard library only (structure, RSA verify via `pow`,
  chunked digest recomputation), and `tests/apk-checks.sh` adds a compiler
  mutation proving the anti-stripping check has teeth.
- **Code generation untouched.** All 204 outputs (`build-so`, `build-elf`,
  `build-obj --pic` over every example) are byte-identical to v0.23.2-dev; only
  packaging changed.

## v0.23.2-dev — wider integer types: i64 / u8 / u16 / u32 (P3.2)

- **The type system becomes one lattice.** The scattered `isPtr`/`isBool`/
  `paramPtr`/`paramBool` booleans are replaced by a single
  `enum class Ty { I32, U32, U8, U16, I64, Ptr, Bool }`, with one set of width
  helpers — `tySize`, `tyIs64`, `tyIsUnsigned`, `tyShift`, `tyName` — driving
  every load, store, arithmetic and comparison choice. `i32` is the default
  branch, so every pre-existing construct takes exactly the path it always did.
- **`i64`.** Literals up to `0x7fffffffffffffff` are accepted; values live in
  64-bit registers and 8-byte slots; `+ - * /`, unary minus, negative literals
  and all six comparisons work on the full width. Calls use the 8-byte AAPCS64
  ABI — `x0`–`x7` for the first eight integer arguments, `x0` for the result,
  and 8-byte stack slots from the ninth argument on — so a single `i64` is
  call-compatible with a C `long` on LP64 Android.
- **`u8` / `u16` / `u32`.** Zero-extending `LDRB`/`STRB` and `LDRH`/`STRH`
  accesses, unsigned condition codes `LO`/`LS`/`HI`/`HS` for the ordered
  comparisons, `UDIV` for division, and wrapping arithmetic at the type width.
  Wide and narrow globals and array element types are supported.
- **Rejections.** Narrowing (`i32` into `u8`, `i64` into `i32`) and sign
  mismatch are errors in initializers, assignments, arguments and returns. A
  literal must fit its target: `let g: u8 = 250;` is accepted and
  `let g: u8 = 300;` is rejected; a literal above the `i64` range is a parse
  error.
- **Latent wide-literal bug fixed.** `emitMov64` built its `MOVK` instructions
  from the base `0xf2a00000`, which already has `hw = 01` (`lsl #16`) baked in,
  so every chunk landed one shift too far — the `>>32` chunk was emitted as
  `lsl #48`, corrupting every i64 constant wider than 32 bits. The base is now
  `0xf2800000` (`hw = 00`) with the shift taken from the chunk index. GAS
  confirms the four forms (`lsl #0/#16/#32/#48` = `0xf2800020`/`0xf2a00020`/
  `0xf2c00020`/`0xf2e00020`), and the disassembly of `1000000000000`
  (`0xE8D4A51000`) now shows `movk #0xd4a5, lsl #16` + `movk #0xe8, lsl #32`.
- **Latent halfword-scaling bug fixed.** `LDRH`/`STRH` encode their unsigned
  immediate in units of the 2-byte access size, unlike the unscaled byte forms
  (GAS: `ldrh w16, [x16, #8]` assembles to `imm12 = 4`). The u16 load/store
  helpers passed a raw byte offset, so `slot*8` became an access at `slot*16` —
  a u16 local was written into its neighbour's slot. Both the slot-grid and the
  arbitrary-offset helpers now encode `bytes/2` (`slot*4`, `off/2`).
- **Byte-level regression caught and fixed.** The rewritten function prologue
  loaded every incoming stack argument (the 9th and later) with a hardcoded
  64-bit `LDR X16`, where the previous release chose the width from the
  parameter type (`LDR W16` for i32). Four examples changed bytes. The load now
  goes through `loadIncomingArgT`, which dispatches on the declared type.
  Verified by an A/B build of all 82 `examples/*.ast` with the v0.23.1-dev
  compiler and the v0.23.2-dev compiler: **82/82 byte-identical** after the fix.
- **Coverage.** `tests/wide-int-checks.sh` (8 steps): strict build → source
  guards (the type lattice, the width helpers, the `MOVK` base, the narrow
  encodings, `loadIncomingArgT` and the four halfword/burst scalings) → a
  rejection matrix → qemu self-checks for i64 arithmetic and comparisons, the
  i64 ABI (3-arg return, i64/u32 returns, 9-arg stack ABI), narrow unsigned
  wrap/`UDIV`/unsigned compare, wide and narrow globals and array elements →
  and a regression step for the halfword scaling and the u16 stack argument.
  The two new runtime cases were verified to *fail* when the bugs are
  reintroduced, so they are not vacuous.
- **Zero regression.** All 82 `examples/*.ast` compile byte-identically under
  `build-so` versus v0.23.1-dev (A/B, 82/82); full `tests/smoke.sh` passes.
- **Follow-up (not this release):** the unsigned narrow types are language-level
  only — they are not yet exposed as distinct C signatures across the ABI. A
  cast expression still does not exist, so there is no way to ask for a
  truncation.

## v0.23.1-dev — `--bounds-check`: run-time array bounds checking (P1.5)

- **The other half of array safety.** Constant out-of-bounds indexes have been
  a compile-time error since v0.22.48-dev; an index whose value is only known
  at run time still read or wrote neighbouring memory in silence. `--bounds-check`
  lowers every dynamic subscript into a fixed-size array — global `i32[N]` and
  local `i32[N]`, the read path `buf[i]` and the write path `buf[i] = v` alike —
  into one unsigned comparison plus a trap:

  ```asm
  cmp  w_index, #LENGTH   ; SUBS WZR, w_index, #LENGTH — flags only
  b.lo skip               ; unsigned < LENGTH: in range
  brk  #1                 ; out of range: SIGTRAP
  skip:
  ```

  One comparison covers both halves of the range: a negative index is a huge
  unsigned value, so `b.lo` fails for `index < 0` and `index >= LENGTH` alike.
  `BRK #1` arrives as `SIGTRAP` (exit status 133 under qemu), turning silent
  corruption into a loud, deterministic failure.
- **Opt-in, and provably so.** Off by default, so a default build emits no test
  at all: all 82 `examples/*.ast` still compile byte-identically versus
  v0.23.0-dev. Constant subscripts are skipped (the compile-time fold already
  proved them in range), so a program that only indexes with literals is
  byte-identical even with the flag on — `tests/runtime-bounds-checks.sh` [5/8]
  asserts both halves. `p[i]` on a pointer, and any array whose length is
  unknown here, has nothing to compare against and is left alone. Lengths above
  4095 exceed the 12-bit compare immediate and are a compile error with the flag
  on.
- **Flag routing.** Accepted by `build-elf`, `build-pie`, `build-obj`,
  `build-so` and `build-apk` (applied to the library compiled from `.ast`
  input). The `link-*` commands consume objects rather than sources and reject
  it with the usage banner, so the flag can never appear to work while doing
  nothing — `tests/runtime-bounds-checks.sh` [3/8].
- **Latent encoding bug fixed on the way.** `CMP Wn, #0` was encoded as `SUBS`
  with `Rd = 0` instead of `Rd = WZR` (31), i.e. `subs w0, Wn, #0`: it wrote the
  subtraction result into `w0`, a live expression temporary, instead of
  discarding it and keeping only the flags. Disassembly confirmed the fix
  (`cmp w1, #0x0`); the 82/82 byte-identical result shows the clobbered value
  was always immediately recomputed in the only shape that used it, so no
  existing artifact changes. `tests/bool-checks.sh` [2/8] now guards the
  encoding, and `cmpImm` (the 12-bit form) is the helper `--bounds-check` uses.
- **Coverage.** `tests/runtime-bounds-checks.sh` (8 steps): strict build →
  source guards (flag, `Codegen` plumbing, `emitBoundsCheck`, `BRK` encoding,
  the branch-over-the-trap condition, the constant-index skip) → flag routing →
  compile-time rejection still wins with the flag on → structural trap counts
  (0 by default, exactly 4 in the fixture) → qemu self-checks that in-range
  dynamic indexes behave identically (including index 0 and `LENGTH - 1`) → qemu
  proofs that `index == LENGTH`, a negative index, an out-of-range local write
  and an out-of-range global write all raise `SIGTRAP` while the default build
  does not → a bit-level check of `BRK #1` (`0xd4200020`), `B.LO +2`
  (`0x54000043`) and the length compares. Registered in `tests/smoke.sh`:
  **126 PASS / 0 FAIL / 3 SKIP** default host, **171 PASS / 0 FAIL / 0 SKIP**
  cross.
- **Zero regression.** All 82 `examples/*.ast` compile byte-identically under
  `build-so` (and the 40 `build-elf` ones too) versus v0.23.0-dev; all 82 also
  build with the flag on. APK rebuilt as versionCode 9 / versionName 1.0.53,
  validated `ALL CHECKS PASSED`, embedded `libnativeapp.so` sha256 unchanged at
  `75e0525b…`.
- **Follow-up (not this release):** the trap has no diagnostic of its own — a
  device run reports only the signal, not the array name or index. Emitting a
  message (or a distinct `BRK` immediate per site) is the next step; so is
  extending the check to pointer subscripts once arrays carry a runtime extent.

## v0.23.0-dev — standalone semantic pass (P3.1)

- **Type checking is now its own pass.** `struct Sema` walks every emitted
  function over the AST — with its own scope stack and a three-type lattice
  (`Ty::I32`, `Ty::Ptr`, `Ty::Bool`) — *before* `Codegen` is constructed. It
  owns the type rules: declarations and assignments, indexing (including the
  constant-folded bounds check), dereference read/write, call arity and argument
  types, return types (including the dangling-local-array rejection), and the
  `if`/`while`/`for` condition rule. A local `i32[N]` binds as a `Ptr` carrying
  its length, which is exactly how codegen treats the array's base slot, so the
  name-still-decays-to-its-frame-address rule is modelled rather than duplicated.
  A non-`extern` function name in a value position is its address (`Ptr`).
- **Codegen keeps its checks as a defensive backstop.** Nothing was removed from
  `Codegen`: a rule can now be applied twice, but it cannot be silently dropped.
  That is what makes the 82/82 byte-identical result meaningful — the pass
  proves the two agree on every example, and the suite fails if either side
  changes its mind.
- **One definition of the shared questions.** `constantInteger` and
  `isComparison` moved out of `Codegen` to file scope, so the semantic pass and
  codegen cannot disagree about whether an index folds to a constant or whether
  an expression is a comparison. `tests/sema-checks.sh` [2/6] asserts each
  helper exists exactly once.
- **Intended behavior change: diagnostic ordering.** A type error is now
  reported *instead of* a later codegen diagnostic. Concretely, a program with
  both a fall-through path and a type error used to report
  `may fall through without returning i32` (codegen checks that first); it now
  reports the type error. With no type error present, the fall-through
  diagnostic still fires. This is the observable proof that checking no longer
  happens during emission, and `tests/sema-checks.sh` [3/6] locks both halves
  down. Every pre-existing rejection message is unchanged — verified by the
  full suite going green without a single test edit.
- **Coverage.** `tests/sema-checks.sh` (6 steps): strict build → source guards
  (pass exists, wired ahead of both `Codegen` call sites including the synthetic
  run-time initializer, shared helpers defined once) → the ordering proof → a
  14-case type-rule matrix (ptr/i32/bool slots, deref, `ptr + ptr`, `i32 - ptr`,
  pointer comparison, argument count, undeclared call, scalar indexing,
  returning a local array, whole-array assignment, constant out-of-bounds) →
  two qemu self-checks that the pass's scope model matches codegen's (for-header
  scope, block shadowing of a global, ptr/array parameters) and that a program
  touching every type still compiles. Registered in `tests/smoke.sh`:
  **118 PASS / 0 FAIL / 3 SKIP** default host, **163 PASS / 0 FAIL / 0 SKIP**
  cross.
- **Zero regression.** All 82 `examples/*.ast` compile byte-identically under
  `build-so` (and the 40 `build-elf` ones too) versus v0.22.51-dev; the APK's
  embedded `libnativeapp.so` keeps sha256 `75e0525b…`. APK rebuilt as
  versionCode 8 / versionName 1.0.52 and validated `ALL CHECKS PASSED`.
- **Follow-up (not this release):** the pass infers types on the fly rather than
  producing a typed IR, so both passes still re-derive what they need. A
  `TypedExpr` carrying its type is the next step.

## v0.22.51-dev — `bool` type, `&&` / `||` / `!`, and `else if` chains (P1.3, P1.4)

- **`bool` is a first-class type.** Comparisons (`== != < <= > >=`) are no longer
  restricted to `if`/`while`/`for` conditions: they are ordinary bool
  expressions that can be stored in `let b: bool = …`, passed to a `bool`
  parameter, returned from a `fn … -> bool`, and held in a `let g: bool = 0;`
  global. A bool occupies a 4-byte slot and is materialized as 0/1 with
  `CSET Wd, <cond>` (`CSINC` with `Rn = Rm = WZR`:
  `0x1a9f07e0 | (cond^1) << 12 | Rd`). `bool` is deliberately **not**
  interchangeable with `i32` — an `i32` is never coerced to a truth value, the
  only integer literals assignable to a bool are `0` and `1`, and a bool global's
  initializer must be one of them.
- **Logical operators.** `&&` and `||` **short-circuit** (the right operand is
  evaluated only when the left does not already decide the result) and `!`
  negates. Precedence is `||` < `&&` < comparisons, so `a < b && b < c` groups
  as written; chained comparisons (`a < b < c`) remain a compile error — use
  `&&`. The `&&` shape preloads `MOV Wt, #0` (and `||` `#1`), evaluates the left
  operand, then `CMP #0` + a conditional branch that skips the right operand.
- **Conditions generalize.** `if`/`while`/`for` now accept a comparison *or* any
  bool expression. A comparison keeps the original `CMP` + conditional-branch
  shape (`emitCondition`'s fast path), so every pre-existing example still
  compiles byte-identically; anything else is materialized and tested with
  `CMP #0`. A non-bool condition is
  `condition must be a comparison or a bool expression` (this message replaces
  the for-specific `for condition must be a comparison`, which is why
  `tests/for-loop-checks.sh` was updated).
- **`else if` chains (P1.4).** After the mandatory `else`, an `if` is parsed as
  another if statement nested in the else branch, so `else if (c) { }` is sugar
  for `else { if (c) { } }` and produces the same code — chains nest to any
  depth and `if` still requires a final `else`.
- **Diagnostics.** Rejected with actionable messages: bool initialized/assigned
  from an `i32`, an `i32` initialized from a bool, an `i32` used as a condition,
  a bool returned from an `i32` function (and vice versa), an `i32` passed to a
  bool parameter, a non-`0`/`1` bool global initializer, `&&`/`||`/`!` on
  non-bool operands, and `fn main() -> bool`.
- **Bug found and fixed while unifying the condition path.** Routing `for`
  through the new `emitCondition` exposed a double inversion: the loop-exit
  branch was patched with `cond^1` while `emitCondition` already returns the
  inverted condition, so a `for` exited on the first test (`1+2+3+4+5` came out
  as `0`). `for` now patches with the same `cond` as `while`; caught by
  `tests/for-loop-checks.sh` [4/7], which fails with exit 241 (`-15`).
- **Coverage.** `tests/bool-checks.sh` (8 steps: strict build, source guard,
  a 13-case rejection matrix, qemu self-checks for `&&`, `!`/`||` with an
  observable short-circuit, bool returns/parameters/globals, and a four-arm
  `else if` chain, plus a bit-level assertion that `CSET` keeps `Rn = Rm = WZR`
  — the encoding trap that first produced `csinc w1, w24, wzr`). Registered in
  `tests/smoke.sh`: **113 PASS / 0 FAIL / 3 SKIP** on the default host and
  **158 PASS / 0 FAIL / 0 SKIP** with `HOSTCC=aarch64-linux-gnu-gcc`.
- **Zero regression.** All 82 `examples/*.ast` compile byte-identically under
  `build-so` (and the 40 that build as `build-elf` too) versus v0.22.50-dev; the
  APK's embedded `libnativeapp.so` keeps sha256 `75e0525b…`. APK rebuilt as
  versionCode 7 / versionName 1.0.51 and validated `ALL CHECKS PASSED`.
- **Known limitation.** There are no `true`/`false` keywords yet; bool literals
  are `0` and `1`.

## v0.22.50-dev — `for` loops, governance docs, and a local-array frame-base fix

- **`for` loops.** `for (init; cond; step) { body }` compiles to the observable
  semantics of `{ init; while (cond) { body; step; } }`: the initializer runs
  once inside the loop's own scope (a `let` there is invisible after the loop),
  the step sits between the body and the back-edge, and `continue` branches to
  the step — so a `continue` in a `for` still makes progress, while a `continue`
  in a `while` re-checks the condition, exactly as before. The initializer
  accepts a `let`, an assignment (`i = 0;`, `buf[i] = 0;`, `*p = 0;`), or
  nothing; the step accepts an assignment or nothing. The condition must be a
  comparison (`for condition must be a comparison`) and may not be empty.
  Header misuse is a parse error (`expected '(' after for`, `expected 'let',
  assignment, or ';' in for initializer`, `for step cannot declare a variable`,
  `expected ';' after for condition`, `expected ')' after for step`).
  Implementation: `Stmt::For` keeps the three clauses as statements; codegen
  places the step after the body, and `break`/`continue` sites — previously
  patched immediately — are now collected per loop and patched after the body
  (while → condition, for → step). The while code shape is unchanged.
- **Local-array frame-base fix (inherited from v0.22.49-dev).** The frame base
  was materialized with `ADD Xd, SP, #slot*8` encoded as `0x91400000|...`,
  whose `sh` bit (bit 22) scales the imm12 field by 4096 — so the base was
  `SP + (slot*8 << 12)`. Every v0.22.49-dev test declared its array first
  (slot 0, offset 0), which masked the bug; an array behind any other local
  wrote far outside the 8 KiB frame (SIGSEGV under qemu). All three base
  materializations (name decay, index read, index write) now use the `sh=0`
  encoding `0x91000000|...`, each site carries a comment naming the trap, and
  `tests/local-array-checks.sh` [2/8] asserts exactly three `sh=0` encodings
  while [8/8] runs an array deliberately placed behind a scalar local.
- **Governance docs (P0.3).** `ROADMAP.md` (planned versions, backlog,
  deliberate non-goals), `ARCHITECTURE.md` (pipeline, frame/register
  conventions, image formats, signing, test architecture — all describing the
  code as it is), and `FEATURE_STATUS.md` (per-feature status, introducing
  version, and covering suite) land in the repository root, indexed from
  `README.md`. They supersede the parallel branch's v0.23.4-perspective
  governance files, which never acknowledged the APK pipeline.
- **Zero codegen change for existing programs.** The 82 `examples/*.ast` all
  produce byte-identical `build-so` output under v0.22.49-dev and v0.22.50-dev
  (the fixed encoding path is only reachable through the new local-array
  layouts, which no shipped example uses). `tests/smoke.sh`: 106 PASS, 0 FAIL,
  3 SKIP on the default host (99 at v0.22.49-dev + 6 new `for-loop` + 1 new
  local-array regression); 151 PASS, 0 FAIL, 0 SKIP with
  `HOSTCC=aarch64-linux-gnu-gcc` (144 at v0.22.49-dev + the same 7).
- **Refreshed diagnostics.** `break`/`continue` outside a loop now report
  `break used outside a loop` / `continue used outside a loop` (they were
  while-specific), and the statement dispatcher lists `for`.

## v0.22.49-dev — local arrays and pointer indexing (P1 language capabilities)

- **Local arrays.** `let buf: i32[LENGTH] = <const>;` is now legal inside a
  function (1..1000 elements). The array lives in the function's existing
  8 KiB frame: elements are packed 4 bytes apart inside the 8-byte-per-slot
  local grid (`ceil(LENGTH / 2)` slots, element `i` at byte offset
  `slot*8 + i*4`), initialized by one constant store per element at the
  declaration point (a `let` inside a `while` body re-initializes each pass).
  Both index paths (`buf[i]`, `buf[i] = v`) materialize the frame base with
  `ADD X16, SP, #slot*8` and then reuse the existing scaled-index instruction
  (`ADD X16, X16, W(index), UXTW #2`) — the same encoding global arrays use.
- **Pointer indexing.** `p[i]` and `p[i] = v` on any ptr-typed local or
  parameter compile to `*(p + i*4)`: the base is loaded from its frame slot
  (`LDR X16, [SP, #slot*8]`) *after* the index is evaluated, then the same
  `UXTW #2` scaling applies. A ptr subscript, or storing a ptr through `p[i]`,
  is still rejected (`pointer indexing is not supported yet` /
  `pointer indexing/assignment is not supported yet`).
- **Safety rails on the new surface.** Constant subscripts on local arrays go
  through the v0.22.48-dev constant-folding bounds check, with shadowing
  resolved through the scope stack (a local `let g: i32[2]` makes `g[5]` an
  error even when a global `i32[8]` named `g` exists). Whole-array assignment
  (`cannot assign to an array as a whole`), indexing a non-array local
  (`cannot index non-array variable`), local pointer arrays
  (`local pointer arrays are not supported`), zero/oversized lengths, and
  non-constant initializers are all compile errors. Returning a local array is
  rejected (`cannot return a local array (its frame storage does not outlive
  the call)`) because the frame dies with the function; returning an element
  stays legal.
- **Zero codegen change for existing programs.** The smoke-suite log for
  v0.22.49-dev is identical to v0.22.48-dev except for version strings, and
  `examples/native_render.ast` compiles to a byte-identical `.so`
  (sha256 `2d077896…f6d619e`, same instruction/section counts) — the feature
  only adds a previously rejected construct.
- **Refreshed a stale test.** `tests/global-deep-checks.sh` asserted a
  diagnostic (`array 'x' must be indexed`) that no longer exists in the
  compiler; the rejection it guards now surfaces as a return/assignment type
  mismatch. The assertion was updated, and the script's old "local arrays are
  rejected" negative case became a positive qemu run (the feature now exists).
  That script is standalone (not part of `smoke.sh`), which is why the stale
  assertion had survived; it now passes end-to-end again.
- **Test coverage.** New `tests/local-array-checks.sh` (strict build, source
  guards, non-constant/OOB/whole-assign/scalar-index/ptr-array/zero-length/
  shadowed-bounds/return-array rejections, plus qemu self-checks of the
  dynamic-index loop and the array-name-decays-to-frame-address rule through
  an odd-length array) and `tests/pointer-index-checks.sh` (source guards,
  rejection matrix, qemu self-checks through parameters, aliases, and a
  pointer viewing a global array), both registered in `smoke.sh`.
  `tests/e2e-c-ptr-index.sh` + `examples/c_ptr_index.ast` +
  `tests/c-ptr-index-host.c` add the C ABI round-trip: a C `int32_t[4]` handed
  to Aster `p[i]` reads/writes, verifying the 4-byte stride and that stores
  through the pointer are visible to the C caller.
- **Device verification (user-confirmed):** 1.0.48 installed on the target
  device and the full-screen gradient renders correctly — the v0.22.46-dev
  pixel byte-offset fix (`(y * stride + x) * bpp`) is now confirmed working
  on hardware, closing the stripes/black-screen investigation loop.

## v0.22.48-dev — merged safety checks from the v0.23.4-dev branch + latent APK signature bug

- **Branch comparison.** A parallel development line (`aster-v0.23.4-dev`,
  uploaded to the project drive) had forked from this line around v0.22.38-dev
  and spent v0.23.0-dev..v0.23.4-dev on a semantic-analysis pass, a canonical
  type table, and constant global-array bounds diagnostics. A systematic
  comparison (all 16 of its semantic negative cases replayed against this
  compiler) showed this line already rejected 13/16; the three real gaps were
  constant out-of-bounds indexes on fixed-size global arrays (reads and writes)
  and pointer-typed global initializers. Those three were adopted; the branch's
  type-table refactor and source modularization were not (this line's
  single-file, zero-dependency build and the full Android APK path have no
  counterpart on that branch). The branch's C-ABI return examples
  (`c_abi_i32_return`, `c_abi_ptr_return`, `c_abi_32_nested_external_call`)
  already compile unmodified on this compiler — that capability predates the
  fork.
- **Constant-folded array bounds checks (from v0.23.4-dev).** The compiler now
  records declared lengths for fixed-size global `i32` arrays (including
  `extern let` declarations) and folds constant index expressions (literals,
  unary `-`, `+`, `-`, `*`, `/` with overflow and divide-by-zero rejection)
  before code generation. `values[2 + 2]` on `i32[4]` and `values[-1] = 7` are
  now compile errors naming the array, the index, and the length; dynamic
  indexes remain runtime-unchecked. Wired into both the read (`Expr::Index`)
  and write (`Stmt::IndexAssign`) paths.
- **Pointer-typed global initializers rejected.** `let value: i32 = get_ptr();`
  silently truncated a pointer to its low 32 bits before; it is now a type
  mismatch ("expected i32, got ptr"). Also fixed the fall-through diagnostic,
  which reported "returning i32" even for `-> ptr` functions.
- **Latent APK-signing bug found and fixed.** `rsaSign()` returned the bare
  big-endian integer of the RSA signature, which strips leading zero bytes:
  for ~1/256 of all signatures the result is shorter than the modulus, and
  every PKCS#7 verifier (openssl, Android's package verifier) rejects the APK
  with "wrong signature length". Bumping the version string to 0.22.48-dev
  happened to produce exactly such a signature and `tests/apk-checks.sh`
  caught it. Signatures are now left-padded to the modulus width, and
  `tests/validate_apk.py` asserts the width structurally ([5e]: embedded
  signature length == certificate modulus size) on every run, so the class of
  bug can no longer hide behind a lucky digest. Verified by mutation: a
  hand-stripped signature is rejected by both [5e] and openssl.
- **Zero code-generation change.** The `.so` emitted for
  `examples/native_render.ast` by v0.22.48-dev is byte-identical to the
  v0.22.47-dev output (same SONAME), so the delivered render APK carries the
  same already-validated rendering code.
- **Regression coverage.** New `tests/array-bounds-checks.sh` (registered in
  `tests/smoke.sh`): strict compiler build, a source guard that fails if either
  index path loses its bounds check, out-of-bounds read/write rejections with
  exact diagnostics, the ptr-global-init rejection, and a qemu-run acceptance
  case (folded write, dynamic-index copy, read-back assert). Smoke suite on the
  default host: 89 PASS, 0 FAIL (retroactively corrected at v0.22.49-dev from a
  non-reproducible "133"; 89 = the 99 measured at v0.22.49-dev minus the 10
  `PASS` lines that release added).
- `example-nativeapp.apk` rebuilt through the one-step `.ast` path
  (versionCode 4 / versionName 1.0.48); `validate_apk.py` passes all checks
  including the new fixed-width signature assertion.
- **Device verification (user-confirmed):** 1.0.48 installed on the target
  device and the full-screen gradient renders correctly — the v0.22.46-dev
  pixel byte-offset fix (`(y * stride + x) * bpp`) is now confirmed working
  on hardware, closing the stripes/black-screen investigation loop.

## v0.22.47-dev — `build-apk` accepts `.ast` directly (one-step APK builds)

- **Question from a device user:** "why does building an APK need a `build-so`
  first — isn't that redundant?" Fair challenge. The two steps are *not*
  redundant as an architecture: `build-so` compiles/links an AArch64 ELF shared
