  object, `build-apk` packages/signs a ZIP+AXML container, and the intermediate
  `.so` is the artifact every verification step in this project actually inspects
  (`objdump`, `readelf -d`, qemu, `validate_apk.py`). But as *ergonomics* the
  manual two-command dance for the common "source → installable APK" case was
  pure friction.
- **`build-apk <input.ast>` now works.** The `build-so` pipeline was factored out
  of `main()` into `buildSoFromSource()` (byte-for-byte the same code path), and
  `build-apk` compiles `.ast` input through it before packaging. The compiled
  library gets `DT_SONAME lib<libname>.so` — matching the path it is packaged at,
  which is tidier than the old two-step default (the temp `.so`'s file name).
- **`--emit-so <path>`** keeps the intermediate `.so` (the inspection artifact);
  without it the intermediate is built in a temp file and removed, exactly like
  the existing `build-elf`/`build-pie` temp-object pattern.
- **`--soname` is now honored for `.ast` input** (previously accepted and
  ignored); a prebuilt `.so` input keeps its own `DT_SONAME`.
- A non-`.ast` input that is not an ELF is rejected with a clear diagnostic
  instead of being packaged as garbage.
- **Proof the refactor changed nothing:** `tests/apk-checks.sh` gained a 4th
  case that builds `examples/so_math.ast` one-step and asserts the packaged
  `.so` is **byte-identical** to the two-step build with the same `DT_SONAME`
  (4/4 cases, 56 OK). `tests/smoke.sh` stays 128 PASS / 0 FAIL; the delivered
  `example-nativeapp.apk` is now built through the one-step path itself
  (versionCode 3, versionName 1.0.47; `.so` code unchanged — verified by
  instruction-level diff against the v0.22.46 build).

## v0.22.46-dev — the real root cause of the striped/black render: pixel byte offsets (stride counts PIXELS)

- **The device screenshot after v0.22.45-dev still showed the artifact**: full-width vertical color bands over only the top ~25% of the screen, everything below black. Quantitative analysis of the screenshot pinned it down exactly — content ended at row ~675 of 2688 (= h/4), colors repeated every **304 px (= stride/4)**, the red channel ramped 0→62 within each 304-px period using the full **1216**-wide divisor, and the green ramp ran **4× too fast**.
- **Root cause**: `draw_frame` computed the pixel byte offset as `px = y * stride + x * 4` (and `x * 2`). `ANativeWindow_Buffer.stride` counts **pixels** per row, so the byte offset of pixel (x, y) is `(y * stride + x) * bpp`. Scaling only the x term wrote every row **1/bpp of the way down**: with stride 1216 the row-y write landed at byte `y·1216` instead of `y·4864`, so four consecutive source rows overlapped into each display row (the 304-px sawtooth — display column X shows the color of `x = X mod 304`) and only the top quarter of the buffer was ever covered (rows 672+ stayed black). No static check could see this: the v0.22.45 instruction census verified `strb` counts and the 565 encoder but never the *address arithmetic*.
- Fix in `examples/native_render.ast`: `px = (y * stride + x) * 4` and `px = (y * stride + x) * 2`, with the reasoning written into the source comments.
- **New regression coverage** (`tests/pixel-offset-checks.sh`, registered in `smoke.sh`):
  1. a source guard that rejects the unscaled form (`y * stride + x * bpp`) in `examples/native_render.ast`;
  2. `examples/pixel_offset_selfcheck.ast` — a qemu-run program that writes a tagged pattern with the correct formula into a simulated padded framebuffer (stride > width) and reads **every byte** back, for both the 4-byte and 2-byte paths, plus a **negative control** that re-runs the old formula and requires the layout check to still flag it (so the check cannot silently go blind).
- Verified on the delivered `.so`: the 4-byte path compiles to `mul(y,stride) → add x → mov #0x4 → mul` and the 565 path to `… → mov #0x2 → mul`; `strb` still 6; the three callback registrations (56/64/72) and three `bl <draw_frame>` call sites unchanged; `DT_NEEDED` stays `[libandroid.so, liblog.so]`.
- `example-nativeapp.apk` regenerated (356 instructions, versionCode 2, versionName 1.0.46); smoke **128 PASS / 0 FAIL**, apk-checks 3/3 cases (42 OK), validate_apk.py 5/5.

## v0.22.45-dev — pixel-format awareness in the render example + lock-failure check

> **Note (v0.22.46-dev):** the format-awareness work below was sound defense (a RGB_565
> window really would have smeared under a hard-coded 4-byte writer), but it was **not**
> the cause of the stripes/black the user actually saw — that was the byte-offset bug
> fixed in v0.22.46-dev. Both fixes are in the delivered example.

- **`draw_frame` no longer assumes 4 bytes per pixel.** It first calls the new extern
  `ANativeWindow_setBuffersGeometry(window, 0, 0, 1)` to *request* `WINDOW_FORMAT_RGBA_8888`,
  then reads the format the buffer actually reports (`ANativeWindow_Buffer.format@12`) and
  branches: `RGBA_8888`(1) / `RGBX_8888`(2) → 4 bytes per pixel; `RGB_565`(4) → 2 bytes per
  pixel, encoded as `r5 g6 b5` little-endian with division/multiplication standing in for
  the shifts Aster does not have (`(r/8)*2048 + (g/4)*32 + 16`, split into bytes via
  `v-(v/256)*256` and `v/256`); anything else (e.g. YV12) is skipped with a log line
  instead of writing a layout that would smear pixels.
- **`ANativeWindow_lock` is now checked.** On failure the buffer fields are not valid, so
  the function returns before touching them (previously it would have drawn into whatever
  the stale scratch area happened to contain).
- Verified by instruction census: the `.so` contains **6 `strb`** (4 for the 32-bit path,
  2 for the 565 path), the 565 encoder's `mov w1,#0x800` (2048 = r5<<11), and the new
  `bl ANativeWindow_setBuffersGeometry@plt`; `DT_NEEDED` stays `[libandroid.so, liblog.so]`.
  Disassembly also confirms the three callback registrations (56/64/72) and the three
  `bl <draw_frame>` call sites are unchanged from v0.22.44-dev.
- `example-nativeapp.apk` regenerated (356 instructions, 4 PLT imports); smoke 125 PASS /
  0 FAIL, apk-checks 3/3 PASS.

## v0.22.44-dev — redraw the frame when the window changes (resize / redraw callbacks)

- **Observed on a real device after v0.22.43-dev:** the render app launches and draws its gradient, but the picture only covers part of the screen — the rest stays black, and the drawn area shows wide horizontal stripes. Root cause: `onNativeWindowCreated` fires while the Surface is still small; the app drew **one** frame and never drew again, so when the window settled at its real (full-screen) size SurfaceFlinger simply scaled the tiny stale buffer up — smooth gradients compress into stripes, and the newly exposed area is black.
  > **Correction (v0.22.46-dev):** this root-cause analysis was **wrong**. The real cause was the pixel byte-offset bug (`y * stride + x * 4`, fixed in v0.22.46-dev): rows overlapped 4× and only the top quarter of the buffer was covered. The resize/redraw callbacks added here are still correct and useful (every notification repaints the whole buffer at the *current* geometry), but they were treating a symptom.
- `examples/native_render.ast` now factors the drawing code into `draw_frame(window)` and registers **three** window callbacks: `onNativeWindowCreated` (**offset 56**), `onNativeWindowResized` (**offset 64**), and `onNativeWindowRedrawNeeded` (**offset 72**). The callbacks receive the window pointer directly, so no global state is needed. Every resize or redraw notification repaints the whole buffer at the current geometry.
- Verified by disassembly: `ANativeActivity_onCreate` emits `ldr x0,[x0]` (callbacks@0) followed by three `mov w1,#imm / str x1,[x0]` pairs for offsets 56/64/72, each function address arriving through its own `.got` slot; all three callbacks tail-call the shared `draw_frame` (`bl 0x1000 <draw_frame>`).
- `example-nativeapp.apk` regenerated (229 instructions, 5 exported functions); `tests/smoke.sh` 125 PASS / 0 FAIL and `tests/apk-checks.sh` 3/3 cases PASS with `HOSTCC=aarch64-linux-gnu-gcc`.

## v0.22.43-dev — fix the render APK launch crash (callbacks@0, not @48) + comment syntax

- **Fixed the real root cause of "install the render APK, it crashes on launch".** `examples/native_render.ast` read the callback table pointer with `load_ptr(activity + 48)`, but in the NDK's `ANativeActivity` struct **offset 48 is `sdkVersion` (an `int32`), not a pointer** — the `callbacks` field is the *first* field, at **offset 0**. The loader therefore read a small integer (e.g. `33`) and executed `*(33+56) = on_native_window_created`, writing a function pointer to address ~89 → SIGSEGV, process dies before any frame is drawn. (`*(cb+56)` and `bits@16` were already correct.)
- **Struct layouts were pinned down from two independent sources** (the `ndk-sys` bindgen bindings on docs.rs, and the Rust `android_ffi` bindings), because a wrong offset assumption is invisible to every static check — only a device run exposes it:
  - `ANativeActivity`: `callbacks@0, vm@8, env@16, clazz@24, internalDataPath@32, externalDataPath@40, sdkVersion@48, instance@56, assetManager@64, obbPath@72`
  - `ANativeActivityCallbacks`: `onNativeWindowCreated` is the **8th** function pointer → **offset 56** (note the historical field order: `onStart, onResume, onSaveInstanceState, onPause, onStop, onDestroy, onWindowFocusChanged, onNativeWindowCreated, …` — `onSaveInstanceState` sits in 3rd place, which is what makes the offset 56 and not 48)
  - `ANativeWindow_Buffer`: `width@0, height@4, stride@8, format@12, bits@16, reserved@24`
- **New language feature: comments.** The lexer previously had no comment syntax at all (`/` was only the division token), so `.ast` sources could not carry any explanation. It now supports `//` line comments and `/* … */` block comments (unterminated block comments are an error). `examples/native_render.ast` documents both critical offsets inline.
- **Clearer diagnostics:** a non-ASCII byte in source now reports `non-ASCII character at L:C (Aster source must be ASCII)` instead of the generic `unexpected character` (a Chinese comment previously produced a confusing mid-line column).
- Bumped the compiler version string to match the history (it had stalled at `0.22.38-dev` while the history advanced to `0.22.42-dev`); the `v0.22.42-dev` entry's disassembly note ("reads `callbacks@48`") is corrected to offset 0.
- **Test-infrastructure debt paid off (the suite had never run end-to-end off-device).** `tests/smoke.sh` previously died at `global-checks.sh` with "Exec format error" (AArch64 binaries on an x86 host), which masked two further stale assertions behind it. Now:
  - `tests/run-aarch64.sh` executes an AArch64 ELF natively on ARM64 and under `qemu-aarch64` (adding the AArch64 sysroot when present) everywhere else; host binaries pass through untouched. The e2e scripts' `RUNNER` defaults to it.
  - `tests/host-toolchain-probe.sh` (sourced by every e2e suite) SKIPs with a visible reason when `$HOSTCC` cannot emit AArch64 ELF, so the suite ends green on machines without a cross toolchain (82 PASS / 2 SKIP / 0 FAIL) and fully green with `HOSTCC=aarch64-linux-gnu-gcc` (125 PASS / 0 FAIL).
  - `tests/guest-libc-alias.sh` bridges one semantic gap: auto-`DT_NEEDED` emits the Android soname `libc.so`, so on a glibc host the suites drop an AArch64 `libc.so` alias into the test directory for the guest loader.
  - Two stale `DT_NEEDED` assertions in `so-dyn-checks.sh` (`== ['libdyn.so']`, `== ['liblinked.so']`) were written before v0.22.40's auto-`DT_NEEDED` and now expect the `libc.so` fallback as well.
  - New `tests/comment-checks.sh` (3 checks) covers the comment syntax and the non-ASCII diagnostic.
- `example-nativeapp.apk` regenerated; `tests/apk-checks.sh` (3 end-to-end cases) and `tests/validate_apk.py` (5 checks, incl. the new `[3b]` `DT_NEEDED` guard) both PASS; `llvm-objdump` confirms the fixed `ANativeActivity_onCreate` is exactly `ldr x0,[x0]` (callbacks@0) + `mov w1,#0x38` + `str x1,[x0]` (slot@56), with the function address arriving through its `.got`/`R_AARCH64_RELATIVE` slot.

## v0.22.42-dev — visible rendering: function addresses, pointer stores, and a software-rendered NativeActivity

- **New language / codegen capabilities** so Aster can draw to the screen, not just log:
  - **A non-`extern` function name in a non-call position is its address (a `ptr`).** In shared-object mode the loader fills a GOT-style slot via `R_AARCH64_RELATIVE`, so a callback table can be populated at runtime.
  - **A global array name is its address (`ptr`).** Lets you hand a `&buffer` (e.g. an `ANativeWindow_Buffer` scratch area) to an `extern` call.
  - **`load_ptr(ptr) -> ptr`** intrinsic: `LDR X` — reads an 8-byte pointer out of memory (e.g. a stored function/window pointer).
  - **`*p = <ptr>`** now stores a pointer (`STR X`) instead of only an `i32`. Lets you write a function pointer into `activity->callbacks`.
- **Shared-object writer:** every function referenced by address gets its own `.got` slot + an `R_AARCH64_RELATIVE` relocation (addend = the function's link-time address), counted by `DT_RELACOUNT`. `build-so` classifies such references as `funcAddrRefs` rather than imports. Verified by disassembly: `ANativeActivity_onCreate` reads `callbacks` (struct offset 0), loads the `on_native_window_created` slot, and emits `str x1,[x0]` into the callback slot at offset 56.
- **`examples/native_render.ast`:** a `NativeActivity` that registers `onNativeWindowCreated`, then software-renders a gradient frame via `ANativeWindow_lock` / `store_u8` / `ANativeWindow_unlockAndPost` — **no EGL, no float ABI**, reusing existing `ptr` arithmetic and byte stores. The app now shows a visible picture instead of a black screen, so it can be verified by eye on a device **without `adb logcat`**.
- `example-nativeapp.apk` is regenerated from `native_render.ast`; `tests/apk-checks.sh` gains a `native_render` end-to-end case. All checks (ZIP/CRC, layout, 4-KiB alignment, `DT_NEEDED`, manifest semantics, v1 signature) PASS, and every involved instruction was confirmed against `llvm-objdump`.

## v0.22.41-dev — fix NativeActivity launch crash (real root cause): binary AXML string attribute encoding

- **Real root cause of "APK opens then immediately closes":** in the binary AXML `AndroidManifest.xml`, **string-valued attributes** (`package`, `android:name`, `android:label`, `android.app.lib_name`'s `value`, action/category names, …) were emitted with `typedValue.data` set to **0** — the real string-pool index was only written into `rawValue`. Android's manifest parser reads the string index from `typedValue.data`, so every string attribute resolved to pool index 0 (the literal `package`). That made `android:name="package"` (no such Activity class) and pointed `android.app.lib_name` at the wrong value, so `NativeActivity` could not find/load the `.so` and the process died on launch. The earlier `DT_NEEDED` fix was correct but only addressed a secondary issue.
- `attrBytes` now writes the string-pool index into `typedValue.data` for string attributes (matching the framework), keeping `rawValue` as a fallback.
- `validate_apk.py` originally read `rawValue` for string values — inconsistent with the framework's `data` read, which is exactly why this bug slipped through. It now reads `data` (like the loader) and adds a regression guard: every `TYPE_STRING` attribute's `data` must be non-zero, the manifest must contain `android.app.NativeActivity`, and `lib_name` must be non-empty.
- `tests/apk-checks.sh` gains a `native_activity` end-to-end case that builds the example, packages it, and runs the semantic `validate_apk.py` checks (with `--package`/`--libname`).
- `examples/native_activity.ast` no longer calls `ANativeActivity_finish` on create; it logs `Hello from Aster` and returns, leaving the activity alive so "it launched" is unambiguous (a black screen is expected — there is no UI toolkit).

## v0.22.40-dev — fix NativeActivity launch crash: auto DT_NEEDED for extern imports

- **Root cause of "APK opens then immediately closes":** a NativeActivity `.so` that calls extern NDK functions (`__android_log_print` from `liblog.so`, `ANativeActivity_finish` from `libandroid.so`, …) but is emitted with **no `DT_NEEDED`**. Android's loader binds PLT stubs eagerly (RTLD_NOW); `dlopen` then fails with `cannot locate symbol` and `NativeActivity` throws, killing the app on launch.
- `build-so` now **auto-emits `DT_NEEDED`** for every extern import via a built-in NDK symbol→library map (`__android_log*`→`liblog.so`, `ANativeActivity_*`/`A*`→`libandroid.so`, `dl*`→`libdl.so`, math→`libm.so`, everything else→`libc.so`). User `--needed` entries are preserved and de-duplicated, so the bare `build-so` command now yields a launchable APK without manual `--needed`.
- Fixed the map implementation: `std::string::compare(pos,count,cstr)` compares lengths first and is **not** a prefix test; switched to `rfind(prefix,0)==0` (starts-with), matching the pattern already used elsewhere in the compiler.
- `tests/validate_apk.py` gains a `[3b]` check that parses the `.so` ELF, lists `DT_NEEDED`, and fails if any **strict** NDK import (`liblog`/`libandroid`/`libdl`/`libm`) is missing its library — guarding against this regression.
- Regenerated `example-nativeapp.apk`; `tests/apk-checks.sh` and `validate_apk.py` both PASS (incl. the new `[3b]` check).

## v0.22.39-dev — native APK packager (`build-apk`)

- Adds `build-apk`: a self-contained Android APK packager with **no Java/Kotlin, NDK, aapt, d8, or Gradle**. Aster emits the native library and APK bytes directly and does not transpile to another language.
- Emits a `NativeActivity` APK: binary-XML `AndroidManifest.xml` (the `android.app.NativeActivity` launcher plus an `android.app.lib_name` meta-data), `lib/<abi>/lib<libname>.so` (4-KiB aligned via ZIP STORE), and `META-INF/{MANIFEST.MF, CERT.SF, CERT.RSA}` v1 (JAR) signature.
- Implements the full signing chain in `src/aster.cpp`: CRC-32, ZIP STORE, binary AXML (ResStringPool / ResXMLResourceMap / namespace + element/attribute chunks), SHA-256, an RSA bignum engine, RSASSA-PKCS1-v1_5, and PKCS#7 `SignedData`. Signed with an embedded RSA-2048 dev key/cert in `src/aster_apk_key.h`.
- Adds `examples/native_activity.ast` (an `ANativeActivity_onCreate` entry point using `extern` NDK calls) and `tests/run-native-activity.md` (device install/run, signature, and verification notes).
- Adds `tests/validate_apk.py`, a self-contained verifier (Python stdlib + `openssl`) that re-derives ZIP/CRC-32 integrity, APK layout, 4-KiB `.so` alignment, binary AXML fields, and the v1 signature chain (incl. `openssl smime -verify`).
- Adds `tests/apk-checks.sh`, which compiles Aster under `-Werror`, runs `build-so -> build-apk`, and validates the result. All checks PASS.

## v0.22.38-dev — 32-argument Aster-to-C PLT boundary coverage

- Adds a host callback harness exported from the executable, validating Aster caller register/stack placement against a native C callee.
- Verifies the imported symbol, PLT relocation, AArch64 ELF64 architecture, SONAME, and strict C harness compilation.
- Adds separate structural and Android/Termux runtime scripts; runtime success must be confirmed on-device.

## v0.22.34-dev — live dependency chain integrity during loader failures

- Extends `tests/so-dependency-failure-host.c` to keep a valid client/base chain loaded while both a missing indirect dependency and an unresolved symbol in another chain fail to load.
- Mutates the live chain's shared state from 3 to 4 before the failed loads and verifies the existing chain remains callable and still reports 4 afterwards.
- Retains repeated failure/`dlerror()` consumption, recovery, and final-close/reload checks (fresh state returns to 3).
- User-confirmed Android/Termux PASS: both failed loads were rejected while the valid chain remained loaded, its state stayed 4, and final-close/reload restored state 3.

# Version history

## v0.22.37-dev — 32-argument mixed AAPCS64 boundary coverage

- Adds a 32-parameter mixed `ptr`/`i32` fixture, exercising the compiler's maximum supported call arity across the eight register argument positions and 24 stack argument positions.
- Adds a reverse mixed signature with `ptr` as argument 32, so the final stack slot is validated as a 64-bit pointer rather than only as an `i32`.
- Adds strict structural validation for AArch64 ELF output, SONAME, exports, and the C harness, plus a separate Android/Termux runtime test script.
- Container structural test: PASS (strict C++17 build, shared-object emission, export checks, strict C harness compilation, ELF architecture and SONAME checks).
- Android/Termux runtime: pending; the structural test does not claim execution of AArch64 code.

- Corrects the CLI usage banner, which lagged behind the actual compiler version.
- Adds `tests/so-argument-checks.sh` covering both option spellings and ensuring rejected invocations create no output file.
- Container validation: strict C++17 build and new negative-argument tests PASS; dynamic shared-object, negative-loader-fixture, dependency-chain, C ABI structural, and C string-literal structural checks PASS. Android/Termux runtime for this new validation change is not required for the CLI-only behavior and was not claimed.
- User-confirmed Android/Termux PASS for the v0.22.35-dev baseline: repeated indirect missing-dependency and unresolved-symbol failures, per-failure live-chain state integrity (4 then 5), recovery, and final-close/reload to initial state 3.

## v0.22.29-dev — multi-level DT_NEEDED chain and shared dependency lifetime

- Added base, middle, and client shared-object fixtures to exercise a two-edge `DT_NEEDED` chain (`client → middle → base`) using Aster-generated AArch64 DSOs.
- Added a target-runtime harness that loads two distinct clients sharing the same middle/base dependency chain, verifies function and global access across both edges, closes one client while the other remains usable, and closes the explicit base handle while the remaining client still works.
- After the final client closes, reloads the chain and verifies the base global returns to its initial value.
- Container checks and Android/Termux runtime status are recorded in `VALIDATION-v0.22.29-dev.md`.


## v0.22.28-dev — DT_NEEDED dependency lifetime coverage

- Added a target-runtime test that opens a dependency explicitly, then loads an importer with `DT_NEEDED` for that dependency and verifies imported function/global access.
- Closes the explicit dependency handle while the importer remains loaded and verifies the importer can still read, mutate, and call into the dependency.
- Closes the importer’s final handle, reloads it, and checks that the dependency’s mutable global returns to its initial value.
- Container validation and Android/Termux runtime status are recorded in `VALIDATION-v0.22.28-dev.md`.


## v0.22.27-dev — dynamic-loader error recovery and close-order coverage

- Added runtime checks that `dlopen` of a deliberately nonexistent path and `dlsym` of a deliberately nonexistent symbol report errors, after which a valid DSO load and valid exported-function calls still succeed.
- Added three-handle close-order coverage: close the middle handle first, then the first handle, confirm the last handle remains usable and sees shared state, close the final handle, and confirm a fresh load resets the zero-initialized global.
- Preserved all v0.22.26-dev repeated-handle, v0.22.25-dev lifecycle, `RTLD_GLOBAL`/`RTLD_LOCAL`, same-name isolation, cross-DSO import, and negative-linker regression tests.

## v0.22.25-dev — isolate DSO lifecycle validation from global scope

- Fixed the lifecycle regression harness after Android/Termux showed the reload state remained mutated when the same process had already loaded the DSO with `RTLD_GLOBAL`.
- Split the harness into `global` and `lifecycle` modes and run them as separate processes. The lifecycle mode uses `RTLD_LOCAL` from its first load so the test measures final-reference close/reopen behavior without assuming a globally loaded object must be unloaded.
- Preserved the v0.22.24-dev global lookup checks and v0.22.23-dev same-name symbol isolation tests.
- Container validation is recorded in `VALIDATION-v0.22.25-dev.md`; Android/Termux runtime validation is pending.

## v0.22.17-dev — fix live temporary preservation across calls

- Fixed AArch64 `MOV` encoding when copying a function-call return value from `w0`/`x0` into a nonzero temporary register. The old encoding reversed source and destination, corrupting expressions that keep an earlier value live across a later call.
- The v0.22.16-dev multi-object `.rodata` test exposed the defect: `helper() + strlen("object-A")` returned an unstable garbage value while isolated calls passed. The corrected move is now `MOV Wd, W0` or `MOV Xd, X0`.
- Updated the multi-object runtime regression to identify v0.22.17-dev and changed the e2e script to preserve build artifacts on failure for easier diagnosis.
- Container validation PASS: strict compiler build, multi-object and string-literal structural suites, linker/PIE/SO/RELRO/dynamic metadata/symbol-table/GNU-hash checks; disassembly confirms `mov w1, w0` is now encoded in the correct direction. The container cannot execute AArch64 output. Android/Termux runtime validation: pending user execution.

## v0.22.16-dev — multi-object .rodata merge regression coverage

- Added two independent PIC object examples, each with its own string literals; one object calls a function defined by the other.
- Added structural and Termux end-to-end scripts for one `link-so` command that merges both ET_REL objects. Checks cover exported symbols, `libc.so` dependency, AArch64 ELF identity, and an R-only `.rodata` load segment.
- Runtime harness expects `multi_object_a=9`, `multi_object_helper=1`, and `multi_object_b=8`, exercising each object's string offset plus a cross-object `CALL26` relocation.
- Container validation PASS: strict build, new multi-object `.rodata` structural suite, existing string-literal structural suite, and linker regression suite. The new test exposed and fixed duplicate private string-symbol names across input objects by namespacing symbols per object and remapping GOT relocation references.
- Android/Termux runtime validation: FAIL on first user run (`multi_object_a=435933185`, then `-1373868031` on rerun; helper and B functions returned expected values). The failure exposed a call-result register-move encoding bug, fixed in v0.22.17-dev.


## v0.22.15-dev — string literal escape regression coverage

- Extended end-to-end string-literal coverage for embedded NUL (`\\0`) and combined escaped quote/backslash sequences. The tests confirm C `strlen` stops at the first embedded NUL and that escaped quote/backslash bytes are emitted without accidental truncation or escape reinterpretation.
- Extended the strict C ABI harness to call the new wrappers through each supported shared-object path. Existing direct `build-so`, PIC ET_REL → `link-so`, `link-obj`, and `link-pie` checks remain in the suite.
- Container validation: pending in this working revision until the structural script is run.
- Android/Termux runtime validation: pending user execution.


## v0.22.14-dev — executable output permission fix

- Fixed executable ELF outputs written by `build-elf`, `build-pie`, `link-obj`, and `link-pie` lacking execute permission. The ELF writer now closes the file and applies executable mode; failures are reported instead of silently leaving an un-runnable output.
- Extended `tests/c-string-literals-structural.sh` to assert that linked ET_EXEC and static PIE outputs are executable files.
- Container validation PASS: strict compiler build, complete string-literal structural suite including execute-bit checks, pointer and pointer-memory structural suites. The mixed ptr/i32 suite was started in a combined run that hit the command time limit; it is not claimed as passed in this run.
- Android/Termux runtime validation: pending user execution.

## v0.22.13-dev — executable linker .rodata support

- `link-obj` and `link-pie` now accept ET_REL objects containing `.rodata` instead of rejecting them.
- Final ET_EXEC and static PIE writers place `.rodata` on its own page-aligned `PT_LOAD` segment with read-only, non-executable permissions; `.data`/`.bss` remain in the writable segment.
- Symbol values and ADRP/ADD relocations now resolve string-literal symbols in `.rodata`; linker summary reports merged rodata byte count.
- Added `examples/string_literal_exec.ast`; structural tests verify ET_EXEC/ET_DYN headers and R-only load segments, while the Termux e2e test executes both outputs and checks that `load_u8` reads the initial `A` byte (exit status 74).
- Container validation PASS: strict build, string-literal structural suite, pointer and pointer-memory structural suites, mixed ptr/i32 ABI structural suite, linker equivalence suite, dynamic shared-object checks, and RELRO checks. An earlier combined run timed out; key suites were subsequently run individually and passed.
- Android/Termux runtime validation: pending user execution of the updated e2e script.


## v0.22.12-dev — relocatable read-only string literal data

- `build-obj` now emits string literal symbols and bytes in an allocated, non-writable `.rodata` section, including for PIC objects.
- `link-so` reads `.rodata` from ET_REL PIC objects and merges it into the output shared object's dedicated read-only, non-executable `PT_LOAD` segment; symbol values and GOT relocations preserve the literal address.
- Added structural coverage for `build-obj --pic` → `link-so`, verifies the linked `.rodata` section and R-only load segment, and checks that `link-obj`/`link-pie` reject unsupported `.rodata` rather than misplacing it.
- Container validation PASS: strict compiler build, direct `build-so` literal tests, ET_REL `.rodata` structure, linked SO section/segment checks, and strict C harness compile.
- Android/Termux runtime validation: USER-CONFIRMED PASS. User reported both direct `build-so` and `build-obj --pic` + `link-so` runs passing, with lengths 17/6, comparisons 0, and internal pointer-call checks passing.
- At this version, `link-obj` and `link-pie` did not yet support `.rodata`; v0.22.13-dev adds those paths.

Some additional legacy pointer/hash scripts were not completed due to execution timeout.

## v0.22.7-dev — external pointer-argument calls into Bionic

- External `extern fn` declarations may now accept `ptr` arguments. Call lowering uses 64-bit X-register argument moves/stores for pointer parameters, preserves i32 W-register handling, and places stack arguments in AAPCS64 8-byte slots.
- Added `examples/c_libc_strings.ast`, wrapping `strlen(ptr) -> i32` and `memcmp(ptr, ptr, i32) -> i32`; C supplies NUL-terminated string buffers.
- Added `tests/c-libc-strings-host.c`, `tests/c-libc-strings-structural.sh`, and `tests/e2e-c-libc-strings.sh`.
- Container PASS: strict compiler build; generated AArch64 ET_DYN; exported wrapper and imported `strlen`/`memcmp` symbol checks; `DT_NEEDED libc.so`; strict C harness compile.
- Android/Termux runtime acceptance: USER-CONFIRMED PASS (`literal_strlen=17`, `literal_compare=0`, `escaped_strlen=6`, `internal_literal_strlen=17`, `internal_literal_compare=0`; pointer structural checks passed).
- Not implemented yet: Aster string-literal syntax/static string data, `size_t`, first-class `u8`, pointer-signature Aster-to-Aster calls, allocation/lifetime checks.

This file is the canonical compact milestone ledger. Keep it synchronized whenever the compiler version, language features, or acceptance status changes. “Device PASS” means the user supplied a successful Android/Termux run log; container-only checks are listed separately and are not device acceptance.

## v0.22.6-dev — unsigned byte memory intrinsics

- Added `load_u8(ptr) -> i32` and `store_u8(ptr, i32) -> i32` compiler intrinsics.
- `load_u8` emits `LDRB W`, zero-extending byte values; `store_u8` emits `STRB W`, writing only the low 8 bits.
- Added `examples/c_ptr_u8.ast`, `tests/c-ptr-u8-host.c`, `tests/c-ptr-u8-structural.sh`, and `tests/e2e-c-ptr-u8.sh`.
- Container checks: strict compiler build, AArch64 ELF/opcode inspection, strict C harness compile.
- User-confirmed Android/Termux PASS: `load_0x80=128`, `stored_low_byte=0xff`, LDRB/STRB and export checks, strict C harness.
- Not yet implemented: string literals, first-class `u8` type, pointer-signature Aster-to-Aster calls.

## v0.22.5-dev — byte-based pointer arithmetic

- Added `ptr + i32`, `i32 + ptr`, and `ptr - i32`; offsets are signed byte offsets, not element-scaled offsets.
- Code generation uses AArch64 `ADD/SUB Xd, Xn, Wm, SXTW` extended-register operations, preserving the 64-bit address and sign-extending negative offsets.
- Added `examples/c_ptr_arithmetic.ast`, `tests/c-ptr-arithmetic-host.c`, `tests/c-ptr-arithmetic-structural.sh`, and `tests/e2e-c-ptr-arithmetic.sh`.
- Container acceptance intended: strict C++ compiler build, AArch64 ELF/opcode/export inspection, and strict C harness compilation.
- User-confirmed Android/Termux PASS: byte-based pointer addition/subtraction, signed i32 offsets, reversed addition, ELF/opcode checks, and `ptr + ptr` diagnostic.
- Not included yet: byte loads/stores, string literals, Bionic string calls, Aster-to-Aster calls to pointer-signature functions, bounds/lifetime checking.

## v0.22.4-dev — raw i32 pointer memory access

- Added `*p` i32 load and `*p = value;` i32 store using AArch64 `LDR W`/`STR W`.
- User-confirmed Android/Termux PASS: load `19088743`; store `-123456789`; C memory reflects `-123456789`; type diagnostic and ELF checks passed.

## v0.22.3-dev — initial pointer ABI slice

- Added 64-bit `ptr` function arguments, local slots, and return values; pointer echo and null-pointer round-trip.
- User-confirmed Android/Termux PASS: C -> Aster -> C pointer identity; input and output address matched; null pointer round-trip passed.

## v0.22.2 — integer C ABI stack arguments and Bionic libc

- Added and tested 1/8/12-argument integer exports and Aster-to-Aster 12-argument calls, including stack-passed arguments 9–12.
- User-confirmed Android/Termux PASS: `aster_sum12(1..12)=78`, `aster_mix12(...)=94`, `aster_call_sum12()=78`; Bionic `abs(int)` import passed.

## v0.22.1 — 8-argument integer C ABI

- User-confirmed Android/Termux PASS: `aster_sum8(1..8)=36`, `aster_mix8(80,7,6,5,4,3,2,1)=76`, and Bionic `abs(int)`.

## v0.22.0 — initial integer C ABI / Bionic smoke test

- User-confirmed Android/Termux PASS: C called Aster `aster_abs`; Aster imported Bionic `abs(int)` through `libc.so`.

## Test-status policy

- Never promote “structural check”, “strict compile”, or “ELF inspection” to runtime PASS.
- AArch64 Android shared objects require execution on an Android/AArch64 host or a suitable emulator. The development container's `Exec format error` is an environment limitation, not a target-device test result.
- After a user reports device results, update this ledger and the current release notes with the exact observed outcomes.
